# 01 – Project Status

> **This document is updated every session.** It's the only place that shows where the project really stands.
> Status: 26.10.2026 · Stage 0 (Foundation) · Next gate: **G0 Go/No-Go** · Status version: 2.8.4

---

## Summary

**The daily deletion job keeps the periods of the legal check (T-11.2, L8, L13, 26.10.2026).** `api/jobs/retention.py` runs in the dispatcher process once an hour, in its own thread next to the kitchen watchdog, first pass at the start of the process; `python -m api.jobs.retention` runs one pass by hand. Per period of docs/03 "Löschkonzept": the caller number of a call after 90 days (`calls.delete_after` is now 90 days, was 24 months); name, phone, notes and the customer link of orders and reservations 90 days after their date, the notes of the positions with them; a caller's allergy note the night after the order or reservation (05:00 local time ends the business day), recognised by "Allergi…", "Unverträglich…" or "Intoleran…"; phone and summary of a callback 90 days after it was done; an outbox payload 7 days after dispatch cut to its ids, an event not sent after 30 days cut and marked failed so no guest-less slip is ever printed, the event row after 90 days; `audit_log` after 12 months. Rows stay and lose their personal fields, required text columns become empty strings, so no migration. Every period is a setting `RETENTION_*_DAYS` (at least one day), and each pass writes one log line "Retention pass" with a count per period. The job also runs in `ENV=dev`, unlike docs/13 said before: the workbench's test calls carry real caller numbers too. Fixed in review: allergy notes also leave the outbox copies of the same orders and reservations the same night (Codex), every period is bounded at 3,660 days (a larger one overflowed `start_call`), each period runs in a savepoint of its own so one failing step no longer stops the others, and a callback never done loses phone and summary 90 days after its creation (ultrareview; option A decided by Maxi). Checked: full suite 5,608 passed plus 40 latency tests; 32 tests of the job, each period at its border, and seventeen mutations of the job (period, cutoff, field, status) each turned a test red; run as a module against a migrated database: one row per period changed, the second pass changed nothing.

**The call log keeps the hour of a call, not the minute (T-11.4, legal check L15, 25.10.2026).** `scripts/call_log.py` reads `time` as the hour (`18`, `18 Uhr`); a row that still carries a minute (`18:42`) passes, and every run cuts it to the hour in the CSV, written the safe way `--loeschen` writes (other cells and file rights unchanged). The case ID now comes from date, hour and the order of the rows in that hour. Cutting a row changes its ID, so the ID directory `.call_log_ids.json` notes the old ID under the new one (`was`) with the fingerprint of the row at review: a case reviewed under its minute ID keeps its row, gets no second draft, and a correction made before the cut is still reported; a run that stops between directory and CSV maps again without losing that fingerprint. The duplicate check no longer merges rows: without the minute a row typed twice and two calls with the same words look alike (the old Codex PR #135 cases want both kept apart), so every row counts and the report names rows with the same free text in the same hour by line number ("doppelt abgetippt? Dann eine loeschen"); bare rows are never named. docs/17, the CSV template and the paper sheet (column "Stunde") follow. Checked: 174 tests of the script green (20 more than before; the two old tests of the merge rule now test the new one), each new mapping path red under a mutation of its guard; run end to end on the template: minutes cut, mode 0600 kept, second run changes nothing; full suite 5,575 passed plus 40 latency tests.

**An allergy said with more than the parser reads is asked about, never noted in part (issues #207 and #331, PR #361, 23.10.2026).** The wish parser noted only the first allergen when more followed the word that closes the allergy phrase: "Ich bin gegen Erdnüsse allergisch und gegen Sesam", "Ich habe eine Erdnussallergie und gegen Sesam", ", außerdem gegen Sesam", "Erdnussallergie und Sesam" and "allergisch gegen Erdnüsse, aber auch gegen Sesam" each gave "Keine Erdnüsse" and closed the question; "laktoseintolerant und allergisch gegen Sesam" noted the sesame alone. Four Codex rounds on a version that read those lists showed that every extension of the reading put new words on the note ("Keine Erdnuss und Leider", a new clause, another person's allergy), so Maxi chose to ask instead of read (option C, 22.10.2026): `_left_unread` in `api/domain/menu/wishes.py` leaves the ingredient open wherever more follows than the parser reads, and the agent asks "Wogegen?"; the parser reads nothing that `main` does not read. "aber bitte keine Zwiebeln" and "aber auch keine Zwiebeln" behind an allergy are a removal now (only "aber keine" was), not when the "keine" denies an allergy word ("aber keine weiteren Allergien", which gave a stray "Keine" on `main`); a removal that waits in `wish.note` for the answer and is longer than an order line is dropped as on the kitchen note, so `draft_order` no longer refuses the order after "Keine."; and the stand-in asks again where an allergy follows a withdrawal ("Keine, aber ich bin gegen Sesam allergisch", "Keine, aber meine Frau ...") instead of closing the question. The stand-in opening of #331 was already fixed in PR #338. Checked: classify_wish compared with `main` on 17,280 generated allergy sentences: no kind changed, no ingredient holds a word `main` does not note, no allergen `main` notes is dropped (only a stray "aber" left the list where the removal behind it is now taken), 2,794 now ask "Wogegen?". Not run: the real-model evals (workbench GPU).

**The core says every booking itself, and a real model's own transfer ends the call (T-2.4, 20. to 22.10.2026).** After fifteen Codex rounds in which every word rule that judged a model's booking sentence let a false one through, Maxi approved the redesign on 21.10.2026: when `confirm` goes through, the core ends the turn with a sentence built from the confirm result ("Ihre Bestellung ist aufgegeben. Ihr Abholcode ist A1.", or that the team still confirms it; "Ihre Reservierung ist bestätigt.") and a goodbye, keeps it as `booking_say`, and refuses every booking word or code from the model except that sentence word for word. Left from the review: a cart repeated after the confirm becomes the active cart again, older than this PR (#207). **Measured on `33843ab` (22.10.2026, 00:02 to 00:37):** 24/43 and 25/43 (`main` 27/43 and 24/43), 22,028 and 22,416 tokens per case, false escalation 17.1 and 22.9 %, two missed escalations on both runs: `allergie_0002` as on `main`, and `reservierung_0035` (six guests tonight, the team calls back), new and stable: the model sent `reserved_for` without an offset, `check_slot` refused it and the model asked the guest for a time zone. Fixed on 22.10.2026: the agent path reads such a time as the restaurant's local time (`dispatch.in_local_time`); the stand-in's sentences after a confirm, which the core no longer asks for, are gone. Targets 2/27, no missed escalation. **Measured on `f0136c1` (22.10.2026, 16:01 to 16:27), the code with every fix of the review rounds:** 26/43 and 26/43 (`main` 27/43 and 24/43), one missed escalation each (`allergie_0002`, as on `main`; `reservierung_0035` passes again), false escalation 22.9 % on both, 21,022 and 21,600 tokens per case (`main` about 22,500); targets 2/27, no missed escalation; hard violations 0 everywhere. **Measured on `38e6653` (23.10.2026, 22:08), the order named by the guest's name instead of the pickup code (Maxi):** one run of the regular cases, 24/43, one missed escalation (`allergie_0002`, as on `main`), false escalation 22.9 %, 22,307 tokens per case, hard violations 0; inside the spread of one code. **Measured on the final head `802e217` (23.10.2026, 00:00 to 00:27):** 27/43 and 26/43, one missed escalation each (`allergie_0002`, as on `main`), false escalation 25.7 and 22.9 %, 21,113 and 21,905 tokens per case; targets 2/27, no missed escalation; hard violations 0. Earlier in the PR: **a real model can no longer tell a guest that something is booked before `confirm`, its own transfer ends the call, and it reads times in local time (T-2.4, 20./21.10.2026).** Three open points of the first full eval run, fixed in `agent/loop.py`, `agent/guards.py` and `agent/state.py`: a sentence with "bestätigt", "gebucht", "reserviert", "Abholcode" or "Abholnummer" is refused while no `confirm` went through in the call (rule 3; strict on purpose: also a denial, a question or a claim that names nothing is refused, and one that names an order or a table passes only once that kind is confirmed and no draft waits), and the model gets a hint; reproduced on the run of 20.10.2026 in `abholung_0078` ("Ihre Abholung ist bestätigt ... den Code 8842", nothing booked) and caught. A `transfer_to_team` the model calls itself and the team takes ends the call instead of running to the hop limit. Every point in time of a tool result goes to the model in the restaurant's zone (`closes_at` 20:00Z is 22:00). From the traces: a `confirm` without any draft gets a hint that names `draft_order` and `create_reservation`, a booking without a number gets the phone hint instead of the validation's text, and a callback without a number tells the model to ask or to hand over. **Measured** (local llama-server, `qwen3.6-35b-a3b`, invented cases, 43 regular cases): `main` 27/43 and 24/43; the first three fixes 23/43 and 20/43; the head with the refusal hints 24/43 and 21/43 (missed escalations 2 and 2, false escalation 14.3 and 20.0 %, about 23,500 tokens per case), targets 3/27 (2/27 on 14.10.). Both head runs are three cases below `main`, inside the spread of one code but on the same side twice, and one missed escalation more on every run with a fix: `eskalation_0016`, a line with nothing but noise, passed on both `main` runs and was missed on all four (a single traced `main` run missed it too). Fixed later in the PR (Codex P1): a callback with nobody's number known now goes to the team where it is reachable and otherwise counts on the ladder once the model has asked for the number; the guard was made strict over the review rounds (no reading of negations, questions or sentences, a claim must name a confirmed order or table, a pickup code must be the confirmed one). **Measured on the final heads (21.10.2026):** `84bd789` 23/43 and `0277573` 24/43, one missed escalation each (`allergie_0002`, as on `main`; `eskalation_0016` passes again), false escalation 20.0 and 14.3 %, 22,455 and 22,914 tokens per case; targets on `0277573` 1/27 (no missed escalation, false escalation 25.9 %). After four more Codex rounds: `22f46fa` 24/43 and 24/43 (one missed escalation each, `allergie_0002`; false escalation 14.3 and 17.1 %; 22,997 and 22,710 tokens per case), targets on `a1ffbbf` 1/27 (no missed escalation, false escalation 33.3 %). The last commits (`a1ffbbf` for the cases, `0403fb4`: any ending of a booking word, "noch" only as "noch ein") are covered by unit tests and the stand-in, not by a Qwen run, since the GPU window ended. Runs that were stopped because a review moved the head, and the stop at 10:25 that freed the GPU, gave no result and are not in the history. The same code differs by four to seven cases between runs although `temperature` is 0; the PR is about what a guest hears, and its merge waits for Maxi (xhigh). Four prompt lines (draft at once, allergy search with the dish only, own allergy as `note`, a weekday is the next one) gave 19/43 with 25,300 tokens per case on one run and were not taken. The most frequent failure stays: the model announces the order instead of calling `draft_order` (open points). All runs are in `docs/metrics/eval_history.csv`, the README charts are redrawn.

**A merge waits for its gate, and `/ship` drives a PR there (insights review, 21.10.2026).** The usage report of 18.09. to 20.10.2026 showed merges that skipped a step of `/done` 7b (PR #339), work that another session already covered, and real-model eval claims from single runs. The project hook `.claude/hooks/merge_gate.sh` now blocks `gh pr merge` until, on the head commit, CI passed, the branch contains `origin/main`, the status is bumped and a review is clean (a Codex state comment for that head, or a comment that opens with `Merge gate: <full head sha>`); `/ponytail-review`, the evals and the `xhigh` rule stay Claude's to check. It takes one allowed form of the command (`--merge --match-head-commit <sha>`, by number, in the folder it runs in) and blocks on a timeout too. The ruleset of `main` (24583491) now takes merge commits only and requires the branch to contain the current `main` (strict status checks), applied with Maxi's word on 21.10.2026: what the hook checks could otherwise be outrun by a merge landing in between. `/ship <PR>` runs 7b, the review rounds under the loop limits, the merge and the tidy-up. `CLAUDE.md` adds a check for parallel work before a task or fix and the rule that what Maxi wants kept lives in the repository; `docs/08_EVALS.md` §4 compares a real model only on the mean of two full runs per side. Not taken from the report: a second merge gate section (the rule and 7b exist), a headless cron for the daily review (it runs as a routine, same usage limit), an orchestrator with a task ledger, and the eval sweep on the workbench, which is a task of its own in Ubuntu. `api/tests/test_hooks_merge_gate.py`, 27 tests after four Codex rounds.

**Four more findings of the audit of 15.10.2026 closed, and the Ollama archive pinned by digest (#252, 21.10.2026).** Finding 5: outside dev and test the API no longer starts on a weak `KITCHEN_BRIDGE_TOKEN` (the proxy offers `/v1/kitchen/*` to the internet) or on one equal to `AGENT_API_TOKEN`, and the dispatcher not on an empty or weak `N8N_BASIC_AUTH_PASSWORD` while `N8N_BASIC_AUTH_USER` is set (checked at its start, the one process that gets the password); the rule is the agent token's (`api/config.py`). Finding 11: the agent site sends `nosniff` and `no-referrer`, and the operations view `default-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'`; the view's inline script and `onclick` moved to `api/gui/static/base.js`, htmx no longer inserts its indicator style, and a test fails when a production template needs inline code, inline style or another host. Loaded through the pinned Caddy in headless Chromium: no policy violation, a header button wrote with its CSRF header, an injected inline script was refused. Finding 12: a refused INVITE already writes no number since T-10.13 (the ACL refuses it before identification and logs only the sender's address); the drill "stranger" now also sends one from a caller's number and checks the output for it, 27 of 27 points; Asterisk's notices cannot be dropped instead, the dialplan's own lines are notices (docs/20 §12). Finding 10, the part without a server: backup and restore refuse a passphrase file that group or others may open, except in Git Bash on Windows, which has no such modes. The comment of 18.10.2026: `scripts/setup_local_llm.sh` fetches the release archive itself, checks it against the SHA-256 of `sha256sum.txt` of Ollama 0.35.1 (amd64 checked against the download too) and hands the installer that file instead of its unchecked download; installed for real in a throwaway Ubuntu container. Findings 7 to 9 and 13 are #359 (merged). Left in #252: the off-site copy of the backups (waits for the server order, #321), finding 4 (T-10.9) and finding 6. Assumptions: (a) since #359 Compose gives the n8n password to the dispatcher only and the user to every service, so API and `voice` start with the user alone; an empty kitchen token keeps the bridge closed; (b) restore refuses an open passphrase file too, its message says `chmod 600`; (c) the n8n and push sites keep HSTS only, their pages are third-party code not tested against the new headers; (d) Jetson and AMD machines still get further Ollama archives the installer fetches unchecked, the workbench is neither. Found by #359 and fixed here: `.env.example` shipped `GUI_BASIC_AUTH_USER=team` with an empty hash, on which Caddy 2.11.7 does not start (the whole proxy down); it now ships both empty, which closes the view (401), and a test checks the pairs. A half-filled `.env` still stops Caddy; Caddy's `{$VAR:default}` does not apply to an empty variable, so a guard for that would sit in the Compose files (open point).

**Containers get only the secrets they read and no rights they do not need (#252 findings 7, 8, 9 and 13 of the audit of 15.10.2026, 21.10.2026).** `api`, `dispatcher` and `voice` had `env_file: .env` and so held the n8n, Caddy, push and kitchen secrets; they now get the settings of `api/config.py` by name plus the secrets each one reads, and a test fails on a setting no service gets or a secret in the wrong one. Every service runs with `no-new-privileges`; in production every capability is dropped (kept: five for the entrypoints of Postgres and Asterisk, `NET_BIND_SERVICE` for Caddy) and every root file system but Asterisk's is read-only. n8n sends no usage data and takes an optional `N8N_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, which must be the key already in its volume (docs/13 §3 has the copy step). The speech spike image installs by hash like `voice`. Checked on throwaway stacks, production (the "New server" steps of docs/13 §3, the real ear and mouth on the processor) and development, and with the outage drills against the hardened Asterisk. Left: `USER` in `api/Dockerfile` (the dev container writes into the mounted checkout), the agent token in dispatcher and `voice` (the start check of `api/config.py` asks every process for it), and Caddy, which does not start with an empty `GUI_BASIC_AUTH_HASH` or `N8N_EDITOR_AUTH_*` although docs/13 says empty closes the access (seen on 2.11.7, belongs to the Caddyfile).

**A team that does not pick up becomes a callback card, and the caller hears so (T-10.8, 20.10.2026).** Before, the dialplan rang `TEAM_PHONE` for 30 s and hung up without a word, and nothing told the team of the call. Maxi decided how the opening hours and the dial result share the work (D25): outside the opening hours nobody is rung and the agent writes the card at once, as before; inside them the call goes to the team, and the dial result decides. When nobody answers at `TEAM_PHONE`, the dialplan reports the call to `voice` (`POST /calls/{id}/missed`, media token), which writes the card through the new `callbacks.team_missed`: the call found by its line id after its log is closed, the number the guest named in the call or the caller id, the reason of the transfer, outcome `callback`. A caller the AI answered then hears from a file `voice` renders at its start that the restaurant calls back, or to call again when no card could be written, also with `voice` or the database down; a caller the AI never answered (paused, shadow, `voice` not reached) hears nothing. Whoever the AI answered heard it say who it is first (L1 of the legal check): the outage sentence, when it is the first of a call (the database down at the start), now begins with the AI notice (`SAY_OUTAGE_FIRST`; before, it went without one). Chosen by a design panel of three designs and three judges (ultracode): sentences from files over a second media leg into `voice`, because only files hold with `voice` down. No migration. Drill 26 of 26, six points new (docs/20 §5, §11); the core and the evals are unchanged. Open around it, Maxi's: a caller who hangs up while the team rings gets no card (docs/20 §12), and the open point of 12.10.2026 on a transfer the model asks for outside the opening hours.

**A thread's Route line is checked again in the desktop app (20.10.2026).** Asked whether simpler work goes to a cheaper model: a session cannot switch its own model, `route_advice.sh` only advises, and the advice is silent on a session's first prompt because the app's `SessionStart` carries no model and the transcript has none yet. Handing a piece to a thread works: a thread started from a plain local session with `claude-sonnet-5-5` and `medium` ran on exactly that. Its brief arrived behind the app's own lines (a `<system-reminder>`, then "Background ... Task:"), so the hook never saw the Route line; it now reads the first line after `Task:`, and with the model unknown it is silent when the routed effort took and reports otherwise, never a stop. The Projects test in docs/19 §6 has its first row; steps 1, 3, 4 and 5 (a real project chat, fork, cloud, Ubuntu) are still open.

**The dialplan rings only numbers from the environment, and Asterisk takes SIP only from the access (T-10.13, 19.10.2026, #252 findings 1 to 3 of the audit of 15.10.2026).** Before, the dialplan dialled any number `voice` named, so a changed `service_config.team_phone` or a `voice` that is no longer ours could send every handover to a number of anybody's choice; a team leg nobody answered ended the call although `TEAM_PHONE` might have worked (P1, rule 5); and Asterisk took an INVITE from any address on the network whose From user was the endpoint's name, `access`, without a password. Now a transfer goes only to `TEAM_PHONE` or a number on the new optional `TRANSFER_TARGETS`, compared exactly as written; any other number rings `TEAM_PHONE` instead, and a listed number nobody answers is followed by `TEAM_PHONE` once. Calls are identified by address alone, and an ACL refuses SIP from every address but those of `ACCESS_HOST`, which `render.sh` looks up once at the start (the ACL takes no names). `render.sh` refuses a target that is no number or our own, and an `ACCESS_HOST` without an IPv4 address. Three new drills with five points, red on the configuration of `main` (the stranger's INVITE taken with `100` and `200`; a number off the list dialled while `TEAM_PHONE` never rang), and 20 of 20 on the branch (docs/20 §5, §11). **The ultrareview of PR #340 (20.10.2026)** found that the ACL took only the A record of `ACCESS_HOST`, so a trunk's calls from the servers behind its SRV records would have been refused while the registration stayed green (a SIP trunk provider's registrar name may have no A record at all): `render.sh` now takes the servers of the `_sip._udp` SRV records, and the name's own A record only when it has none; a team leg goes by those records when they exist, and our own number is recognised in its bare international form too (Codex on PR #340; the SRV path is not run end to end, docs/20 §5). A failed SRV lookup (timeout, SERVFAIL) now stops the start instead of passing for "no records", which would have left the ACL with the A record alone once DNS came back, and so does an SRV server without an IPv4 address, which the ACL would otherwise leave out (Codex on PR #340, checked in the image; a trunk with an IPv6-only server would never start, T-10.12). Also fixed from it: IPv6 refused by the ACL as well; `TRANSFER_TARGETS` cleaned of blanks, empty fields and `TEAM_PHONE` in another form, which would have been rung twice; the team legs ring with `r`, so a network's announcement that a number cannot be reached no longer makes the caller hang up before `TEAM_PHONE`; the drill counts only the ACL's `403` as refused (a `401` means the ACL is gone) and checks the dialplan's own refusal; comments that credited `endpoint_identifier_order` with what `identify_by=ip` does. Drill 20 of 20 again. **Open, Maxi's call:** a listed mobile whose mailbox answers keeps the call, and `TEAM_PHONE` never rings (docs/20 §12). Unanswered at `TEAM_PHONE` the call still ends: T-10.8. Next on the phone path: T-10.1 step 2 at the pilot's router, after its security audit (docs/09 §5).

**Three ways to lose an allergy are closed (review of the merged PR #330, 19.10.2026).** PR #330 was merged without Maxi's look at the diff although allergens are an xhigh trigger; a separate review found two regressions and one older gap. (1) A denial joined by a bare "und" to an allergy adjective with an ingredient ("ich habe keine Allergie und allergisch auf Erdnüsse", "ich bin nicht allergisch und intolerant gegen Laktose") was read as one denial and dropped without a question; the negation no longer reaches such a word, so the agent asks "Wogegen?". A second noun stays denied ("keine Allergien und Unverträglichkeiten gegen Laktose", Codex PR #338). (2) The stand-in judged an opening denied when every part with "allergi" denied, so a "Laktoseintoleranz" or "vertrage keine" next to it was not looked at, and the order was confirmed without it; it now uses the domain's allergy words (`mentions_allergy`) and the guard sends the call to the team as before #330, also for "Ich vertrage keine Erdnüsse und möchte Pho Bo zum Abholen", which it lost before #330 too. (3) Older than #330: the ingredient behind a second "gegen" or "auf" ("allergisch gegen Erdnüsse und gegen Sesam", "gegen Erdnüsse gegen Sesam allergisch") never reached the kitchen; it is read with the list now, only for the same preposition again and a one-word item, never a time ("und gegen 18 Uhr"), "auf jeden Fall" or a clause like "und auf Diät", and an order verb after the list ends it ("und möchte bestellen"; not "und möchte keine Zwiebeln", which the kitchen still reads). The stand-in judges neighbouring allergy parts of the opening as one clause, so "keine Allergien und Unverträglichkeiten gegen Laktose und möchte Pho Bo" is one denial (Codex PR #338). Grammar fuzz of `classify_wish` (112,320 sentences as written, 139,968 lowercase without punctuation, 36,960 second-"gegen" forms): no affirmed allergen lost, no pure denial turned into an allergy with an ingredient. Maxi's direction for allergy handling is recorded as D24; the other review findings are open points.

**The README images and the README's generated parts move once a week, Sunday 22:00 UTC (23:00 Berlin in winter, Monday 00:00 in summer; Maxi, 19.10.2026).** A Claude Code cloud routine, "Weekly README update" (Sonnet 5.5), redraws them from `main`, opens one PR and merges it after green CI and a clean Codex review, so the README is current on Monday morning. A task PR no longer has to redraw: the CI image check is advisory, and the daily maintenance run reports only stale PNGs and a social preview not yet uploaded. The routine's prompt is in `docs/15_README_STRATEGY.md`, "The weekly routine".

**GitHub's default CodeQL setup stays (Maxi, 19.10.2026).** The explicit `codeql.yml` workflow from PR #333, which would also have scanned Dependabot pull requests, is removed again before it was ever switched on: a workflow file to maintain and merge commits for every Dependabot pull request outweigh a scan of Dependabot and fork pull requests before the merge; both gaps are accepted, `main` is scanned after every merge. Reasoning in docs/13 §6 "CodeQL and dependency updates".

**The operations controls require a CSRF token (PR #316, 19.10.2026).** The page and the dev-only simulator console set a `csrf_token` cookie that a script in `base.html` copies into each HTMX request; every write needs the matching `X-CSRF-Token` header, otherwise 403 before any change. The cookie is Secure outside dev/test (also behind Caddy) and on local HTTPS; a page reload keeps it, so other tabs stay usable. The Jules branch was reconciled with current order controls and proxy authentication.

**A removal said before an allergy stays, a denied allergy gives no note (#252 follow-up, 18.10.2026).** "ohne Zwiebeln, ich habe eine Erdnussallergie" gave only "WICHTIG: Keine Erdnuss. Grund: Allergie"; the kitchen note is now "WICHTIG: Keine Erdnuss. Grund: Allergie. Ohne Zwiebeln". "ich habe keine Erdnussallergie" gave that note too, and "ich habe keine Allergie" the question "Wogegen?". A phrase that only denies an allergy now gives no allergy, only the wishes around it; a negation mixed with more ("keine Allergie gegen Erdnuesse, aber gegen Sesam") gives an allergy whose ingredient the agent asks for, never one read from such words. Strict on purpose: what the denial grammar does not know keeps the allergy, so no allergy is lost. The pattern CodeQL flagged on guest text (`gegen ...` `allergisch`, 66 s at 4,000 characters of spaces) is a scanner with the same results, under a millisecond; the ingredient after "allergisch gegen" is read up to the end of its clause only (a chain of 76,000 characters: 10.7 s before, 0.09 s now). Evals `allergie_0006` and `allergie_0007`.

**Recording waiting voice results on a branch no longer deletes them (#207, 16.10.2026).** `python3 scripts/readme_assets.py pending --record` wrote the drill and spike rows from `evals/reports/voice_pending.csv` into `docs/metrics/voice_history.csv` and then removed the pending file in every report folder; a branch closed without a merge took the only copy with it. A row now leaves the file only once `origin/main`'s history holds it, so on `main` it shows as pending again until a merged branch has recorded it; a row that does not parse (a kit killed mid-write) is skipped with a note instead of stopping `pending`, the next appended row starts a line of its own, the pruned file is swapped in whole, and `pending` reads the history once instead of once per row. `api/tests/test_readme_assets.py` records on a branch against the main folder's pending file, prunes against a stand-in for main's history, and keeps a cut-off row.

**The operations view answers only requests that came through Caddy (T-9.7, 15.10.2026).** Since Asterisk, a service that takes outside input, shares the Docker network with the API, Caddy's Basic-Auth alone was not enough (security audit, #252 finding 6). Maxi chose the smaller of two fixes: Caddy sends a secret header, `GUI_PROXY_SECRET`, and every route of `/gui` checks it outside dev and test. A server without the secret locks the tablet out, so a deploy checks the tablet. Separate Docker networks, which would protect the database, n8n and Asterisk as well, are noted as T-9.8 for later.

**The workbench's model setup runs the Ollama installer only when its checksum matches (#252, 15.10.2026).** `scripts/setup_local_llm.sh` fetched `ollama.com/install.sh` and ran whatever came back. It now fetches `install.sh` of the Ollama 0.35.1 release on GitHub, the version the numbers in docs/18 §5 were measured with, checks it against the SHA-256 in the script head and stops with exit code 1 before running it on a mismatch; `OLLAMA_VERSION=0.35.1` makes the installer fetch the archive of the same release. Run in an Ubuntu 24.04 container with the installer run stubbed: matching checksum runs it with the version, a changed checksum stops with the message, `--dry-run` prints the check; `api/tests/test_scripts_setup_local_llm.py` holds the same three cases with a stubbed download (Codex, PR #314). **Left open:** the installer itself downloads the program archive from ollama.com without a checksum of its own (pinned by version, not by digest); closing that means installing the archive without the official installer. Workbench only, invented data only.

**The security audit runs at fixed triggers, and the first one under those rules found one P1 in the dialplan (15.10.2026).** Maxi did not want the audit to run all the time and eat the usage quota: `docs/09_OPERATIONS_LEGAL.md` §5 now names when it is due (before the first call from outside the workbench, at every gate from G1 on, before a component becomes reachable from outside, or 30 days plus changes in the exposed areas), `/start` step 5c and `/gate` step 1b recommend it, Maxi decides, and an audit log records date, commit and scope. The audit of 15.10.2026 (voice layer, deployment, token paths; findings on #252) found: a team leg that is not answered ends the call without trying `TEAM_PHONE` (P1), the SIP endpoint takes calls identified by the From user alone, and the dialplan dials any number `voice` names. All three go into T-10.13, which T-10.1 step 2 now waits for. Six more P2 and P3 points (kitchen token strength, one flat Docker network with Asterisk in it, secrets in `env_file`, container hardening, headers, backups off the host) stay in #252; the flat network needs Maxi's decision between separate networks and a secret header from Caddy. The #252 point about a lock per call no longer holds.

**The daily maintenance run reports stale README images and a changed social preview in the one decision issue (T-0.11, 15.10.2026); the self-updating README is complete.** Two PRs merged one after the other can each be green and still leave `main` stale, and GitHub has no API for the social preview: the run now checks the images on `main` first, without `PROJECT_TOKEN`, and compares the social preview with the digest recorded by `python scripts/readme_assets.py mark-uploaded`. Both land in the decision issue under "README images", keyed by asset and digest, mentioned once, closed when nothing is open; when the board step fails, the issue keeps its board sections and does not close. The bot reports and never commits (no new token, no new write right; why and the upgrade path: docs/15). The social preview is uploaded (Maxi, 15.10.2026; GitHub serves the same bytes as `.github/assets/social-preview.png`) and recorded with `mark-uploaded`.

**The README's status and its lists "What works" and "Not yet working" are written from data, and a new component on the call path cannot be forgotten (T-0.10, 15.10.2026).** `render` writes the status table (stage and next gate from this file, "As of" from the last finished task in docs/07; the version stays manual for `/gate`) and both lists from `docs/metrics/capabilities.toml`, where a capability moves to "What works" by itself once all its tasks are done; one that waits for something outside the task list (C1, a setup step) says so in `waits_for` and stays until that line goes. A test fails when a tool of `agent/dispatch.TOOLS`, a Compose service or a telephony adapter has neither a node in the call flow nor a reason in `NOT_DRAWN`. Next: T-0.11.

**Measurements record themselves (T-0.9, 15.10.2026), the first of three PRs for the self-updating README (Maxi, 14.10.2026).** `make eval` and `make eval-targets` record a full real-model run in `docs/metrics/eval_history.csv` and redraw the README images on their own, on the host after the container run, best effort and with the eval's exit code; a run with tags, the stand-in and a run in which no request reached the model are not recorded. The voice kits record their headline numbers (drill and spike points, the silent-wrong rate per judged ear, the dishes the best ear heard right). On `main` nothing is written (CLAUDE.md §8): the result waits in `evals/reports/`, `python3 scripts/readme_assets.py pending` lists it, `/start` shows it and `/done` records it on the task's branch. The metrics CSVs merge by union. Proven on the workbench on a branch and on `main`. Two runs of the same code on `qwen3.6-35b-a3b` gave 20/41 and 24/41 (the head of 14.10. had 26/41), a spread of four cases, wider than the noise of up to three measured on 13.10.; only the branch run is in the history. Next: T-0.10 (README text from data) and T-0.11 (the maintenance bot reports stale images).

**The AI telephone hears and speaks with the engines of the test phase (T-1.11 part 2, 14.10.2026).** `VOICE_EAR=whisper` and `VOICE_MOUTH=piper` load Whisper large-v3-turbo and Piper `thorsten-medium` into the service `voice`, from the image of a new target `voice` of `api/Dockerfile` (the engines' packages hashed in `api/requirements-voice.txt`, the CUDA libraries included, 4.9 GB) and the model files of the speech kit, mounted read-only. On the workbench `voice` is healthy 4 s after its start, and a whole call through `/media` on the engines as the service loads them works: Piper greets, the caller's "Ich hätte gern zweimal die Nummer 45." arrives at the core word for word, the goodbye is played and the line hung up. Three points of the reviews are closed with it. **A hung engine:** a watchdog ends the process when one engine call takes longer than 30 s, and Compose starts it again (a thread cannot be cancelled, and the model is shared by every call; Codex on PR #292). **The call log:** it is closed when the line reports the end of the leg, not when the core asks for the hangup or the transfer: after the goodbye was played, and as `error` when the line fails on the goodbye or refuses the transfer (Codex on PR #286). **A tool that waits on a lock:** the phone line's database sessions end a statement after 2 s, and the call goes to the team instead of keeping the guest waiting past the turn's deadline (Codex on PR #287). Drills on the branch: 10 of 11 and then 15 of 15; in the first run the caller's leg stayed open after the team had rung and hung up, seen once (docs/20 §12). No migration.

**The README has a visual identity and draws its numbers from dated files (14.10.2026).** Logo MÆX (the Æ as a neon tube, A in Neon Yellow, E in Electric Violet, a voice waveform as crossbar), banner, an animated call flow whose nodes read built, in progress or planned from this task list, build progress per block with the gates, the latest real-model eval and its trend, the voice layer measurements and the six hard rules, all on Midnight Purple. `scripts/readme_assets.py` writes every image into `.github/assets/` from `docs/metrics/eval_history.csv` (backfilled from the runs of 06.10. to 14.10.2026 in this file), `docs/metrics/voice_history.csv` (from `docs/20_VOICE_LAYER.md` §11), `docs/07_WORKPACKAGES.md`, this file and the README; CI fails when an image no longer matches its data, `/done` renders, `/gate` also records runs. Rules and tokens: `docs/15_README_STRATEGY.md` "Assets and design". The social preview, which the API cannot set, was uploaded by Maxi under Settings on 15.10.2026. Assumption: crashed cases and two of the hard metrics of the backfilled runs are not in the status notes, so the images show them as not recorded instead of 0.

**The AI telephone runs on the workbench, and every way it can fail ends at the team telephone (T-1.11 part 1, 14.10.2026).** Maxi let T-1.11 start without T-10.2, on the result of T-10.1 step 1. Before the adapter was written, a capture run against Asterisk 22.10.1 settled what its docs leave open, and the frames are the fixtures: a sentence is reported played by a mark of its own (`MARK_MEDIA`), and `QUEUE_DRAINED` would race with the next sentence; `FLUSH_MEDIA` drops the marks before it; **a full queue drops audio without a word and still reports the mark behind it** (25 s sent, the mark at 20.5 s), so `MediaLine` keeps at most about 6 s unplayed at Asterisk and holds the rest back, or a long read-back could count as heard while its end was never played (hard rule 3). Dialled with `n`, the first sentence answers the call, and a call the core hands on before a word is never picked up by us. `telephony/router.py` takes the media channel (`/media`) and answers the dialplan's question when our leg is gone (`/calls/{id}/ending`); both want the media token as Basic credentials. `telephony/app.py` is the service `voice`: it does not start without token, own number, engines or model, with the drill engines outside dev and test, or with the team on its own number (loop guard). The dialplan in `deploy/asterisk/conf/` ends a call only on an explicit "hangup"; a transfer dials its number, and everything else rings `TEAM_PHONE`; a call from our own number is refused. Compose services `voice` (not root) and `asterisk` behind the profile `voice`; production publishes nothing new: the media range comes with the trunk and its public media address. **Drills on the workbench, `python3 -m scripts.voice_drill`, 15 of 15 points:** the team rang after 0.4 s when `voice` was killed mid-call, 0.2 s in mode `paused` (never answered by us), 5.2 to 5.5 s with `voice` stopped and 8.3 s with the database stopped (both mostly WSL's name lookup of a stopped container); a silent caller is asked twice and let go by us; a call from our own number is refused. **Found by the drills and fixed:** Asterisk's verbose output printed the media token with every call (it runs without it, and the kit checks every container's output for token and password); a team URI without a port cost 2 to 7 s of DNS lookups before the team rang (0.1 s with it). **Found by my own review and fixed:** a frame Asterisk sends that the line does not understand was logged as a caller who hung up (now a failure of the voice layer); the loop guard compared only the E.164 form (now also 0049... and 0...); `asterisk` started with the team on its own number, which would have dropped every call (it refuses now); a ";" in the access password cut it short (escaped). **Codex on `21b2791` (P0, P1x4, P2), fixed same-day except one:** Asterisk logged the team number with every transfer (it logs the kind of ending only now, and the drill checks for the team number too); `render.sh` let the team be our own number in the `0049` form; production published the media range, which carries nothing without the public media address of the trunk (it publishes nothing beside Caddy until then); the access password and the media token reached `api` and `dispatcher` through `.env` (blanked per service, with a test); a stalled connection was logged as a caller who hung up (a failure now). Left for T-10.8, open point below: a call the team does not pick up ends without a callback. **Codex on `c889f67` (P0x2, P1x3), fixed same-day:** the caller's number travelled in the media URL, which Asterisk logs when `voice` is down (P0), and the call id travelled the same way, which Asterisk 22 can hand to another call's connection (issue #2194, P1); a call is named by variables the media channel inherits now, reported in `MEDIA_START`, and a second connection for a live call is refused. Asterisk 22.10.1 waited for ever on a `voice` that takes the connection and answers nothing (issue #1979, P1): the pin is 22.11.0, and a new drill froze `voice` and failed on 22.10.1; 22.11.0 also brings pings, which ring the team 8 to 10 s after `voice` freezes in the middle of a call (a drill of its own, red without them). `asterisk` refuses a `TEAM_PHONE` it cannot dial (P1), and a missing tenant no longer names the restaurant in the error (P0). **Codex on `661b936`, P2 only (three):** registration gave up for good after a password refused for a while (measured; it retries for ever now), and `asterisk` was healthy while unregistered (its health is the registration now); fixed. Recorded as an open point, not changed: `router.py` and `app.py` name Asterisk outside `adapters/`. **Found by the security review and fixed:** uvicorn wrote every caller's number to the output of `voice` with the media URL; since Codex's second round the URL carries nothing (below), and the drill checks for the numbers. Evals unchanged against `main` (cases 41/41, hard metrics 0; targets 1/27; numbers 211/211). **Part 2, the points T-10.5 and the reviews left for T-1.11** (open points below): the speech engines of T-10.5 in `voice` (settings and an image with their packages), a watchdog for an engine that hangs, the call log closed on the line's report, a database timeout for the phone line's session. Then the pilot's router (T-10.1 step 2, T-10.2) and real voices (T-10.7).
**Ear and mouth of the test phase run as adapters of the voice layer (T-10.5, 14.10.2026).** Maxi let T-10.5 start on the local pair of T-10.3 before that task is closed. `adapters/stt_whisper.py`: Whisper large-v3-turbo, the ear for orders by the run of 13.10.2026, behind `SegmentingEar`, which adds what the session needs and Whisper lacks: the silence detector of faster-whisper judges the caller's audio in windows of 32 ms on a thread of the ear, reports a partial while the caller speaks and ends an utterance after 704 ms of silence; speech shorter than 256 ms is no speech and is never transcribed, so a click is no yes. The detector carries its state from window to window; without it its probabilities differ from the measured run by up to 0.9. `adapters/tts_piper.py`: Piper `thorsten-medium` at the line's 16 kHz. **Key terms per turn, never the menu:** after each turn the handler hands the engine the dishes the agent just named and those of the order (`TelephonyPort.expect`, `Ear.expect`), from the searches of the call; a first version took only the dishes that became known in the turn and gave a repeated question none. **Measured on the workbench's graphics card, warm, synthetic voice through the modelled line:** a spoken order arrives as one utterance, its end found to the frame; Whisper 0.28 s median per utterance, the detector 0.08 ms per window, Piper 0.14 s to its first audio. A read-back of 12.5 s takes 0.76 s to synthesize whole and 0.17 s to its first piece, and the session plays only whole sentences (open point). Of six sentences with a phone number the core reads four as said and takes nothing wrong. The engine packages stay out of the API image; their tests run in the image of `deploy/speech/` and are skipped in CI, the segmenting runs against stand-ins there. Evals unchanged against `main` (cases 41/41, hard metrics 0; targets 1/27; numbers 211/211). **Codex on `5a92366` (P1), fixed same-day:** a frame that carries several windows stamped all of them with its arrival, so a "Ja" at its start with silence behind it, in a frame reaching past the end of a read-back, counted as said after it (hard rule 3); each window is stamped with the end of its own audio now. **Codex on `fb9e39a` (P1x2, P2x3), fixed same-day:** two clicks with a pause between counted as speech by their span and could be transcribed into a "ja" (hard rule 3); the windows of speech are counted now. Key terms that held every dish of the active menu (a menu of one dish) were the menu in the engine's prompt (hard rule 6); such terms are not handed over, by a count of the active dishes (`menu.items.active_count`). A dish the agent named without "(4 Stück)" was not seen as named; the sentence is compared with the spoken name. The command for the engine tests is runnable as written now, with a form for a machine without a graphics card. **Codex on `fc91f40` (P1, P2), fixed same-day:** a window whose audio came in two frames took the time of the later one, so a "Ja" that waited for a late frame could count as said after the read-back (hard rule 3); each window is stamped with when its audio began now, which the speech ended at or after. A closed ear worked through the audio still queued and could start transcriptions for a call that was gone; it drops it now. **Next on the phone path:** T-1.11 (media adapter, the service `voice` with the settings and the image that build these adapters), then T-10.7 with real voices.
**First full eval run on a real model, and the state a model needs to keep a call together (T-2.4 part 4, 13./14.10.2026).** Both case sets ran on the workbench's local llama-server (`qwen3.6-35b-a3b`, temperature 0, invented cases only; Ollama is not installed in the workbench's Ubuntu, so the `.env` line for `qwen3:14b` was not used). `main` gave 17/41 and 16/41 on two runs of the regular cases and 2/27 and 3/27 on the targets, with 3 missed escalations, false escalation 15 to 18 % and about 25,500 tokens per case; the stand-in gives 41/41 and 1/27. Two runs of the same code differ by up to three cases, so a change counts only beyond that. The traces showed a model that keeps nothing between turns losing the thread: it searched "Auf den Namen Mueller" on the menu, said goodbye on "Nein, das wars" with an empty order, asked `get_service_status` at the start of every turn and greeted again, and then answered from the status instead of calling `check_slot`. **Built:** the state now carries `last_say`, the one sentence that ended the last turn (at most 300 characters, never on a tool hop), and `service`, whether the restaurant was open when the status was asked in this call; the first sentence of a call counts as the greeting; the prompt asks for the status once per call before an order, not for a table, and for `search_menu` as soon as a dish or number is named; `max_results` is no longer offered (the model sent 10 and the tool refuses more than 5). **Result on the same model, on the head of the PR:** regular cases 26/41 (pickup 13/21 from 6/21, reservations 8/13, escalations 5/5; the round before measured 25/41), missed escalations 1, false escalation 9.1 %, refused attempts 9 from 16 to 18, 22,200 tokens and 306 requests (from about 377) per run; targets 2/27, false escalation 30 % from 41 to 48 %. The stand-in stays at 41/41 and 1/27, the number eval at 211/211. Found and left for a session at `xhigh`, because each touches opening hours, the confirm step or the transfer: the model reads `closes_at` in UTC and says "bis 21 Uhr" for 22:00; with nothing booked it told a guest the order was confirmed and gave a pickup code it made up; after a successful `transfer_to_team` it called the tool again until the hop limit (open points below).
**An order that waits too long for "Passt" turns red on the tablet (Maxi's decision of 13.10.2026, the open point of the order limit of #252).** Since the order limit an order can wait for the team in every mode, and an order nobody approves is cooked by nobody; its card looked the same after an hour as after a second. Now an order that needs "Passt" and has waited longer than `service_config.order_approval_overdue_minutes` (5 from migration 008, the restaurant's number and never one in the code) since the guest's yes is overdue: red edge instead of the yellow one, the text "Wartet seit 18:42 auf Freigabe", sorted below the cards whose slip did not print and above everything else. The tablet is told by its event stream, although nothing in the database changes at that moment (the list of overdue orders is part of the column's fingerprint), plays the alarm of a complaint once and scrolls to the card. The wait counts from the audit entry `confirm` writes, not from the draft; without that entry from the order's creation, so no card stays yellow for ever. Nothing else changes: the order stays confirmed and waiting, and "Passt" is still the way out. Looked at in a browser at tablet size, including a card that turns red while the view is open. **Not built:** a push message to the team's devices for a held order, which waits for the push setup on the server (the devices are decided: D14, option A).

**The call session of the own voice layer is built: what protects a booking now holds on the wire, against fakes (T-10.4, 13.10.2026).** Step 4 of the programme, real voices, waits for the phone path (Maxi, 13.10.2026), and T-10.4 is its first package; Maxi let it start on the result of T-10.1 step 1 instead of waiting for the router test. `telephony/session.py` is what a platform used to hide: one worker and one queue per call take audio, keys, what the ear heard and what the line finished playing strictly one after another, decide what is the caller's turn, and play what the core says through the mouth. Three ports in `telephony/speech.py` (`Ear`, `Mouth` and `Line`, the caller's leg) with fakes of each; the media adapter of T-1.11 implements `Line`, the engines of T-10.5 the other two. **The rules of docs/20 §4, each with its tests:** a read-back is never interrupted, and an utterance or a key is its answer only if it ends after the read-back was played to the end, also when the ear reports it late; words without speech energy are line noise and no yes; two words of the caller stop a sentence that may be interrupted, and so does a finished "Nein"; queued speech is played in order and a transfer or hangup waits for the end of the last sentence; keys are collected until `#` or a gap of 3 s; the first sentence answers the line, and a transfer before any sentence leaves the call unanswered for the team; a failure of ear or mouth (an engine error, or speech the ear makes nothing of twice in a row) is told to the core, which says the outage sentence as far as it still can and transfers. **The handler** says per sentence whether it may be talked over (a question may; the disclosure, everything while a draft waits for its yes, the confirmation, the farewell and what precedes a transfer may not), asks twice into a silent line ("Sind Sie noch da?") and then says goodbye. **Found while building, hard rule 3:** a "Ja" to "Sind Sie noch da?" would have confirmed the draft read out before it; the question now counts as the last thing the guest heard, so the core reads the draft out again and only the yes to that books (test on the handler and on a whole call). A session that breaks hangs nothing up, so the dialplan rings the team. **Codex on PR #286 (P1x2, P2x3), fixed same-day:** a line that never reports the end of playback had been treated as if the caller had heard everything after the length of the audio plus 5 s, so a read-back nobody may have heard could be answered with a yes; it is a failure of the call now and goes to the team, and a transfer that waited for the report still happens (P1). A mouth that hangs held the one worker of the call and with it the caller's hangup and the way to the team; each sentence has a deadline of 10 s now (P1). An ear without partial transcripts reports that the caller is speaking, so a long order is not interrupted by "Sind Sie noch da?" (P2). Playback begins when the audio is on the line, so what the caller said while the mouth was working is no barge-in (P2). Found in the own review of that round: after a failure the core does not answer, the session ends as failed instead of sitting on a line it no longer listens to. One P2 is not built and is an open point below: the handler closes the call log when it asks for a transfer or hangup, not when the line has done it. **Codex on `035ff7a` (P1x2, P2x3), fixed same-day, all of one root:** the order in which a session takes things. A "Ja" that was already waiting in the queue was looked at before the read-back of the same moment had been queued for the line, and the core counted that read-back as heard: booked on a yes to nothing (P1, hard rule 3). What the core asks for in a turn is now carried out the moment the turn returns, before the next queued event, so the read-back protects itself first. A mouth that failed on the farewell let the hangup behind it drop the caller; a call whose voice failed is never hung up by us now (P1). Timers ran on the clock and overtook events that had waited: two keys became two answers after a slow turn; they run on the time of the events now (P2). A second sentence that took long to synthesize was given up on before it could have played (P2), and a line that refused the flush during a failure left the core's call open (P2). **Codex on `ae49fb2` (P1), fixed same-day:** after the caller talked over a sentence, playback was dropped and no timer was left; an ear that then never delivered the finished utterance left the call waiting for ever. The ear owes the end of an utterance within 10 s of its last partial now, or it is a failure and the team; a lone `#` that stopped a sentence had the same gap. The tests check after every step that an open call waits for something with a deadline. **Codex on `8825398` (P1, P2x4):** a farewell whose end the line never reported was followed by the hangup, which tells the dialplan that all is well; the session ends as failed now and the team is rung (P1). A sentence the core counts as heard (it asks for the guest's number, or names a dish that may then enter the order) could be talked over; such a turn is played to the end (P2). No silence is counted while the ear still owes an utterance (P2), and an utterance ends after 60 s whatever the ear reports, so a stuck detector cannot keep a call open past the tenant's limit (P2). Not built, an open point below: speech is queued on the line only when a sentence is fully synthesized (P2, a matter of latency for T-10.5). **Codex on `686bd9c`, the head merged with #283 and #287 (P1, P2x2):** a mouth that returned no audio without an error had "spoken", so the line could report a read-back as played that nobody heard; no audio is a failure now, and a sentence without words is not spoken at all (P1). An entry on the keypad is handed over at 20 digits, so keys without end cannot keep a call from ever reaching a turn (P2). An event's time and its place in the queue are taken under one lock (P2). **A second pass of Codex on `686bd9c` (P1, P2x3, and one repeat):** a telephone system that stalled in a transfer held the one worker of the call (P1). The first fix, a deadline of the session on every call to the line, was taken back a round later: a command the session gave up on could still be carried out afterwards, a hangup while the team is being rung (Codex on `f654c17`, P1). The line must not block at all now, by the contract of `Line`; an adapter whose transport can stall bounds and cancels its own commands, which is a requirement on T-1.11. A mouth that failed is not asked again for the outage sentence, which cost a second deadline of silence before the team was rung (P2). Speech that began before the answer was on the line stops nothing, as for a finished utterance (P2). Not changed, by decision: a line on which nobody speaks is ended by the silence rule and not handed to the team at the tenant's call limit (P2; docs/20 §4). **Codex on `f654c17` (P1, P2x3):** next to the line deadline above, a partial counts by the time of its audio and not of its report, and a key that was pressed before the answer was on the line stops nothing (P2 each); the test for the order of the queue holds the first poster between clock and queue, so it fails for certain without the lock (P2). Whole calls run through session, handler and the real core with the scripted model: a reservation booked by the yes after the read-back, a yes spoken into it, noise after it, a silent guest, a breaking ear, a call in mode `paused`. No telephone is connected yet. **Next on the phone path:** T-10.5 (first speech adapters; the silence that ends an utterance must be at least 700 ms, and Whisper turbo is the ear for orders by the run of 13.10.2026), then T-1.11 (media adapter and the service `voice`), then T-10.7 with real voices. T-10.1 step 2 and T-10.2 at the pilot's router still need Maxi and still gate G0.

**On the phone a guest no longer waits in silence for the model beyond the restaurant's deadline (follow-up of #252, Maxi's decision of 12.10.2026, built 13.10.2026).** Until now one sentence of the guest could cost six model calls of fifteen seconds each before anything was said. Now one guest turn, all its model calls and tools together, has `service_config.turn_deadline_seconds` (10 from migration 007, the restaurant's value and never a number in the code). The phone line reads it with the routing at the start of the call and hands it to the conversation loop. Each model call is cut off after what is left of the deadline; an answer that still arrives too late is neither spoken nor carried out, and what the model heard in it (a phone number for the callback) is kept. The guest hears the outage sentence and the call goes to the team, by transfer or by a callback where nobody answers. The text phone and the evals have no deadline: nobody waits on a receiver there, and a local test model takes longer to load. With it the second half of the audit point: the call handler closes the model client when a call ends, however it ends (one client per call held connections that were never closed). **Open, with the voice layer (T-1.11):** the hold sentence "Bitte warten Sie einen Moment" while a turn runs; with it Maxi's value is 15 seconds, set in the database. **Not measured:** how often a turn of a real model reaches the deadline. A turn with several tool calls adds their answers up (a table needs the slot check and then the sentence), and the local test model answered one call in about a second when warm (docs/18 §5); with a slower model more calls would go to the team, which the call log shows as transfers after the outage sentence.

**A large order waits for the team before a slip prints, in every mode (follow-up of #252, Maxi's decision of 12.10.2026, built 13.10.2026).** Until now only the mode decided: in `primary` every order of the agent went straight to the kitchen, whatever its size. Now `confirm` reads the restaurant's limit, `service_config.order_approval_above_cents` (150 euros from migration 007, changed in the database and never in the code): an order whose total is above it is confirmed for the guest as before, with its pickup code, but no ticket goes out until somebody taps "Passt, Zettel an die Kasse" on the tablet. The card is the one a waiting order always had, with the yellow edge; the text phone says the sentence it says for an order that waits. Above the amount, not from it; a limit of 0 holds every order; the decision stays with the order, so a limit raised later prints nothing nobody approved. The audit entry says why a ticket waited (`waits_for`: `mode`, `amount` or nothing). One condition in `domain/ordering/confirm.py`, no migration. **Answered 13.10.2026:** a held order nobody approves turns red on the tablet after the restaurant's minutes (summary above); a push message for it is not built.

**Step 3, spoken orders of 2 to 12 dishes from the real menu: no ear is close, and the wrong dishes add up with the length of the order (speech kit `--orders`, 13.10.2026).** The kit now speaks whole orders by card number in one sentence ("Zweimal die 13, die 26d und einmal die S12."), the same 180 orders on every run, and judges each as one order: right with every dish and quantity, wrong when something is taken that was not said or a dish is missing without a question, asked otherwise (`evals/heard.py`; the judge cuts a sentence where the conversation cuts it, pinned by a test against `position_parts`). Building it found the two list bugs of PR #284 before any audio was heard; with them fixed all orders are right as text, so the losses below are the ear's. **One run, synthetic voices, 720 utterances per ear.** Orders right / asked / silent wrong: Whisper turbo 62.4 / 19.9 / 17.8 %, Parakeet v3 47.9 / 34.0 / 18.1 %, Canary v2 47.9 / 36.2 / 15.8 %, Whisper small 49.2 / 25.7 / 25.1 %. By size, Whisper turbo: two dishes 82 / 15 / 2 %, four 72 / 21 / 8 %, six 59 / 18 / 22 %, twelve 38 / 26 / 36 %. Of all dishes said Whisper turbo brings 82.5 % through, Parakeet 73.4 %. Per dish said, 5 to 7 % are taken as a dish the guest did not say on every ear, which is what the single numbers showed; it adds up, so the goal of no silent wrong dish and 90 % right is in reach for an order of two and out of reach for a long one, whatever the rule does. **What follows:** (a) the read-back with its correction decides whether an order is right, so it has to hold through several corrections; (b) a silence of 300 ms cuts 45 % of the orders in two, 500 ms 11 %, 700 ms 3 %: the turn must not end earlier while an order is said; (c) Whisper turbo is the ear for orders; Canary, the new sixth ear that can be held to German (Parakeet cannot, checked in the runtime), is no better than Parakeet here and four times slower; (d) a rule for the words synthetic voices produce is not worth building before real voices are heard. **Next in the programme:** (4) real voices (T-10.7).

**A dish said in a list is never swallowed (found while building the spoken orders of the speech kit, 13.10.2026, evals `abholung_0081` and `abholung_0082`).** Two errors in how the conversation finds the positions of a sentence (`position_parts` in `domain/menu/search.py`), both without any speech recognition involved. (1) **A dish without a quantity later in a list was not its own position.** The sentence alone cuts "Einmal die 13, zweimal die 23 und die S12" into "Einmal die 13" and "zweimal die 23 und die S12", because a Sushi number opens no position without the menu; the second part was then a question about two numbers, and the guest who said "Nein, das wars" got the 13 alone. With a name in that place the same: of "Einmal die 23, zweimal die 47 und Pho Bo" the part "zweimal die 47 und Pho Bo" answered "Einen Moment, ich nehme das der Reihe nach auf" and neither of its dishes was taken. Read as text, 27.5 % of 1,200 generated orders of 2 to 12 dishes from the real menu ended in that question, since 43 of its 173 dishes carry S or SM. Each part the sentence cut off now gets the check with the menu that a whole sentence gets: every piece at the separators that clearly hits a different dish is a position. (2) **A card number the menu does not have, next to a dish by name, vanished with its quantity:** "Zweimal die 99 und Pho Bo" stayed whole because the 99 hit nothing, the name search over the sentence found Pho Bo alone, and the guest got Pho Bo twice without a word about the 99. A piece that is exactly one card number is now a position whether the menu has it or not, and its search says "Nummer 99 gibt es nicht"; any other piece that hits nothing still makes the "und" part of a name ("Ente süß und sauer", "7 Kostbarkeiten und Reis" stay whole). Red first: both eval cases and 12 unit tests; 7 more hold the sentences that must stay whole. The hot path keeps its budget: the extra check runs only for a part that still holds a separator, and the menu is read once per call.

**A trailing letter heard as a word asks which dish instead of taking the plain number (step 1 of Maxi's programme of 12.10.2026: silent wrong numbers to zero, then 90 % right; built 13.10.2026, eval `abholung_0080`).** On the real menu half of the dishes carry a letter behind the number, and the ear writes that letter as a word the number rule read as filler: "Nummer 26d" arrives as "Nummer sechsundzwanzig der", "30a" as "dreißig ja", "27e" as "siebenundzwanzig, die?", and the plain 26 was taken, which the menu has too. Now a word of that kind (`ja` for a, `war` for b, `der` and `dann` for d, `die` for e or g, `äh`, `eine`, `einen` for e, `ist` for f) that stands directly behind a number, with nothing but filler or the quantity behind it, keeps the lettered number as a second candidate, and the search asks by name ("Meinen Sie Nummer 45, Chop Suey mit Huhn oder Nummer 45g, Soße Chop Suey?") or offers the one dish the menu has; such a number is never taken without a question, as with the "ist" in front of a Sushi number (PR #257). **The menu decides (rule 1):** the card format now carries the lettered numbers of the active dishes (`CardFormat.lettered`, read with the prefixes, no extra query), and without a 26d on the menu "Nummer 26 der" is the 26 as before, so a plain order costs no question. "Die 26 bitte" and "Ja, die 26" stay the plain number on every menu; "Nummer 26 ja genau" asks where the menu has 26a and is the 26 where it has not. **The recorded run on the real menu judged again** (`--judge-only`, same transcripts, synthetic voices): the picked ear takes a number the guest did not say in 29 instead of 42 of 1,464 number sentences (2.0 % instead of 2.9 %), on the 692 real card numbers in 14 instead of 27 (2.0 %, trailing letter 4.0 % instead of 7.8 %); 13 utterances moved from a wrong dish to a question, none that was right asks now. The kit's 2 % limit is met by 0.02 points on one run, so this is at the limit, not under it, and not zero. **What is left is nothing a rule can see:** the letter gone without a trace (4), heard as another letter the menu has (6), other digits (4). Those need a better ear for letters, the read-back with its correction (PR #271), and real voices. Red first: 7 lines of the number eval, the conversation case (the 45 was booked silently) and 22 unit tests. **Codex on PR #282 (P1), fixed same-day:** behind a number with a prefix the word was still dropped ("Nummer S sieben ja" took the S7 where the menu has S7a); the lettered number is now a candidate behind every candidate, with or without a prefix. Found in the own review of that round: a quantity behind the word ("die 26 der zweimal") no longer hides it, and "die 26 eine Portion" stays the quantity. **Codex on `3cd6227` (P1, P2), fixed same-day:** a filler behind the word took the question away ("Und die 26 der noch" was the 26), so whatever a pure number sentence may hold behind its number now leaves the candidate standing, "Nummer 35 ja genau" included; and a full stop behind the number ends the order, so "Die 26. Ja, gerne." is the 26 and an answer, not a question about 26a. A hesitation between number and letter is skipped like the comma ("die 26, ähm, der"). **Step 2, the second run on the real menu with all ears (13.10.2026, docs/20 §11): no-go, and no ear is near 90 % right.** Full dish names, key terms per utterance, 1,520 number sentences per ear. Silent wrong over all number sentences: Parakeet v3 2.4 %, Whisper turbo 4.9 %, with key terms 5.0 %, Whisper small 7.2 %. On the 692 real card numbers, right / asked / silent wrong: Parakeet 56.9 / 40.2 / 2.9 %, Whisper turbo with key terms 76.2 / 19.2 / 4.6 %. Three findings: (a) the same ear moved by about one point between the two runs with the same rule (2.0 and 2.9 %), so one run of synthetic voices decides nothing at the limit; (b) none of Parakeet's 20 silent wrong card numbers is a letter heard as a word any more, 11 are the letter not written at all; (c) Parakeet's many questions are not questions of a rule: in 23 % of the card number utterances it wrote the two words in another language or as noise ("Number four.", "Número cuanci."), Whisper with the language set in 3 %, and Whisper turbo understands 91.9 % of the plain numbers and, with key terms, 80.9 % of the dish names letter by letter (25.1 % without). **So the way to 90 % right is the ear, not the number rule.** Step 3 is the paragraph at the top of this summary.

**On the server the application no longer connects as the Postgres superuser (follow-up of #252, Maxi's decision of 12.10.2026, built 13.10.2026).** Until now one database user did everything, so a flaw in the application would have reached every table's definition, the files of the database server and programs on it. Now `.env` can name two: `DATABASE_URL` the application's own role, `OWNER_DATABASE_URL` the owner of the schema (the user the Postgres image creates). `bash scripts/db_role.sh` creates the role with the password of `DATABASE_URL`, lets it read and write every table and nothing else, also on tables a later migration creates, and then logs in as the role and checks what it holds. `bash scripts/migrate.sh` (and `make migrate`) runs Alembic as the owner, in a container of its own so that the running application cannot read the owner's URL from the process list (found by the security review of this branch and reproduced); backup and restore connect as the owner, and a restore gives the role its rights on the restored database before the swap, because a dump comes back without privileges. Two guards: outside `ENV=dev` and `ENV=test` the API does not start when it connects as a superuser, and the Compose file keeps the owner's password and URL out of the environment of `api` and `dispatcher`. The workbench and CI keep the single user, nothing changes there: tests and evals create their own databases. No migration and no data change; an existing volume is moved over by two lines in `.env` and one run of the script (docs/13 §3). Run end to end on a throwaway stack in the production layout: an existing volume moved over, a fresh one set up role first, a migration down and up, backup, restore and the operations view reading the restored database as the role. **Maxi's call:** the start-up refusal is an addition to the decision; it is one function (`refuse_a_superuser_role` in `api/db.py`) and can become a warning.

**The restaurant's limits for a reservation by phone are in the database and the agent keeps them (follow-up of #252, Maxi's decision of 12.10.2026, built 13.10.2026).** Three new fields in `service_config` (migration 007): `reservation_max_days_ahead` (90), `reservation_max_party_size` (8) and `reservation_max_party_size_same_day` (5). The same migration carries the two other limits of #252, `order_approval_above_cents` (15000) and `turn_deadline_seconds` (10), as the order of the follow-ups below says; `confirm` reads the first since the same day (summary above), the phone line reads the deadline since the same day as well (summaries above); neither needed a migration of its own. `check_slot` refuses a wish further ahead than the horizon with a sentence that names the limit and asks for an earlier day; the days are calendar days of the restaurant, so late in the evening the last day can still be booked. A table that is free by the rules is booked by the agent only up to the party limit, on the same day up to the smaller one: above it the answer is `team_callback` with the sentence "Für neun Personen kann ich den Tisch nicht selbst zusagen, das bespricht das Team persönlich mit Ihnen.", and the wish goes to the team as a callback (reason `out_of_scope`, party size, day, time and name in the summary). **The conversation core creates that callback itself, in the turn that learns of it, and the model is not asked again** (`agent/loop.py`, Codex PR #280): a model answers with a sentence or a tool call, never both, so the tool's sentence would end its turn, and a guest who hangs up then would be lost. The turn ends with the tool's sentence, the callback and its sentence. Without a number to call back (caller ID withheld), or when the callback fails, the call goes the way of every handover: a transfer where the team is reachable, otherwise the sentence that nobody can be reached. `create_reservation` runs the same check and its conflict carries the same signal (`TeamDecides`), so a model that skips `check_slot` gets no draft and ends with the team too. The system prompt, the tool reference and the scripted stand-in are unchanged: no call about anything else pays tokens for this, and every addition to the prompt of a reservation call cost cases on the local model (07.10.2026). Checked: 13 new tests on the rule, 7 on the migration (up, down, the bound of each field), eight through the conversation loop (number known, no name yet, a name nobody said, a time given in seconds, draft without check, number withheld with the team reachable and without, failing callback), tests for the endpoint, the dispatch hint, the draft guard and the scripted stand-in; four eval cases (`reservierung_0034` to `0037`), three of them red on `main`; cases 38 of 38 (34 of 34 before, no case changed), targets 1 of 27 as before, number eval 195 of 195; full suite 4,153 passed; `check_slot` p95 16.8 ms, `create_reservation` 28.0 ms. Twelve older capacity tests filled a window with one party of 40 and now lift the limits first (`lift_party_limits` in `conftest.py`). Not measured: a real model on this path, the local model server did not answer on 12.10.2026. Assumptions below.

**The phone line ends a call by the tenant's time limit (audit finding of 12.10.2026, P2).** `service_config.max_call_seconds` exists per tenant, but `CallRouting` did not carry it and `CallHandler` built the conversation loop without a limit, so every call ran on the setting `MAX_CALL_SECONDS` (420 s): with a tenant at 30 s the model answered at second 31 and the call went on. Reproduced on `790627e` with `FakeTelephony`. `call_routing` now returns the tenant's limit with mode and team number, read once at the start of the call, and the handler passes it to the loop (`domain/calls/routing.py`, `telephony/handler.py`). A change in the database applies from the next call, like the mode. What happens at the limit is unchanged: the team, a callback when nobody is there, and the limit is read again after the model's answer. The handler takes a clock so tests set the time instead of waiting. The text phone (`sim/session.py`) ran on the setting until 14.10.2026 and takes the tenant's limit since (#207): on the setting its drafts stopped holding seats at the tenant's limit while the session went on. A tenant at 30 seconds therefore also ends a typed session after 30 seconds.
**The speech kit heard the pilot's real menu (12.10.2026): no-go against the 2 % limit, and the open problem is the trailing letter, not the prefix.** Maxi asked why the kit is not tested with the real menu, where the numbers mean other dishes. `python3 -m scripts.speech_spike --menu imports/menu_items.csv` now takes a menu from outside the repository: its dish names and every one of its card numbers are spoken, and the judge counts against its numbers (`evals/heard.py`); `--judge-only` judges a recorded run again after a change to the number rule. The register copies were converted read-only into the ignored `imports/` folder: 173 dishes, 43 plain numbers, 37 with S, 6 with SM, 87 with a trailing letter; 30 of the 37 S numbers also exist as plain numbers. **One run, synthetic voices, the number rule of PR #257:** over all 1,464 number sentences the picked ear (Parakeet v3) is right in 60.7 %, asks in 36.5 % and takes a number the guest did not say in 2.9 % (2.3 % another dish); the Whisper ears 5.7 to 7.7 %. On the 692 utterances of real card numbers: 57.2 / 38.9 / 3.9 %. Plain numbers, S and SM were never taken wrongly (S and SM at the price of a question in two of three utterances, most of them from the new "ist" rule). **All 27 silent wrong numbers are numbers with a trailing letter** (7.8 % of their 348 utterances, 24 of them another dish): the ear writes the letter as a word ("26 der", "27 die", "30 ja") that the rule reads as filler, and the plain number exists too. 20 of the 27 come from one of the four synthetic voices. Dish names arrive letter by letter in 18 % (Parakeet) and 26 % (Whisper). A key term lifts a name to 90 %, but only 25 of the 173 names fit the ear's prompt at once, and the full menu may go into no model context anyway (hard rule 6): the kit now hands the ear a window of 10 names per utterance, and T-10.5 has to choose the terms per turn (docs/20 §12). **What this does not say:** how large the letter problem is with real callers (T-10.7). **Open, Maxi's decision:** a rule for a loose word behind a number where the menu has that number with a letter (docs/20 §12). Numbers: docs/20 §11; no dish name of the pilot is in the repository.
**A wrong number can be corrected at the read-back (Maxi, 12.10.2026, eval `abholung_0079`).** On the real menu the ear loses or changes the trailing letter of a card number in 7.8 % of such utterances (speech kit, PR #268). Maxi's decision: every number is confirmed at the read-back before booking, and the guest can say "Falsch, ich meinte die Nummer 25c". The confirmation existed; the correction did not: the scripted stand-in answered every no with "Dann noch einmal von vorn" and threw the order away, and three phrasings of that sentence ended with nothing booked. Now one card number named clearly replaces the one dish it can stand for, the one with the same digits ("25c" for a 25d that was read back, "S12" for the 12) or the only dish of the order (`sim/scripted_order.py` `_correction`). Quantity, the note for the kitchen and the rest of the order stay, a mandatory choice of the new dish is asked, and the whole order is read back again before a yes; the draft read back first stays a draft (rule 3). Never a guess: behind the no the sentence has to be the card number alone, so two numbers, a number that is not on the menu, two dishes it could stand for, a time ("um 13 Uhr"), one more dish ("und noch die 13"), a dish named by its name or a bare no start the order again as before.
