A real model is told the same by the system prompt, and the core drops the old draft either way; how well a model does it is measured with T-2.4. Red first: the eval case and 8 unit tests; 13 more hold the cases that must not be read as a correction. **Codex on PR #271 (P1x1, P2x5), fixed same-day:** a lookup that failed without an answer restarted the order instead of handing the call to the team (rule 5); a dish that is out today is now said so; "und die 13" adds a dish and replaces none; a bare quantity before "Nummer" counts; after the mandatory choice of the new dish the order goes straight back to the read-back. Second round (P1, P2): the stand-in read the card numbers from the database while it parsed the guest's sentence, and a database that was gone there ended the call with an exception; it now raises `LLMError`, which the loop turns into the handoff, also for the two older reads of that kind (`_numbers`). A number that is not on the menu is said before the restart. Full suite 4,070 passed, `cases/` 32 of 32 with hard metrics 0, `targets/` 1 of 27, number eval 195 of 195. **Next:** a loose word behind a number ("26 der") asks instead of taking the 26 (docs/20 §12), then the ears again and spoken sentences with several numbers.

**A Sushi number heard as "ist zwölf" is no longer taken as dish 12 (bug from T-10.3, 12.10.2026, eval `abholung_0077`).** Every ear measured writes "S zwölf" as "ist zwölf", and the number rule read "ist" as a filler word: "Zweimal ist zwölf" booked the soup twice without a question. Where the menu has the prefix S, "ist" directly before a number now adds the S number as a second candidate next to the plain one (`CardFormat.prefixes_of` in `api/domain/menu/numberwords.py`, the mechanism "Sushi zwölf" already uses for S12 or SM12), after any lead ("Ich nehme ist zwölf"). **A number that went through the heard word is never taken without a question** (`ItemNumber.heard`): with both dishes on the menu the guest is asked by name ("Meinen Sie Nummer 12, Wan-Tan-Suppe oder Nummer S12, Thunfisch Maki?"), with one of them that one is offered ("Meinen Sie Nummer S1, Lachs Nigiri?", eval `abholung_0078`), because the dish that exists may not be the one that was said. Which letters count comes from the numbers on the menu (rule 1); a menu without S reads "ist" as before. Only pure number sentences change: next to a name the sentence is what it was. The price: "die Nummer ist 23" now gets a confirming question on a menu with the prefix S. **Measured on the recorded transcripts of T-10.3, no new audio:** silent wrong numbers of the picked ear 12 to 8 of 724 (1.1 %), all ears 174 to 142 of 2,929; 32 utterances move from a wrong dish to a question back, none becomes wrong, and 15 utterances of "die Nummer ist 23" move from taken to asked. Red first: 8 lines of the number eval, the conversation case and 8 unit tests. **Codex on PR #257, four rounds, P1x4 and P2x2, fixed same-day except one P2:** a number heard through "ist" was taken when only the S dish existed, then when only the plain one existed, the rule was skipped after a verb, and the scripted stand-in took the offered dish when the guest only repeated "Ist eins" (it now waits for a yes or the name). "Ist zwölf und Pho Bo" lost its first dish, because the unclear number kept the sentence whole; it is a position of its own now (`_position_parts`). Not changed (P2): the two new eval cases keep the suite's German ids and tags (`abholung`, `nummer`), which the mandatory coverage of docs/08 §6 and `--tags` read; they are translated with the suite, not case by case. Full suite 3,864 passed, number eval 195 of 195 (14 lines added), `cases/` 31 of 31 with hard metrics 0, `targets/` 1 of 27. **Not built, two decisions for Maxi (docs/20 §12):** the letter dropped completely ("Ich hätte gerne zwölf"), because asking on every bare number that also exists with S would cost a question on most plain orders; and the ending g heard as "die", two utterances on the picked ear.
**The read-back names the total so that any voice can speak it (bug from Maxi's listening test, 12.10.2026, eval `abholung_0076`).** "Macht 25,70 Euro" was written for a hosted voice that knows prices; our test-phase voice read it as "fünfundzwanzig Komma sieben null Euro". `spoken_euro` (`api/domain/ordering/readback.py`) now writes two plain numbers and no decimal comma: "25 Euro 70", a round amount "7 Euro", below one euro "50 Cent". The surcharge sentence of `search_menu` uses the same function ("50 Cent Aufpreis"). Two forms Maxi did not name are assumptions (below): single cents behind euros carry their unit ("17 Euro 5 Cent"), and a lone one is a word ("ein Euro 50"), because the voice read "1 Euro" as "eins Euro", which it did before this change too. The amount is unchanged, only its wording: totals and surcharges still come from the database (rule 1). Red first: the eval case and the unit tests for each form. So that an eval can be red on a sentence the code writes, a case can now expect a piece of the read-back (`expected.readback`, docs/08 §3); it is judged on what the agent said in the turn of the draft, not on the text the tool returned, so a real model that rewords the amount is red (Codex PR #248, P2x2, fixed same-day). Checked on the voice itself, not only in tests: the phonemes of the Piper voice `thorsten` for every amount from 0 to 300 Euro contain no "Komma" and no "null"; nobody has listened to it yet. Full suite 3,522 passed, `cases/` 28 of 28 with hard metrics 0, `targets/` 1 of 27 and the number eval 181 of 181, each case as on `main`.
**A name or phone number nobody said books nothing (T-2.4 part 4, 12.10.2026).** On the pickup runs of 07.10.2026 a real model sent "Max Mustermann", "Gast" and a number nobody had said to get `draft_order` through, and once wrote the caller ID again with digits missing. The name is read back, the number is not, so an order could end under a number that reaches a stranger. The core now hears the guest's words and numbers itself, in the guest's sentence and before the model is asked (`agent/guest_details.py`, `state.note_guest_details`), and keeps them for the call: words and numbers, no sentence, nothing of it goes to the model. **A name** is taken only when every word of it was a whole word of a guest sentence (umlauts in either spelling) or was spelled letter by letter in one sentence, and it holds nothing but those words. **A number** only when it is the caller ID or the last digits of a guest turn when they begin with an area code or a country code, compared in E.164: another spelling counts, a missing or changed digit does not, and a date, a time or a number without an area code is no phone number. Checked in two places: in `slots` an unsaid name or number is dropped without a hint, the slot stays as it was (an invented number never replaces the caller ID) and it is no success on the ladder; `create_reservation`, `draft_order` and `create_callback` are not dispatched with one, and the hint tells the model to ask the guest or to take `phone` from `slots` digit for digit. With a number in `slots`, a tool call must book under that one. A refused call costs a tool hop, so a model that insists ends with the team. **Codex review PR #244 (P1x1, P2x2) fixed same-day:** a date the guest said passed as a phone number, because `normalize_phone` makes +16092026 of "16.09.2026"; only a run that begins with the zero of an area code or the plus of a country code is a phone number now, and one that begins like a date is none. A name with something added that is no word ("Müller 999") passed, the check only read its words; the name must be nothing but said words. And after a guest named another number than the caller ID, a model could write it into `slots` and still book under the caller ID, both being known in the call; the number in `slots` is now the one a tool call must use. **Limits, by design:** which of the said words is the name stays with the model, the readback follows; a title the guest did not say ("Herr Müller" after "Müller") is refused; a number in words, a number without its area code, digit groups separated by a comma, letter names when spelling and a changed accent are not heard and cost one more question (extended with the ear of the voice layer, T-10.5). **Own review of the head `df98ccf` (Codex out of review quota, its own comment on the PR), fixed same-day:** a run of digits that begins with 0 and is too short to be a number ("um 0 Uhr", "die 07") was kept by its digits and could be put into `slots` in the place of the caller ID, which would have blocked every booking and the callback of the handoff; a number is now E.164 or nothing. A period that ends a sentence joined the number with the digits behind it ("0171 2345678. 19 Uhr passt"), so the number the guest said was refused; the period ends the run now. The hint names the key `phone`, since the answer format of a table call lists no slot names. "(0721) 5551234" written by the model is read like the same text from the guest. A number that is taken stands in `slots` in E.164 (CLAUDE.md §8). A timing test holds the two patterns on guest text linear. No prompt change, no migration. **Codex review of the merge head `c6df625` (P1x1, P2x1), 12.10.2026:** P2 fixed: a dash between blanks joined the number with the digits behind it ("0171 2345678 - 19 Uhr passt"); the first fix read it both ways, which the next review turned down (below). P1 not changed, recorded for Maxi: Codex asks that a run of digits counts only with phone context around it, since a guest could name an order reference that looks like a phone number. This product hands out no such reference (pickup codes are A1, A2, ..., a reservation has none), a number said without any context word is the usual answer to the question for it, and a context rule would refuse exactly that. What remains true is that a number taken from speech is not read back, so a model that picks the wrong one of two said numbers goes unnoticed; that is in Open Points with a proposal. **Codex review of `13ed34f` (P1x2, P2x2), fixed same-day:** reading a spaced dash both ways kept the joined run, a number nobody said (+49171234567819). A run with a comma or a spaced dash between its digits is no number at all now, neither whole nor in parts, since the parts can be a number with digits missing ("0171 234 - 5678"); the guest is asked again. Before a word a comma or a dash ends the number as before. The scripted stand-in handed a pickup or a callback with a refused number to the team, and before the opening hours that ended with "Ich gebe an das Team weiter", no transfer and no callback (replayed: 08:00, withheld caller ID, "555 1234"); it asks for the detail again in all three flows now. A name passed with separators around it ("---Müller---"); a separator counts only between two words of the name. And the letter of a card number was joined to a spelled name ("die 23a ... M E I E R" gave "ameier"); a spelled name is a row of single letters that follow each other. **Independent review of `35f1fac` (Codex out of quota again; a second reader with no part in the fixes), P1x2, P2x3, fixed same-day:** a number interrupted by a filler word, a period or an en dash was still taken with digits missing ("0171 234 äh 5678" gave +49171234, a valid number). Only the last digits of a guest turn can be the number now; digits followed by more digits are none, whatever stands between, which also covers the joined readings of the rounds before. The scripted stand-in still ended a call with a promise and nothing behind it whenever a caller ID was present, since it asked again only when the state lacked the detail (replayed at 03:00 with a caller ID); it now goes by the hint of the refusal and says that the number is needed with its area code. A letter next to a spelled name was still joined to it across a period or an apostrophe ("die 23 a. M E I E R", "ich bin's, M E I E R"); a row of spelled letters takes only blanks, commas and hyphens between them. And a name was checked without its inner blanks but stored with them; it is checked as written, and kept without the blanks around it. **Verification of `31a3a81` by the same independent reader, fixed same-day:** its five findings were closed but for one variant, and the rewrite had brought two regressions. The last digits of a turn could be only the tail of the number ("0171 20, 034567" gave +4934567, and "+43 (0)664 1234567", read at the bracket as two, a German number); the last digits count only when no other digits of the turn may be the head of the number now, and the trunk zero in brackets stays with its number. Letters with periods between them no longer spelled ("M. E. I. E. R."); the period is allowed again, and the letter of a card number is told by its place at the digits instead. **The seven review threads still open, fixed on Maxi's word ("fix the reviews", 12.10.2026):** four had been left for his decision, three came with Codex's review of `9a65efe`. *Phone context (P1) and the superseded caller ID (P2):* digits are a number to call only when the guest's sentence says so ("meine Nummer ist", "erreichbar unter", "Handy", "unter" right before them) or answers the agent's question for one, so "zu meiner alten Bestellung 0171 2345678" names none; and the number the guest gave is written into `slots` by the core itself, so a model that leaves it out can no longer book under the caller ID. *Unspaced hyphen (P1):* one or two digits behind a hyphen, a slash or a closing bracket, after what is a complete number already, leave open whether they belong to it ("0171 2345678-19 Uhr"); the guest is asked again. *Names (P2x3):* a word counts as often as one sentence held it ("Müller Müller"), a period at the end of the name is refused ("Müller."), and letters are compared in one Unicode form. *Spaced date (P2):* a date with blanks and a full year is no head of a number, so "am 01 10 2026, meine Nummer ist ..." is heard. No eval case differs from `main`: every transcript names the number with a word for it or after the question. **Verification of `8835fa3` by the same reader, and Codex on `31a3a81` (P1x1, P2x1):** the reader's findings are closed, nothing raises and nothing is slow on generated input. Two points taken: an hour with a leading zero counted as the head of a number, so "um 09:30 Uhr, 0171 2345678" was not heard; and digits in pairs that look like a date were exempt, so "02 11 55, 01 23 45" left its second half standing as a number. Recorded, not changed: the tail of a number whose head is not told as one, digits joined to a number by an unspaced hyphen (Codex, P1), and a repeated word in a name (Codex, P2); all three in Open Points. **Codex review of `ce93c95` (P1x1, P2x3), 12.10.2026, read against the head `31a3a81`:** two held and are fixed: a date written with blanks ("am 01 10 2026") passed the date check and became +491102026, and the letter of a card number a blank apart was joined to a spelled name ("die 23 a, M E I E R"). One was fixed in `31a3a81` already (a line break inside a name). One is not changed and recorded for Maxi: with a caller ID in the state, a model that ignores another number the guest names and books under the caller ID is not stopped; that is the model's reading of which number applies, as before, and the readback proposal in Open Points now covers this direction too. **The eval case for a refused number found a crash (`reservierung_0031`, added on the own review):** a guest with a withheld caller ID gives the number without its area code, then with it. The scripted stand-in built the draft from the state, where the core had not taken the number, and the turn ended with a `KeyError`; on the text phone that was every such call on the default model. It now asks for the number again, and the case is green in `evals/cases/`. Checked: 141 new tests, 36 of them fail with the check switched off; the full suite; `cases/` 28/28 with hard metrics 0, `targets/` 1/27 and the number eval 181/181, each older case with the same result and the same number of turns as on `main`. **Not measured on a real model:** the run needs the local model server; it is the next step, and it starts against the baseline of `main` (reservations 6 of 6, pickup 1 of 14) with this guard and the kept wish in.

**Ear and mouth for the test phase are measured on the workbench (T-10.3, local engines, 12.10.2026): a recommendation, no clean go.** `python3 -m scripts.speech_spike` builds one image with the open speech engines and runs it without a network while it measures: the 181 sentences of the number eval and the 31 dish names of the eval menu are spoken by four Piper voices, sent through a modelled telephone line (300 to 3,400 Hz, 8 kHz A-law) and transcribed by five ears, 848 utterances each; what an ear heard is judged by the rule that resolves numbers on the phone (`evals/heard.py`): right, asked back, or a number the guest did not say taken without a question. Two full runs. **Ear: Parakeet v3 on the CPU**, 0.15 s for a sentence, so the graphics card stays with the text model (Whisper large-v3-turbo there needs 2.3 GB and is as fast; on the CPU it takes 3.9 s). Silent wrong numbers: 1.5 and 2.1 % for Parakeet against the proposed limit of 2 %, 4.6 to 9.0 % for the three Whisper ears. So the limit is met in one run and missed in the other. Where the errors are: plain numbers ("die 23") are safe, 0 and 1 of 148 utterances; a number with a letter is not, "S12" is heard as "ist zwölf" and taken as dish 12 in 9 to 11 % of 88 utterances, by every ear. That is a gap in the number rule as much as in the ear and is recorded as a task of its own (Open Points). About a third of all number sentences end in a question back instead of the number. **Mouth: the Piper voice `thorsten`**, 0.08 s (medium) or 0.27 s (high) to the first audio; samples for Maxi's ear are in the run folder (D4). **End of a turn:** neither ear reports it, so the call session needs a local silence detector; at 500 ms it kept 98 to 99 % of the sentences in one piece. **Speed:** ear and mouth take 0.24 to 0.41 s of the 1.5 s from voice to voice; the text model on the workbench alone needs more than the rest, so that threshold is a number for the server. **What this does not say:** the callers are synthetic voices, which mispronounce dish names (18 to 37 % heard letter by letter, 81 % when Whisper is given the menu's names), the line is a model, and single sentences flip between runs. Real voices over a real line are T-10.7. **Open:** Maxi confirms or changes the thresholds (docs/20 §6); the EU services for operation are not measured, they wait for accounts. Numbers: docs/20 §11, handover §10.

**Call audio runs over the media channel on the workbench (T-10.1 step 1, 11.10.2026): go, 28 of 28 points.** `python3 -m scripts.voice_spike` starts three containers on their own network, without a published port: a test PBX (Asterisk 22.10.1, the distribution's 20.6 is too old), a scripted softphone (a second Asterisk that dials in over SIP, plays a tone, presses keys and records what it hears) and a probe that stands where the service `voice` will stand (`api/telephony/probe.py`). The kit places the calls and judges them from the probe's logs and the softphone's recordings. Measured with tones, not speech: audio to the caller (4.00 s heard of 4 s sent) and from the caller (one 640-byte frame every 19.96 ms, 16 kHz), the caller number, every pressed digit, playback that Asterisk paces itself (4 s sent at once, queue empty after 4.01 s), playback stopped by a key (15 s queued, the tone ended within one or two 20 ms frames of the flush, which is as fine as the method resolves), a hangup that waits until everything was spoken, and three calls at once with their own tones, numbers and digits. So the three things the call session rules of docs/20 §4 need from the line exist: know when a sentence was spoken, stop a sentence, end the call after the last word. With the voice service unreachable `Dial` fails after one try and the dialplan continues, which is where the outage fallback of T-10.2 goes. **Built to stay:** the wire format of the media channel (`api/telephony/adapters/asterisk.py`) with the recorded frames as test material for T-1.11, and `api/telephony/audio.py` (tones and their detection). **Not answered by step 1:** speech, the delay from mouth to ear, a softphone outside the Docker network, and everything at the pilot's router (step 2, waits for Maxi's access; G0 still waits for T-10.1 and T-10.2). The media socket of the spike has no access control and the Asterisk image is a third-party build pinned by digest; both are noted for T-1.11 in docs/20 §12. Handover and numbers: docs/20 §10 and §11.

**The state says what the guest called for from the first sentence on (T-2.4 part 4, 08.10.2026).** Until now the core set `intent` only at the draft, and a model keeps nothing between turns: on the pickup runs of 07.10.2026 the turn after "ich möchte etwas zum Abholen bestellen" began from a state without that wish, and "Es zwölf bitte" was read as a table for twelve. A model cannot write `intent`, it is no guest slot. The core now hears the wish in the guest's own sentence before the model is asked (`agent/intent.py`, `state.note_intent`): closed word lists for a pickup and for a table, the way `escalation.py` hears its triggers, no tokens and no prompt text. Strict on purpose, since a wrong wish would stand in every turn after it. Only the first wish of a call is taken: a word in a later sentence never replaces it ("dürfen wir unseren Hund mitnehmen?" in a call about a table), it is never taken against what the state shows already (an order being put together, or a party size or a time), and from the draft on `intent` follows the tools as before. A sentence that names both (a pickup word next to anything table-like), none, a delivery, or rules something out ("nicht zum Abholen", "ohne", "statt") says nothing. "Tisch" counts only as a whole word (a guest named Tischler asks for no table) and "Platz" not at all, it stands in addresses. "bestellen" alone is a pickup unless something table-like is named ("einen Vierertisch bestellen" says nothing); with "Tisch" it is a reservation. **Limits:** a guest who changes their mind before a draft keeps the first wish in the state until a tool of the other flow drafts, and words are not understanding ("ich wollte meine Jacke abholen" as the first such sentence is heard as a pickup). If the call log shows either often, the model writes the wish instead, which needs a line in the answer format and a run against the baseline. It is a hint for the model, not a decision: which tool is called stays with the model, the guards are unchanged. Side effect: a call that ends before a draft is now logged with its wish in `calls.intent` instead of empty, also one that goes to the team on an escalation trigger in the same sentence. A complaint is the exception: one about an order is no request for a pickup, its `intent` stays empty and the reason of the transfer says what the call was. Checked: 72 new tests, the full suite, the detector on every guest sentence of both eval sets (291 sentences, none read as the other wish, none of the 90 in delivery cases read as a pickup), `cases/` 27/27 with hard metrics 0 and `targets/` 1/27 as before on the scripted stand-in, which keeps its own detection. **Not measured on a real model:** the local model server was down on 08.10.2026. The state of a reservation call now carries `intent` from the first turn, and every change to what that model reads has moved cases before, so the first full run starts with this against the baseline of `main` (reservations 6 of 6, pickup 1 of 14).

**The evals count what went through, not what a model tried (T-2.4 part 4, 07.10.2026; Maxi's decision).** A `confirm` without a yes and a dish no search delivered are hard violations only when the call went through. Until now every attempt counted, also one the core had refused: on the first runs with a real model three pickup cases were right in the database and the run still failed with "unconfirmed transactions". `evals/recorder.py` now takes its note back when the result of the call comes back failed (refused by the core or rejected by the tool) and counts it as a refused attempt, a soft number in the report (`refused_attempts`) that shows how much a model leans on the core. A refused confirm is also no confirm for the repeat of a case and for the count of bookings without one; a refused draft is no draft a later yes could answer. A call whose result never comes back keeps its entry. The scripted stand-in is unchanged: 27/27, hard metrics 0, refused attempts 0.

**Own voice layer instead of a hosted platform; G0 stays open (decisions by Maxi, 07.10.2026).** Maxi set out to close G0 and decided on the way to drop the hosted voice platform (E1 flipped, `docs/00_PCF.md` §3). Asterisk takes the call on our own server, a service `voice` turns speech into text, drives the existing conversation core and speaks the answer (`docs/20_VOICE_LAYER.md`). It starts as a chain (speech-to-text, core, text-to-speech), because the core, its guards and the eval suite then stay as they are; a model that hears the audio itself and a speech-to-speech model are measured against the chain later (T-10.10) and replace it only by numbers. The product must not depend on a restaurant's router or carrier, and nothing heavy runs in the restaurant (E16). The test phase is meant to cost nothing: the workbench hosts, ear and mouth are free local models, and the software registers as one more telephone at the pilot's router. The EU server (D3) and a SIP trunk come with operation. Budget ceiling 100 EUR a month and 300 EUR once (D17). Complaints are to be resolved by the AI by fixed rules from the database, else a callback (D19, E8 changed, not built). The legal check is worked through by cross-checking two AI models, without a lawyer (D20, Maxi's decision, limits recorded there; answered 20.10.2026 with D9 decided: Maxi himself, the duties before the first real call are in `docs/09_OPERATIONS_LEGAL.md` §1). The stage order is confirmed (E2). **G0 is not passed:** Maxi wants the telephony path measured first, so the gate waits for T-10.1 and T-10.2 at the pilot's router. The work packages are Block 1c in `docs/07_WORKPACKAGES.md` (T-10.1 to T-10.12, T-1.11 rewritten, T-9.1 dropped). A package that needs Maxi's PC carries `Environment: Ubuntu (WSL) on the workbench`, and `/task` checks that before it reads anything else (`docs/12_CLAUDE_CODE_PLAYBOOKS.md` S10). Nothing of the voice layer is built or measured: every price and product property in docs/20 is web research of 07.10.2026 and listed under the assumptions. The comparison of hosted platforms, speech-to-speech models and telephone access with vendor names is kept outside the repository.

**The order travels in the conversation state (T-2.4, the cart, 07.10.2026), so a real model keeps it across turns.** A model gets the compact state and one input per request and keeps nothing itself; until now a dish found in one turn was gone in the next, and a pickup order over several turns could not work on a real model. Now the model writes the whole order as `cart` in its answer, in the form of `items` of `draft_order`, and the state sends it back with every turn. **What the core decides (`agent/guards.py` `take_cart`, `agent/state.py`):** a cart is taken whole or not at all, and only when every line names a dish from a clear match of `search_menu` or a candidate the guest was asked about by name (rule 2); a refused cart stops the whole answer, neither its sentence nor its tool call is carried out, and the model gets a hint. Number, name and the mandatory options still open (`open`) are added to a line by the state from the search result; a name or a price a model writes there is dropped, and no price goes into the state at all (rule 1). A cart that differs from the order that was read back, or one that was refused, drops the draft that waits for its yes, an order or a reservation, so a later yes cannot confirm what the guest just changed or a table the conversation has left (rule 3); a successful `draft_order` makes its items the cart, a confirmed order empties it. A `cart` left blank (`null`, `""`, `{}`) says nothing; a list is the order, also the empty one, so a guest can remove the last dish and the draft that held it is gone (both from the Codex review of PR #237, P1x2: in the first version `[]` counted as "unchanged" and a reservation that was read back survived a changed cart). Second round of that review (P1, P2x2), fixed: `false` or `0` as `cart` is refused instead of read as blank; a dish that was sold out at its search does not get into the cart; and the same order in another sequence of lines or options, or with an option in another spelling (third round, P2), is no change, so a model that repeats the order the other way round with its `confirm` does not cost a second readback. Last round on the merge head (P2x3, recorded at the merge of PR #237 and fixed in the follow-up PR): a refused cart stands in `called`, so the model sees that it tried; the options of a cart line are checked against what the search delivered for the dish, the way `draft_order` checks them; and a `draft_order` whose items differ from the `cart` of the same answer is refused. **The rules for the cart go out only in a call about an order.** The answer format has two versions (`agent/llm.py`): the short one of before, and `ORDER_FORMAT` with the field `cart`, its rules, the names of the slots (the model wrote `customer_name` and lost the name) and the sentence that a cart is no order yet (the model called `confirm` on its cart). The client sends the long one only while the state carries `cart`, which it does from the first menu search of a call on. A call about a table sends what it sent before: about 2,240 estimated tokens, an order call about 2,430 of 2,600. **Also new, in every call:** on tool hops the state lists the tools already called in the turn (`called`), because the model asked `get_service_status` again on its own result until the hop limit. **Taken out again:** a first version also showed the dishes a search had found that were not in the order yet; on the real model the candidate the guest had not chosen stayed in sight and was ordered two turns later, so only the order goes back to the model. **Measured (local llama-server, `qwen3.6-35b-a3b`, invented cases only; the model answers the same prompt the same way, two identical runs gave identical results):**

| What the model gets | Reservation cases (6) | Pickup cases (14) |
|---|---|---|
| as on `main`: short format, no cart | 6 passed | 0 passed, 10 ended with the team |
| as built: cart in the state, its rules after the first menu search, `called` | 6 passed | 1 passed, 6 ended with the team |
| cart rules in every request (tried, not kept) | 1 to 3 passed over three variants | 0 to 5 passed over five variants |

So the cart costs the reservations nothing and moves pickup from 0 to 1 of 14: the state now carries the order, and in the replays the model drafts the right order from it turns later (`abholung_0002`, `abholung_0003`), but that is not what keeps this model from finishing a pickup call. It loses the thread: it greets and asks the service status again in later turns, drafts before it has a name, calls `confirm` without a draft, and copies an order id wrongly on the final yes. Every addition to the prompt of a reservation call cost cases (4 of 6 with the slot names, 3 with the cart text, 1 with both), which is why a table call gets none of it. In each pickup run up to three more cases were right in the database and red only because the model tried a `confirm` the core refused. Three gaps the runs showed (invented name and phone number, the eval counting refused calls as hard violations, the guest's wish not kept across turns) are in "Open Points from Reviews" and are the start of part 4.

**Simulator console in the browser (T-2.5, 07.10.2026).** `/gui/dev/console` is the text phone of `sim/` as a page: a button starts a call, a field sends what the guest says, and each turn shows the guest sentence, the tool calls with their duration, the agent's answer and the state, as `sim.cli` prints them. It writes into the same database, so a booking made there appears on the tablet. The router (`api/gui/dev.py`) is mounted only when `ENV=dev`; in any other environment the path is a 404. Open calls live in the API process (one worker), a restart drops them and leaves the call row open; hanging up writes `calls.outcome` through `SimCall.finish`. Checked with 19 tests (conversation to call log, tool calls with duration, empty sentence, unknown call, missing tenant, `HX-Request` required, absent outside dev, concurrent sentence and hang-up, concurrent starts, failed hang-up and failed turn retried); not yet clicked through in a browser. No migration. **Codex review PR #236 (P1x1, P2x4) fixed same-day:** the tests build their own app in dev mode, because CI runs with `ENV=test` where the global app has no console; a closing turn (goodbye after a confirmed booking) now hangs the call up instead of offering the input again; starting a call hangs up any call the reloaded page lost; a gone call or a missing tenant answers 200 with the problem text and a "Neuer Anruf" button, because htmx does not swap a 4xx. Later rounds added a lock per call and one for starts, keep a call until its finish succeeds, drop a stale call that cannot be finished, show failed taps on the page, end a call whose turn failed, and write the call row only after the model and loop are built.

**The text phone and the evals can run on a real model (T-2.4, part 3 of 4, 06.10.2026); the default and CI stay on the scripted stand-in.** `python -m sim.cli --model qwen3:14b`, `python -m sim.replay <case> --model ...` and `make eval MODEL=...` send the conversation to the model of that name on the server of `LLM_BASE_URL`; without a server the run stops with a message instead of falling back to the script. What a model gets on every request: the system prompt, the tool reference (the tool sections of `prompts/tools_v2.md`, without the file's notes for the voice platform) and the answer format, about 2,240 estimated tokens together. **Budget for the whole system message: 2,600 (Maxi, 06.10.2026)**, tested in `test_agent_prompt.py`; the 800 of docs/05 §5 stays the budget of `system_v2.md` alone; to be shrunk with the numbers of the first eval run. The compact state now carries `now`, the restaurant's local date with weekday, because a model has no clock. `LLM_REASONING_EFFORT` is sent as `reasoning_effort` when set; `qwen3` needs `none` (docs/18 §5). The eval report shows tokens per case and the model cost per case (the run priced once, then divided); the recorder passes the count of the model on. The text phone prints one line with model, requests, tokens and cost when a call ends, since it configures no logging and the log line was lost there. `Usage.model` is the name the server answered with, not only the configured one. **Not built in that part, on purpose:** the cart in the state, without which a real model could not keep found dishes from one turn to the next; it followed on 07.10.2026 (paragraph above). It touches hard rules 1 and 2 (what a model may write into the state) and is routed to effort `xhigh`, so it is its own PR. Reservations and single-turn requests are not affected. **First run on a real model (06.10.2026, local llama-server, `qwen3.6-35b-a3b`, invented cases only):** the reservation case `reservierung_0001` runs through four turns to a confirmed reservation in the database (12 requests, 31,481 + 1,249 tokens); the six reservation cases of the suite give 4/6 with hard metrics 0, false escalation 33 % and about 28,800 tokens per case. Three things had to change for that, each found by the run: **a sentence next to a tool call no longer rejects the answer, the tool call wins (Maxi, 06.10.2026)**, since the model did both on its very first turn and every call ended in a handover; the readback-plus-`confirm` case that the rule was for is stopped by the guards. The guest's sentence rides along on tool hops of the same turn (`guest_said`), or the model called `get_service_status` until the hop limit. A rejected tool call tells the model which field is wrong (`hint`, never the value), or it repeated `create_reservation` without the phone number instead of asking for it. And `dispatch` drops `call_id` and `tenant_id` from a model's arguments: the model passed invented ones and the adapter crashed on two values for one keyword. **Seen and left for part 4:** the model asks `get_service_status` at the start of every turn, which costs a request each time; its closing sentence named the wrong weekday; two of six cases ended in a handover. **Own review of PR #235** (Codex out of quota), fixed: an unusable model left an open call row behind, the phone-line handler sent the prompt without the tool reference, the date in the state used the timezone of the settings instead of the tenant's, a crashed eval case lost its token count, and a run in which no request reached the model now fails with a hint instead of looking like a model that fails every case. The four findings first left open are fixed as well (Maxi, 07.10.2026): `--now` without an offset is refused at the argument, the eval runner closes the model client of each case, a model other than `LLM_MODEL` gets no cost because the prices of the settings are not its own (`Usage.priced`), and `usage` is read the same way on the client, the recorder and `SimCall`.

**Model selection (06.10.2026): shortlist for operation, local test model, setup script; the final model is measured in T-5.3.** `docs/18_MODEL_SELECTION.md` derives the requirements from the hard rules (German, one JSON object per turn, rule following, model turn about 0.6 to 0.8 s, EU processing with a DPA) and estimates cost per call: about 15,000 input and 1,000 output tokens per call (assumption) cost 0.3 ct (Mistral Small) to 2 ct (Claude Haiku 4.5); the voice platform's minutes dominate the bill, so evals, latency and the EU path decide, not the token price. Maxi chose: shortlist Mistral Small (favourite, EU, open weights so the workbench runs the same family), Claude Haiku 4.5 (quality reference, needs a second client class) and Gemini Flash via Vertex EU; locally `qwen3:14b` with `mistral-small` for comparison on the RTX 4080 (16 GB, 64 GB RAM; measured on the workbench, see item 10 of What's next); 1 to 3 agents as schedule plus overflow (weekdays 1, Friday to Sunday evenings up to 3, overflow rings the team), built after D1 because the Fritz!Box allows one AI call today. `scripts/setup_local_llm.sh` installs Ollama on Ubuntu or WSL, picks the model by GPU memory, pulls it and checks one invented order line against the answer contract. Run in the cloud container (CPU, `qwen3:4b`): **Qwen3 over `/v1/chat/completions` reasons until the 600-token limit and answers empty unless the request carries `"reasoning_effort": "none"`** (87 s and no JSON without it, 12 s cold with it); the core has to send it (T-2.4 part 3). Both small models tried answered with a sentence and a tool call at once, which the contract rejects. The Ollama installer needs `zstd` on a minimal Ubuntu; the script installs it. GPU numbers come from Maxi's run (handover in docs/18 §5).

**A call now knows what its model cost (T-2.4, part 2 of 4, 06.10.2026).** The model client counts the tokens the server reports for every answer, also for one outside the contract; a failed request counts as a request without tokens, and missing or odd numbers add nothing instead of being estimated. When the text phone closes a call that ran on a real model, `calls.model` and `calls.cost_cents` are written and one log line "model usage" carries the call, the model, the number of requests and both token counts. Prices come from `LLM_INPUT_CENTS_PER_MTOK` and `LLM_OUTPUT_CENTS_PER_MTOK` (euro cents per million tokens); without both the cost stays empty, and a call on the scripted stand-in gets neither model nor cost. No migration: both columns exist since 001. **Known ceiling:** the cost is whole cents per call, rounded up, so a budget is never undercut, but a call on a cheap model counts as 1 cent where it cost 0.3. The exact tokens are in the log line only; a token column on `calls` is due when a KPI needs the exact cost (T-8.3). Measured on a local model with invented sentences: about 1,180 prompt tokens per request with `system_v2` and the answer format, before the tool reference is in the prompt; the 800-token budget of docs/05 §5 is for the system prompt alone and was set before the answer format existed, to be looked at with part 3. The eval report still shows no tokens: the recorder between core and model does not pass the count on yet (part 3). **Codex review PR #211 (P1x1, P2x2) fixed same-day:** a request whose tokens are unknown (a failed one, or an answer without usable numbers) is counted as `unmetered`, and one such request makes the cost of the whole call unknown instead of pricing the metered part as if it were everything; negative prices are rejected when the settings load; a call that ended before the model was asked once gets no model in the call log and no log line.

**A real model can sit behind the conversation core (T-2.4, part 1 of 4, 06.10.2026); nothing uses it yet.** `ChatCompletionsLLM` in `api/agent/llm.py` implements the existing `LLMClient` interface over the chat completions API. Which model answers is `LLM_BASE_URL`, `LLM_MODEL` and `LLM_API_KEY`: a local model on the workbench for tests with invented data, a hosted one with EU processing for operation (`docs/13_DEPLOYMENT.md` §0), with no code change between them. The model answers with one JSON object: a sentence or a tool call, never both, plus what the guest named (`slots`) and what was not understood (docs/05 §5). A sentence next to a tool call is rejected instead of picked from, because the sentence may be the readback and the tool `confirm` (rule 3). A timeout, an HTTP error, an unreachable server or an answer outside the contract is `LLMError`, and `loop.py` now hands the call to the team on it with the outage sentence (rule 5); before, such an error would have left the loop as an exception. Run once against two small local models (Ollama) with three invented inputs: the request goes out and the answer comes back as a turn; a first call took 18 to 20 s because the model loads, later calls 0.5 to 0.6 s; one of six answers was outside the contract and became `LLMError`. That says the plumbing works, nothing about quality. Seen in that run and open for part 4: a model copied a tool result (`open`, `closes_at`) into `slots`, where it would stand in the next prompt as if the guest had said it; a later turn could then answer from it instead of asking the tool (rule 1). **Codex review PR #208 (P1x1, P2x4) fixed same-day:** when the core gives up, the team is not reachable and no number for a callback is known, the guest no longer hears a sentence that promises the team ("Ich verbinde Sie", "Ich gebe an das Team weiter") while the call simply ends; `_handoff` has one last-resort sentence for that, `SAY_NOBODY_REACHABLE`, and it holds for every way into the handoff (model outage, time limit, tool-hop limit, ladder, escalation), not only the new one. The wording is a draft (docs/05 §6). Empty slot values (`null`, `""`) are dropped, so a model that fills unused fields cannot erase the caller's number; an answer is limited to `LLM_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (600), a cut-off one is no valid turn; `.env.example` names 60 s as the timeout for a local model, because the first call loads it, and says that `host.docker.internal` exists on Docker Desktop only. The text phone and the evals still run on the scripted stand-in: token count and cost (part 2), `--model` plus the tool reference in the prompt (part 3) and the first eval run (part 4) are open.

**The conversation core holds hard rules 1 to 3 itself (issue #216, 06.10.2026), a precondition for wiring a real model.** Until now only the scripted stand-in and test doubles drove the loop, and both follow the rules by construction; the system prompt asks a real model to follow them, which is no enforcement. Found by the Codex review of PR #208 (P1x3). Now the loop asks `agent/guards.py` before it dispatches a tool call. **Rule 3:** `confirm` needs an explicit yes in the guest's current sentence to the draft that was read back before this turn; a draft built or replaced inside the turn does not count, so "Ja, guten Tag, einen Tisch ..." confirms nothing. The yes detector moved from `evals/recorder.py` to `agent/consent.py` and both use it. **Rule 2:** `draft_order` takes a `menu_item_id` only from a clear match of `search_menu` in this call, or from a candidate of an unclear result that the guest heard, by its full name or as "Nummer <card number>" in the sentence that ended a turn; the core remembers both in the conversation state and never sends them to the model. **Rule 1:** a model writes only guest details into `slots` (`GUEST_SLOTS`: party size, date and time, name, phone, note); a tool result copied there is dropped and is no success on the understanding ladder. A refused call goes back to the model as a failed tool result with a `hint`, stands in `calls.tool_calls` and costs one tool hop, so a model that insists ends in the handoff. Also from that review (P2): the call time limit is read again when the model has answered, a late answer is neither spoken nor dispatched. Checked: full suite, evals `cases/` 26/26 with hard metrics 0 and `targets/` 1/27 as before, number eval 181/181; with the guards switched off, 19 of the new tests fail. **Limits, by design:** which of several offered dishes the guest's answer means stays with the model (measured by the evals, followed by the readback and its yes); the yes detector is strict and its word lists are hand-made, so an unusual yes ("von mir aus") costs one more question, never a booking; the order a model keeps across turns is its own field with its own guard since 07.10.2026 (`cart`, first paragraph), not a guest slot. **Open from the same review:** the token budget of the system prompt (docs/05 §5, 800) is measured on `prompts/system_v2.md` alone, while PR #208 appends an answer format of about 178 estimated tokens to every request; that needs PR #208 on `main` and Maxi's decision on a budget for the whole system message. The T-2.4 row in `docs/07_WORKPACKAGES.md` is updated by PR #208, not here. **Codex review PR #222 (P1x3, P2x1) fixed same-day:** a yes is now a sentence that is nothing but the assent, an assent word plus words that carry no order; before, any sentence holding "gern", "ja" or "okay" counted, so "Ich hätte gerne noch eine Suppe", "Ja, die 23" and "Ja, kein Reis" would have let a `confirm` through. One closed rule instead of a list of what must not follow a yes, and it tightens the eval metric "unconfirmed" with it. A candidate of an unclear search becomes usable only when the guest heard it, not simply because the next turn began: a model that asks for the phone number instead of passing the offer on makes no candidate usable; an offer that comes a turn later (the allergy question goes first, Codex PR #139) still counts. A late answer of the model gives its `state_patch` to the state before the handoff, so a phone number said in that sentence still reaches the callback. `agent/consent.py` is English. **Codex review of the merged head (P1), fixed in a follow-up PR:** the name of an offered dish counted as heard when it stood inside another word, so "Welchen Preis möchten Sie wissen?" made a dish called "Reis" usable; a name now counts only as whole words.

**"ohne statt Reis" no longer crashes the wish classification (06.10.2026, eval `abholung_0075`).** When "statt" or "anstatt" followed "ohne", "kein" or "keine" directly, `classify_wish` in `api/domain/menu/wishes.py` called itself with the same text until `RecursionError`: `_split_addition` steps one word back from "statt" to take what is wanted instead, landed on the removal word itself and returned an empty removal with the whole text as the addition. Reached from `search_menu` through the wish of a position ("die 23 ohne statt Reis"); found by comparing the menu functions on generated sentences while fixing the CodeQL alerts (PR #220). Now the part in front of the addition must contain the removal word; if it does not, nothing left out is named and the wish is `unknown`: the agent says it cannot offer it and takes the dish as it is on the menu, nothing goes to the kitchen as a free note. A removal that is named stays noted ("ohne Zwiebeln, kein statt Reis" keeps "ohne Zwiebeln"). Checked: the eval case and four unit cases were red before (`RecursionError`) and are green; on 200,000 generated sentences `main` crashed 9 times, this branch never, and no result changed where `main` did not crash. 2979 tests, 22 latency tests, the eval suite (27 of 27) and the number eval (181 of 181) are green.

**CodeQL alerts 3 to 12 (06.10.2026): the ten patterns on caller text in `domain/menu/split.py` and `wishes.py` run in linear time.** GitHub code scanning listed ten open High alerts (`py/polynomial-redos`) on `main`; the green `CodeQL` check on each PR only said the PR added none. Measured before the fix: every one is a real quadratic, a run of 20,000 spaces, tabs, commas or letters took 3 to 16 s in `split_positions`, `raw_pieces`, `separator_pieces`, `wish_candidates` and `classify_wish`. A tool call was never that slow, because `SearchMenuRequest.query` is capped at 300 characters (about 4 ms worst case), but `sim/` and any later caller reach these functions without the cap. The fix: no pattern begins with a repetition any more. `_SEPARATOR` takes the whitespace behind a separator only, the piece before is stripped; in `wishes.py` the separators in front of a match are removed in plain code (`_separators_start`, `_cut_tail`, `_without`, `_drop_trailing_glue`), and the pattern for compound allergy words is the scanner `_compound_stems`. A first attempt with look-behinds was linear too, but CodeQL reads a look-behind in Python as text that is consumed, so it kept reporting `_SEPARATOR`; it only stopped reporting the others because they were built from f-strings, which it does not read. That version was dropped: every pattern is a plain literal the analysis can see. `api/tests/test_domain_menu_regex_cost.py` holds both halves: ten timing cases (red before: 103 s, now 0.2 s in total) and a comparison of each operation with the way it was written before on 4,000 generated sentences, which fails for a deliberately wrong variant of every operation. Every public and private function of both modules was also compared with `main` on 500,000 generated sentences, without a difference; that comparison caught one slip on the way (the joining word before the next part of a wish was matched case-insensitively for a moment, on `main` it is lower case only). 2988 tests, 22 latency tests, the eval suite (26 of 26) and the number eval (181 of 181) are green. Two things seen on the way and left as they are, both in Open Points: `_split` reads the growing part again at every separator, and a joining word is cut off the end of a word it is only the ending of. The third, an endless recursion in `classify_wish` on "ohne statt", is fixed (entry above).

**Real register menu, import dry run (04.10.2026): 173 dishes and 797 options would be imported, nothing blocks technically, the real run waits for Maxi.** Converter on the register copies: 173 dishes taken (130 with a plain number or a letter behind it, 37 `S`, 6 `SM`, in 13 categories), 797 options (4 dishes with the required size group klein/groß, 106 dishes with 3 to 11 of 11 distinct extras, none free or negative), 173 allergen rows, all empty. Not taken: 376 blocked rows, 117 in groups without phone orders (72 drinks, 30 lunch menus M/VM in groups 100 to 102, 15 others), 1 placeholder; no number is rejected. 5 errors, one class: allergen letters in the register without a checker (the four known test entries plus one Sushi dish that passes since T-4.12) - those dishes are imported with "no information". 6 warnings in 5 classes: 2 extras cut at 16 characters, 4 names cut at 40, size 6 without a name on 13 dishes (3,60 to 12,50 cheaper), an action price `A_PREIS1` on 23 dishes (4,60 to 9,60 below the regular price), 76 dishes whose recipe names an allergen carrier while the allergens are empty. With `--allergens-confirmed-by` the converter ends with 0 errors and the import would write allergens for those 5 dishes. `import_menu --dry-run --deactivate-missing` against a copy of the local test database, brought to migration 005: exit 0, 166 dishes new, 7 changed (the demo dishes there share their numbers with register dishes and are overwritten; their 7 prices change only with `--apply-price-changes`), 797 options new and 3 removed, 9 demo aliases removed, nothing deactivated, one warning: no dish has an alias, there is no `item_aliases.csv` for the register menu yet. All 173 numbers resolve to exactly their dish in `numberwords`, in eight typed forms (with and without "Nummer", with a space, in lower case, with a quantity); spoken forms with number words or spelled letters were not tried on the real numbers. Found on the data, not in the reports: 8 Sushi dishes share 3 names, and one dish name carries a "free from" claim with no allergen maintained. **Bug found and fixed:** the script's session has autoflush off, so a dry run never sent options, allergens and aliases to the database and could pass where the real import fails; it now flushes before it rolls back, and the real data passes with that too. From the review of PR #169: the script reports a database rejection as a message with exit 1 instead of a stack trace, `apply` rolls back when that flush fails, and `parse` names a NUL character with file and line. The import without `--dry-run` was not run, not even on a throwaway copy; steps and backup are in "What's next" 3.

**Card numbers with a prefix and the ending g (T-4.12, 26.09.2026): the Sushi and sauce numbers from the register are understood.** T-4.11 dropped 50 dishes because search and import only knew a number up to 999 plus a to f. The register has `S1` to `S53` and `SM1` to `SM6` (Sushi) and `25G` to `60G` (sauces). Which prefixes exist is not in the code: `numberwords.CardFormat` reads them from the active numbers of the card (`items.card_format`, hard rule 1), and a one-word category whose numbers all carry a prefix becomes a spoken word for it. So "S zwölf", "Es zwölf", "S 12", "SM eins", "Es Em eins" find S12 and SM1, "Sushi zwölf" looks up S12 and SM12 and takes the one on the card, "Sushi eins" asks "S1 oder SM1?". The ending g works like a ("fünfundzwanzig G", "25 g"). Never a guess: "S 13" is not dish 13 and "Nummer 23g" is not 23, both are `not_found` when the card lacks them. The import accepts `s12`, `sm1`, `25g` (duplicates by `canonical_card`, `S7` equals `S07`), the tablet sorts S2 before S12 and finds "S1" in its search field. Red first: 20 of the first 26 new lines in `evals/cases/nummern.jsonl` and the four prefix cases among the five new conversation cases (`abholung_0070` to `0074` since eval suite v2) were red on `main`. Now number eval 181/181 (68 lines added in total, including the review rounds of PR #155), the conversation suite v2 `cases/` 26/26 with hard metrics 0, `search_menu` p95 under 300 ms with 260 dishes. Review PR #155 tightened the rules: "es" before a number only means S at the start of the sentence, also after a wish or greeting ("Ich hätte gern Es zwölf", "Hallo, Es zwölf"), never after a verb ("nehme ich es zwei"); a prefixed number directly next to a name ("S12 Lachs") or after a digit asks back, separated by "und" or a comma ("S12 und Pho Bo") it is two positions like a plain number; a category word only counts when none of its numbers lacks a prefix; a prefix before a number above 999 ("S1000", "Sushi tausend") is a named number and `not_found`, with or without "Nummer", read back with a space ("s tausend"); the import rejects prefixes that already mean a quantity, marker or spoken word (`x`, `st`, `nr`, `no`, `hm`, `ja`, `es`, `so`, `um`, `zu`, `da`, `du`, `er`, `ob`) with its own message. After "Nummer", a card number written in one piece ("S12", "Z12h", "ZZ1000zz", "1000zz") is read as one unit (`numberwords._glued_card`): a known prefix in range is a number, everything else the whole piece as a named number that does not exist, instead of one special case per combination. Next to a name, "250g" stays in the name search as a weight. The agent reads the card format once per `search_menu` tool call. Lunch menus `M`/`VM` (groups 100 to 102) are not offered by phone and stay excluded in the converter (Maxi 04.10.2026).

**Menu from the register (T-4.11, 26.09.2026): the six `.dbf` copies become the import CSVs.** `python -m scripts.kasse_to_csv imports/kasse --out imports` reads the register tables read-only (own dBase reader, standard library, cp437 from the file header) and writes `menu_items.csv`, `item_options.csv`, `item_allergens.csv`. On the real files: 123 dishes, 751 options (soups as a required group klein/groß, extras such as a sauce change at 3,50 per dish group). Worked out on the real rows: `GRPREIS(n-1)` is the surcharge for size n (mask: klein 6,50, groß 11,00), size names 1 normal, 2 klein, 3 groß, 4 party from the register mask; extras hang on the dish group (`WRGSHOW`), their price is a price level from `zutgrp` (`ZPREIGRP3`); there is no lock column, the register marks a blocked article as deleted. Migration 004 `menu_items.pos_code` keeps the register spelling (`35B`), the import takes it as an optional column; `--deactivate-missing` sets dishes missing from the file inactive, dry-run lists them first. Not taken at that time: 50 dishes whose numbers the search could not resolve (`25G`, sushi `S1`...`SM6`; taken since T-4.12, see above), four register allergen entries (test entries, need `--allergens-confirmed-by`), size 6 without a name.

**Number sentences from eval suite v1 (26.09.2026): two phrasings resolved wrong in `sole_item_number`.** Building T-5.2 turned up two gaps in rule A. A leading "und" counted as a connector to a second number, so "Und noch die 24." - the normal way to add the next item - was a question back instead of item 24. And "würde", "hallo" and "dazu" were not filler words, so "Hallo, ich würde gern die 13" went to the name search. Fix per docs/08 §7: 14 lines added to `evals/cases/nummern.jsonl` first (9 red), then the rule: an "und" with no number before it is a continuation; between two numbers it still makes the sentence ambiguous ("die 23 und die 24"), and a leading "oder" stays a question. Review round on PR #147: the leading "und" only counts when it really opens the sentence - after a quantity ("zweimal und die 24") or a marker ("Nummer und 24"), or before a tens word ("und zwanzig", the cut-off half of "drei und zwanzig") it stays a question; the new filler words (plus "würd", "Guten Tag/Abend", "bestellen") are not skipped between marker and number and now also stand in `normalize.FILLER`, so the name search gets "pho" instead of "würde pho". Number eval 117/117. With the scripted model `abholung_0026` (Positionen nacheinander) and `abholung_0044` (Gericht im ersten Satz mit würde) turn green, no other case changes, and their `pending` field is removed: the suite is now 101/107 with six known gaps, hard metrics 0, false escalation 4.5 %. `abholung_0023` ("Zweimal die 23, ach nein, doch drei") stays pending: that is the quantity correction, not number recognition.

**Eval suite v1 (T-5.2, 26.09.2026): 107 cases, every mandatory line of docs/08 §6 covered.** Pickup (by number, name, alias, spoken numbers, quantities, options, ambiguous and sold-out dishes, unknown dishes, abort, read-back, closing time), reservations (times, dates, corrections at read-back, capacity, closed days), escalation (complaint, human, cancellation, noise) and allergens. A run takes about 12 s. With the rule-based stand-in model: 99/107 green, hard metrics 0, false escalation 4.5 %. Eight cases are **known gaps** (`pending` with the task that closes them: T-2.4 real model, T-6.5 delivery); CI demands every other case green and every gap red, so a gap that heals is noticed. The suite found four defects: two in the stand-in (a "yes" to the only offered dish was searched as a dish and asked again forever; a card number in the first sentence was stripped as a phone number), both fixed here; and one in the hot path, `check_slot` offering the previous day's times as alternatives (on closed Monday it offered Sunday 21:30 as "halb zehn"), fixed in its own bug PR; and the number rule for "und die 24" and "ich würde die 13", fixed in PR #147 (since then 101/107, six known gaps).

**"Gericht aus" on the tablet (T-4.8, 26.09.2026): the team takes a dish off, the agent stops offering it.** A button in the header (with "2 heute aus" next to it) opens a box above the columns with a search field and big switches. One tap marks a dish sold out until the end of the business day (05:00 local time), so it is back by itself the next morning; a second tap ("Wieder da") undoes it. Every tap is locked on the row and written to `audit_log`; other tablets follow over a new event `dishes`. `search_menu` then says "Frühlingsrollen ist heute leider aus. Stattdessen hätte ich Nummer 24 Sommerrollen." - up to two available dishes of the same category, never anything off the menu (D8). No migration: `sold_out_until` exists since 002. Clicked through in the browser against the eval menu.

**Kitchen ticket over a print bridge (T-4.6, variant B, 25.09.2026): a confirmed order reaches the printer, and the tablet knows whether it did.** The server sits in a data centre and cannot reach the receipt printer in the restaurant, and neither can n8n without opening the restaurant's router. So a small print bridge (`printbridge/`, Python standard library only) runs on a machine in the restaurant and fetches tickets over HTTPS (`POST /v1/kitchen/claim`, own token), prints them as ESC/POS on the Epson (network port 9100 or the Windows print queue for USB) and reports back (`/ack`). `handover_state` finally moves: `sent` when the newest revision is printed, `failed` at once on a print error or when no ticket was fetched for 60 s (watchdog in the dispatcher process, alarm, `order.handover_failed` to n8n), and back to `sent` when it prints after all. An outdated revision is never delivered after a newer one; a print log in the bridge stops a second slip when an acknowledgement got lost. Until today nothing ever set `sent` or `failed`, so no card could turn red. Tested against simulated printers (TCP with status query, Windows queue with stuck job); the first real print on the Epson TM-T20II needs Maxi on site. The dispatcher no longer sends `order.confirmed` to n8n. No migration.

**Eval runner (T-5.1, 25.09.2026): hard rule 4 has numbers now.** `make eval` plays every case from `evals/cases/` on its own throwaway database through the same conversation core as the text phone and judges the database, not the model's words. An observer between core and model counts the three hard metrics: a `menu_item_id` no search returned (guessed), a `confirm` without a preceding yes, and a missed escalation. Exit 1 on a crashed case, any hard violation, or a case that was green in the last passing run with the same model and tags and is red now (compared per case, not by accuracy). Reports go to `evals/reports/`; the whole suite also runs in CI. Today it measures the rule-based stand-in model; T-2.4 plugs in a real one and fills tokens and cost. 4/4 cases green, hard metrics 0. `/eval` and step 2 of `/bug` work from now on.

**Tablet column "Neue Bestellungen" (T-4.7, 25.09.2026): outside `primary` an order now reaches the kitchen.** Until today an order confirmed in `overflow`, `shadow` or `paused` waited for an approval nobody could give. The first column of the operations view lists every order the team still has to act on: confirmed today and not yet ticked off, every order still waiting for release (past 05:00 too), and every red card (kitchen not reached), even after "Passt" and past 05:00. "Passt" sets `approved`; outside `primary` it is the release and sends the ticket, in `primary` the kitchen already has it. A red card offers "Nochmal senden" instead. "Korrigieren" opens its own box above the columns (the column keeps updating and ringing): quantity, swap a dish by card number (quantity and note stay), add a dish, pick options as big buttons, save with one tap on the reason. Prices and totals come from the database on every tap. A note such as an allergy never disappears silently. Every correction is an `order.corrected` row in `audit_log` without personal data, raw material for the accuracy KPI. A ticket carries `revision`; a newer state overwrites a ticket nobody has tried to send, so an old state cannot overtake a correction (contract for T-4.6 in docs/04). Before building, the design went through a three-angle review (domain safety, concurrency, tablet UX) with adversarial verification; all 12 confirmed findings are built in. No migration: open PR #139 holds 003.

**Call log without recording (24.09.2026): real calls before the legal check.** Maxi asked to record team calls from tomorrow as learning data. Not possible yet: recording needs consent of customer and team (§201 StGB) and the full legal check (`docs/09_OPERATIONS_LEGAL.md`, all points open, T-7.x blocked); router, phone provider and handsets are not captured either (C1). Instead the team writes one line per call on a paper sheet (`docs/vorlagen/anrufprotokoll_druck.html`): time, intent, outcome, the customer's words verbatim, no names, no numbers. `python -m scripts.call_log imports/anrufprotokoll.csv` turns the typed CSV into the C1 baseline (volume, intent mix, outcomes, peak hours) and with `--cases` into eval case skeletons without any real customer sentence (`source: handcrafted`, empty transcript; the team re-enacts the sentences in its own words before a case moves to `evals/cases/`, DSFA M15). The script rejects the whole file if a free-text field looks like a phone number, e-mail, street address or a person's allergy (DSFA M8), and a semicolon inside text instead of shifting columns silently. Format and rules: `docs/17_ANRUFPROTOKOLL.md`.

CI enhancement (17.09.2026): The `docker-smoke` job builds the API image without local CA certificate and starts it without bind mount. It checks `/health`, rejection of missing/incorrect tokens, and an authenticated ping. This automatically catches earlier Dockerfile errors with certificate and package path. The test checks container startup, not DB integration; that stays in the pytest suite. Local Docker execution was not available in this session; execution via GitHub Actions.

The plan is set (`docs/00_PCF.md`, v1.0, released). The repo is set up and contains a **runnable minimal skeleton**: FastAPI with `/health`, token auth, response envelope, JSON logging, DB session, and the ten stage-1 tables per Alembic migration 001 with an idempotent seed with test configuration; hot-path tools (`get_service_status`, `check_slot`, `create_reservation`, `confirm`, `transfer_to_team`) respond in roughly 8–15 ms (p95); 295 tests passing. Reservation is complete: `create_reservation` creates the draft with `readback`, `confirm` makes it valid, writes `audit_log` and places the `reservation.confirmed` event in the outbox. The dispatcher (`api/events/`) empties the outbox: its own process, one POST per event to n8n with event id as idempotency key, backoff 5 s / 30 s / 2 min / 10 min, then `failed` with alarm in log. Callbacks are also done: `create_callback` creates the task for the team, logs it, and reports it via outbox. `transfer_to_team` hands escalations to the team: always the extension from `service_config.team_phone`, never a main number, and runs at most once per call (state on `calls.transfer_reason`). The call log is done: `POST /v1/calls/start`/`/end` open and close the `calls` row the rest of Stage 1 depends on, and every `/v1/tools/*` call now lands in `calls.tool_calls` with duration and result, written generically by `core/tool_log.py` so no individual tool needed to change. `prompts/system_v1.md` and `tools_v1.md` are also done, scoped to the six tools that actually exist today.

`agent/` now exists (T-2.1, 17.09.2026): `prompt.py` loads `prompts/system_vN.md` and can append a menu index once one exists; `state.py` is the compact per-call state from docs/05 §5 (`stage`, `intent`, `slots`, `open`) instead of a growing transcript; `dispatch.py` is the direct, non-HTTP equivalent of `api/tools/*.py` — validates arguments, calls `domain/` straight (no HTTP detour, per docs/11 §agent), auto-derives `idempotency_key` for `create_reservation`/`confirm` so the model never has to (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 1), and writes every call into `calls.tool_calls` itself since the HTTP middleware never runs on this path; `loop.py` drives customer-turn → model → tool-calls → reply, aborting cleanly on `max_call_seconds` or a runaway tool loop; `llm.py` defines the swappable `LLMClient` interface plus a `FakeLLM` test double — a real model with token counting is T-2.4. **Codex review PR #101 (P1×3, P2×2) fixed same-day:** `LLMTurn` gained an optional `state_patch` so conversational details the model extracts (name, date, party size, ...) survive into the next compact state instead of vanishing the moment the turn ends; `state.to_prompt_json()` now exposes `reservation_id` once a draft exists, so a stateless model can still supply `entity_id` to `confirm` on the customer's "yes"; the loop rechecks `max_call_seconds` before every tool hop, not only once at turn start; a tool's `say` is now forwarded to the model instead of dropped, so it isn't left to invent wording for cases the code already has a prescribed sentence for; and — the one that mattered most — hitting `max_call_seconds` or the tool-hop limit no longer just *announces* a handoff to the team and hangs up: it now actually calls `transfer_to_team`, falling back to `create_callback` when a phone number is known and the team is unavailable, and only falling back further to the plain apology when neither is possible (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 5).

**T-2.2 done (17.09.2026):** `agent/ladder.py` is the understanding ladder as a small state machine (docs/05 §2) — per-field failure counts (`UnderstandingLadder`), one level-up after two failed attempts at the same information, and `should_end_call()` once three level-ups pass without success; stages 4 (keypad) and 5 (SMS) are context-specific tools per docs/05 §2, not mandatory rungs, so Stage 1's reservation fields (date, party size, name, phone) only ever climb through 1–3 before the ladder gives up on its own. `agent/escalation.py` is the immediate-trigger check from docs/05 §4 (complaint, human-requested, cancellation) — plain keyword matching on the raw customer text, run in `loop.py` before the model is ever called, so an obvious case costs no tokens. Both are wired into `loop.py`: `LLMTurn` gained `understanding_failure` (the field name that failed) so the model only has to report failures, never count them; a ladder hint (`{"field": level}`) rides along in the compact prompt state once a field is elevated; escalation runs first in every `run_turn()`, ahead of even the `max_call_seconds` check; and every loop-internal handoff (timeout, hop limit, ladder exhaustion, or a genuine escalation trigger) now goes through the same `_handoff()` from the T-2.1 Codex fix, just with the real reason threaded through instead of a hardcoded placeholder. **Codex review PR #102 (P2×3) fixed same-day:** a repeated `understanding_failure` for the same field within one `run_turn()` call (e.g. reported once on a tool-call hop and again on the final reply) now counts once, not once per hop, so the ladder can't climb faster than the customer actually failed; `escalation.py`'s cancellation trigger now matches the noun "Storno" itself (`"storn"`), not only the verb forms; and `_handoff()` now threads the triggering customer text into the `create_callback` summary, since a pre-model escalation never calls the LLM and would otherwise leave the team a fixed phrase with no record of what the customer actually wanted. Still open at that point: the GUI.

**T-2.3 done (17.09.2026): the through-cut without a phone runs.** `sim/` is the text phone: `python -m sim.cli` holds a call in the terminal, `python -m sim.replay <case>.json` replays a transcript in the format from docs/08 §1, and both write to the real database — a confirmed reservation is in `reservations` afterwards, without any voice platform. `sim/session.py` holds what both entry points share: `start_call` opens the `calls` row, the compact state runs through `agent/loop.py`, every tool call is shown with the duration that `calls.tool_calls` recorded, and `end_call` derives the outcome from the conversation state (`confirmed` to `completed`, `transferred`, `callback`, otherwise `abandoned`) instead of from what the model claims. `sim/noise.py` garbles input on purpose — letter swaps, cut-off and swallowed words — with a seed, so a failure of the understanding ladder is reproducible instead of anecdotal. Because a real model only arrives with T-2.4, `sim/scripted_llm.py` stands in for it: rule-based, scoped to the reservation flow of `prompts/system_v1.md` (status, collect, `check_slot`, `create_reservation`, read back, confirm on a clear yes). It recognizes party size, day and time, name and phone number; what it does not recognize it reports as a failed attempt to the ladder rather than guessing (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 2), and a failed tool without a prescribed sentence makes it really hand over instead of announcing it (rule 5). Measured on the first case (`evals/cases/reservierung_0001_tisch_fuer_vier.json`): four customer turns, tool calls between 7 and 15 ms, reservation `confirmed` in the database. **Codex review PR #104 (P1x4, P2x2) fixed same-day:** a yes is now matched at word boundaries, so "Im Januar muss ich erst nachsehen" no longer counts as the explicit yes that turns a draft into a booking (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 3); an out-of-scope request (menu, pickup, delivery, allergy) is checked before the status call and remembered until the callback exists, because the follow-up turn usually carries nothing but the phone number and would otherwise drop the caller back into the reservation questions; a reply naming only a new time ("dann halb acht" to an offered alternative) keeps the day already given instead of rebooking the evening onto today; an impossible spoken date ("am 31.02.") counts as not understood rather than being silently replaced by today, and no longer escapes the conversation loop as a ValueError; and `finish()` closes the call at the current time instead of the start time, so a conversation from the terminal no longer records zero seconds (`--now` still pins the clock for a reproducible replay); and a correction during the read-back ("Nein, wir sind fuenf Personen") now builds a new draft with a new read-back instead of leaving the old one in place, where a later yes would have confirmed the four-person draft the customer had just corrected. The corrected-away draft stays in the database as a draft. Since #250 (12.10.2026) it holds seats against other callers only while its call can still confirm it, and since #207 (14.10.2026) it no longer counts against its own call. 46 new tests, 341 in total.

**T-0.8 done (17.09.2026):** `api/domain/menu/numberwords.py` turns spoken German numbers into integers, as a pure function without database or HTTP. The speech recognition delivers the same number in four shapes - as digits ("23"), as one word ("dreiundzwanzig"), written apart ("drei und zwanzig") or spoken digit by digit ("vierzig sieben") - and all four now yield the same value. Three entry points, because they differ at the phone: `parse_cardinal` (the whole text is the number), `find_item_number` (the number sits inside a sentence; an explicit "Nummer ..." beats everything, and two numbers without a marker give `None` instead of the first one) and `find_quantity` (only with a marker: zweimal, 2x, drei Portionen - a bare number is a dish, not an amount). No match gives `None`, never a guess (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 2); what follows from that - a question back, a rung of the understanding ladder, a handover - the caller decides. 375 tests: a round trip over 0 to 199 and every seventh value up to 999 against a separately written speller, plus the cases that actually go wrong on the phone. `sim/scripted_llm.py` now uses this module instead of its own provisional table, so "einundzwanzig Personen" works where twelve used to be the ceiling. Measured at 3 to 5 microseconds per call. 716 tests in total. **Codex review PR #105 (P1x2, P2x1) fixed in a follow-up, since the review arrived after the merge:** a number no longer grows across a punctuation mark or across the article of an amount, so "Nummer 20, eine Portion" is item 20 with one portion and no longer item 21 with amount 21 - the rule for digit-by-digit speech ("vierzig sieben") reached straight through the comma; an article that *begins* a number now counts, so "die ein und zwanzig" is 21 instead of 20, where the "ein" was discarded as an article before anyone checked whether it opens a number; and a number attached to an amount marker is no longer counted as a second dish number, so "2 x die 23" resolves instead of asking the guest back for nothing. Scanning now carries the position of each number (`_Span`), which is what makes the last one decidable at all. 8 new tests, 724 in total.

**T-0.7 done (17.09.2026):** the seven slash commands in `.claude/commands/` were played through once and the CI steps run green. `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` and `pytest` against a real Postgres: the whole suite green, lint clean - the same three steps the `test` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs. Two defects came out of the play-through and are fixed: `/gate` pointed at a section "Gate Workflow" that `docs/15_README_STRATEGY.md` does not have (it is called "Ablauf bei einem Gate"), and CI only ran on pushes to `main` and `task/**`, so a push to the `dev/**` branch a web session develops on triggered nothing until the pull request existed. `api/tests/test_commands.py` keeps the commands honest from here on: every command from CLAUDE.md §6 exists, every file and directory a command names exists (except `evals/reports/`, which the runner creates and `.gitignore` excludes), every `make` target it calls exists in the Makefile, and no command carries an emoji (CLAUDE.md §8). Two commands stay blocked and that is expected: `/eval` and step 2 of `/bug` need `evals/runner.py`, which arrives with T-5.1; `make eval` fails with ModuleNotFoundError until then, now noted in the README under Known Issues. `/done` step 2 uses `make lint`/`make test`, which need a Docker daemon - this session had none, so the identical commands ran natively against local Postgres instead.

**T-4.4 done (22.09.2026): `get_item_details`.** `POST /v1/tools/get_item_details` answers questions about a dish the caller already picked: description, option groups and allergens for a `menu_item_id` that `search_menu` delivered. Allergens come only from `item_allergens`; without a maintained row the answer is `known: false`, never a guess and never "free of it" (CLAUDE.md §9).
