The sentence from the code that the team will call back is tied to the mandatory `allergen_question` flag in the request: the same tool also answers questions about options, and those must not end in a callback promise. Maintained codes are read in the order of the LMIV list so every call hears the same order, and `confirmed_at` is the most recent proof, as the local date of the tenant (CLAUDE.md §8). Options and the state "sold out today" now come from one shared building block (`domain/menu/items.py`) that `search_menu` uses as well, so both tools cannot drift apart. p95 around 16 ms with a local database.

**T-4.3 done (18.09.2026): `search_menu`.** `POST /v1/tools/search_menu` resolves what the caller said to a `menu_item_id` in the order of docs/04: number, then exact alias, then trigram over name and aliases. One result above the high threshold is `fuzzy_single`; otherwise everything above the low threshold is `ambiguous` with at most three suggestions and a question in `say` ("Meinen Sie Nummer 47, Ente knusprig oder Nummer 48, Ente süß-sauer?"); nothing is `not_found`. Thresholds come from `MENU_FUZZY_THRESHOLD_HIGH`/`LOW` (0.72 / 0.45). Only active dishes are found; a sold-out dish comes back with `sold_out: true` and "… ist heute leider aus." Options are grouped, default first. `normalize_query` in `domain/menu/normalize.py` strips filler, number and quantity words; `numberwords.has_item_number_marker` is new. **Rule A (Maxi, 21.09.2026), after fifteen rounds of Codex findings on number parsing:** search_menu only takes a card number directly when the whole sentence is exactly that one number. Besides the number, a marker („Nummer“/„Nr.“), filler words, hesitations, punctuation and one quantity („zweimal die 23“, „zwei Nummer 23“) are allowed - nothing else. Anything mixed in - a second number, a name, an „oder“ - is `ambiguous` with one question: ‚Welche Nummer meinen Sie? Bitte sagen Sie mir nur die eine Nummer.‘ Without a marker, a number next to a name stays a quantity („zwei Frühlingsrollen“) and the name search decides. One rule instead of a growing list of special cases: every sentence shape that is not unambiguously a number becomes a question back rather than a silent wrong dish (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 2). `numberwords.sole_item_number` carries the rule; `find_item_number_ref`/`find_marked_item_numbers` remain for the ladder. **Eval set (21.09.2026):** eight rounds of Codex findings followed, each a genuine wrong-dish risk, and two of them broke a form that had been right before - every single fix was correct on its own. The rules for marker, card suffix, quantity and connector word interlock, and single test functions do not show that. `evals/cases/nummern.jsonl` now holds all 64 known sentences with the outcome each must produce (`"23"` the card number, `"!23h"` spoken but not a valid card form, `"?"` ask back, `"name"` leave it to the name search); `evals/number_eval.py` runs them without database, HTTP or model in milliseconds (`make eval-nummern`), and `api/tests/test_evals_nummern.py` drives the same file in CI so a case is maintained in exactly one place. Measured against the commits before the last fixes, the set fails with 3 to 11 deviations - it is a real net, not a snapshot of today. Verification: pure tests (normalize, marker, numberwords) run locally, ruff clean; the DB and HTTP tests including p95 with 200 dishes run in CI.

**T-4.2 done as code (18.09.2026): the menu import.** `python -m scripts.import_menu imports/ --dry-run` reads the four CSVs per docs/14 and reports; without `--dry-run` it loads. `domain/menu/importer.py` splits into `parse` (reads and checks without a database, every rule from docs/14 plus the ones the schema needs: prices only as digits and comma, never via float; required option group with exactly one default; LMIV letters only, lowercase accepted and upper-cased; allergens without `confirmed_by` rejected) and `apply` (one transaction, file is the truth for every dish it lists). Price changes on existing dishes stay in the report until `--apply-price-changes`. Aliases from calls or by hand survive a re-import; only aliases from an earlier import are removed. Dishes in the database but not in the file stay untouched and are listed. A second run with the same files changes nothing and writes no audit entry. `domain/menu/normalize.py` holds the alias normalization that `search_menu` (T-4.3) will share. Test data is invented and lives in the tests; the real CSVs come from the chat (C1) into the git-ignored `imports/`. Verification: CI on PR #115 green, 916 passed. **Codex review PR #115 (P2x2) fixed same-day:** a new `confirmed_by` for the same allergen codes, or codes kept while others change, now re-stamps every row of that dish with the file's confirmer and time and shows up in the report (before, the database kept the old confirmer); a dry run with `--apply-price-changes` reports "würde übernommen" instead of "übernommen".

**T-4.1 done (18.09.2026): migration 002, the schema for stage 2.** Six tables per docs/03: `menu_items` (card number as text, unique per tenant, `sold_out_until` for "Gericht aus"), `item_options`, `item_allergens` (no row = no information; `confirmed_by`/`confirmed_at` mandatory), `item_aliases` (source `manual`/`call`/`import`, the same alias may hang on two dishes), `orders` and `order_items` (`menu_item_id` mandatory, unit price frozen, options as JSONB). `pg_trgm` is enabled with GIN indexes on `name` and `alias` for `search_menu` (T-4.3). The database checks what the code must never get wrong: amounts in cents >= 0, `total = items + fee`, quantity > 0, known status and handover values, a dish that was ordered cannot be deleted. `test_migration_001` is now pinned to revision 001; the model-versus-schema diff runs on head in `test_migration_002`. Verification: offline SQL of up and down checked, ruff clean; the DB tests run in CI. **Review finding on PR #114 fixed same-day:** `item_allergens.allergen_code` now only accepts the 14 LMIV letters (`A`-`H`, `L`-`P`, `R`); the model comment had claimed "A bis N", which would have rejected sulphites (O), lupins (P) and molluscs (R), and without a check an import typo would have been stored as a confirmed allergen. **Codex review PR #114 (P2x2) fixed same-day:** `order_items` carries `tenant_id` with composite foreign keys to `orders (id, tenant_id)` and `menu_items (id, tenant_id)`, so an order can never hold another tenant's dish; the model-versus-schema and downgrade tests migrate the scratch database themselves instead of relying on test order.

**Design pass on the operations view (18.09.2026, after Maxi's feedback on T-3.4):** checked in the browser at tablet width (1180 px), light and dark. Fixed: summary and notes were 13 px and badges 12 px (classes from the desktop mockup) - now 18 px as docs/06 §1 rule 3 demands; "Erledigt" in dark mode was white on light green (about 2:1 contrast) - `button.primary` now uses the surface colour as text; callback cards lead with a large time like the Heute cards, the reason badge next to it; complaints carry a wide red left edge instead of a thin frame; "Anrufen" and "Erledigt" split the card width evenly; the column headings Heute and Rückrufe show a count, which moves with the fragment. Phone numbers are still shown in E.164 without grouping - a correct grouping needs the area code length, left open.

**T-3.4 done (18.09.2026): the callbacks column is operable, the operations view is complete for stage 1.** Open callbacks appear as cards: time taken, reason in words (Beschwerde, Nicht verstanden, Will Mitarbeiter sprechen, Anderes Anliegen), phone, the one-sentence summary, **Anrufen** as a `tel:` link and **Erledigt** without confirmation (docs/06 §1 rule 6). Complaints always stand on top with a red frame, after them the oldest first. `domain/callbacks/board.py` holds the reads and `mark_done` (row lock, `done_by`/`done_at`, `audit_log` `callback.done`; a second tap or a second tablet writes nothing). The event stream carries a third signal `callbacks`, fingerprinted over the set of open ids rather than `updated_at` - the lesson from Codex on PR #111. A new card plays a tone generated in the browser (WebAudio, no audio file, works offline), complaints their own. Verification: CI on PR #112 green, 809 passed. **Codex review PR #112 (P1) fixed same-day:** tablet Safari unlocks Web Audio only inside a user gesture, so the audio context is now created in the first tap (`touchstart`/`click`) with a silent buffer, not when the first callback arrives - before, the tablet stayed silent.

**T-3.2 done (18.09.2026): the header is operable.** Four buttons under the status row: **KI pausieren** is the emergency stop (one tap, no confirmation, `call_mode` to `paused`, header turns grey), **KI einschalten** asks once (`hx-confirm`) and restores the mode from before the pause, **Lieferung aus/an** and **Wartezeit +15 / +30**. The writes live in `domain/status/config.py` as docs/11 names it: every switch locks the `service_config` row (`FOR UPDATE`) so two tablets tapping +15 at once give +30, and writes `audit_log` with actor `gui:tablet` and the value before and after. The router in `gui/` only picks the tenant and renders `fragments/kopfzeile.html`. The event stream now carries a second signal `header` (fingerprint: `service_config.updated_at`), so a switch on one tablet - or a change straight in the database - shows on every tablet within two seconds, without reloading the column Heute. A failed tap still swaps in the header with the red bar (docs/06 §5) instead of looking like nothing happened; an unreachable server shows the yellow bar. Verification: ruff clean, SSE tests (12) run locally; the DB-backed tests (domain and router, 27 new) need Postgres, which this session did not have - CI on PR #111 ran them: 782 passed, lint clean, docker-smoke green. **Codex review PR #111 (P2x2) fixed same-day:** the header fingerprint now carries the shown values (mode, delivery, both wait times) besides `updated_at`, because `updated_at` is set only by the ORM and a raw `UPDATE` in the database left the tablets stale; and `app.js` swaps an error response into the page only when it is HTML - `main.py` answers `HTTPException` and unhandled errors with the JSON envelope, which would otherwise have replaced the buttons or the list. Those now keep the view and show the red bar with "Nochmal versuchen" (`#stoerung` in `base.html`).

**T-3.1 and T-3.3 done (17.09.2026): the milestone "Durchstich ohne Telefon" is closed.** A reservation talked into the terminal is on the tablet a moment later, without a phone line. `api/gui/` is the operations view: `router.py` serves the page under `/gui/` and the column "Heute" as an HTMX fragment, `templates/` holds `base.html`, `betrieb/index.html` and `fragments/heute.html`, `static/app.css` carries the variables and classes of the approved mockup (`gui/mockups/admin-desktop.html`), and HTMX lies next to it locally - nothing is fetched from a CDN, the GUI works without internet (docs/06 §2). The header shows mode, delivery and wait times from `service_config`, never from the code; its buttons and live refresh are T-3.2 and are deliberately absent. `domain/reservations/today.py` is the data behind the column: confirmed reservations of the business day, ordered by time - a draft is not a booking (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 3) and does not appear on the tablet. `gui/sse.py` is the event stream: it polls a cheap change token (count plus newest `updated_at`) every two seconds and sends a signal, the page then fetches the fragment itself. Polling the database rather than an in-process channel on purpose - `sim/`, the dispatcher and later the telephony write from their own processes, and an in-process channel would never see their bookings. A dropped database yields a `problem` event and the yellow bar from docs/06 §5 instead of a dead stream. **Decision D6 is settled: Jinja2 and HTMX, no React** (rationale in docs/06 §2). Measured end to end: `sim/replay` confirms a reservation in one process, the event stream in the API process reports the change within about two seconds and the reloaded fragment shows guest, time and party size. **Codex review PR #108 (P1x1, P2x2) fixed same-day:** `/gui/*` now sits behind basic auth in `deploy/Caddyfile` with user and hash from the environment - the checked-in production stack proxied the view straight through before, so guest names, phone numbers and notes were open on the internet; `/gui/events` resolves the tenant in its own short-lived session instead of the request-scoped one, because a stream open for hours would have pinned a pooled connection for just as long and a handful of tablets could have starved the hot path; and after a database outage the stream now sends a signal on the first successful poll even when nothing changed, so the yellow bar disappears instead of hanging until the next booking. 23 new tests, 747 in total.

Before the first real call three things are missing: the current state of operations (C1, Maxi), the own voice layer (Block 1c, decided 07.10.2026 instead of a voice platform) and the duties of the legal check, whose questions were answered on 20.10.2026 (`docs/09_OPERATIONS_LEGAL.md` §1).

**Verified (16.09.2026):** `make up` builds the API image and starts Postgres and API, `/health` responds `{"status":"ok"}`, `make lint` and `make test` run in the container (T-0.1 done). External code review (Codex) delivered four findings on Docker setup, all fixed: CA certificate optional in build, package path `/app/api` preserved, `scripts/` and `evals/` mounted in container, README quickstart reduced to what runs today.

Full roadmap from here to the target state: section "Roadmap" below. Full details per stage: `docs/00_PCF.md` §5.

| Stage | Goal | Gate | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| P Plan | Plan released | – | passed 11.09.2026 |

**We are here:** Stage 0, block "Stufe 2: Abholung" (`docs/07_WORKPACKAGES.md` Block 3) - pickup runs over HTTP from `search_menu` to the pickup code (T-4.1 to T-4.5 and `confirm` for orders done, 23.09.2026). Since 24.09.2026 the agent core uses the menu tools too, and a pickup order runs end to end in the text phone (caller ID, repeat-back, pickup code). Since 25.09.2026 the tablet releases, corrects and resends orders (T-4.7). Since 25.09.2026 the kitchen ticket is printed by a print bridge in the restaurant (T-4.6 variant B). Since 26.09.2026 wishes to a dish (T-4.10) and "Gericht aus" on the tablet (T-4.8) are done. Next: the first real print on site, see "What's next". The milestone "Durchstich ohne Telefon" for reservations is closed. **Where we're going:** Stage 6, ongoing operation with AI as primary intake and team as fallback.

## What's Next

### In Claude Code (can start immediately, without vendor)
1. **First input slip on paper: done 06.10.2026** (T-4.6, PR #231). The test slip prints on the main receipt printer at the register (`EPSON TM-T20II Receipt`, port ESDPRT001, RAW) and reads correctly: umlauts, 48 characters, partial cut. Next for the print bridge: the first slip of a real order through the server, see "First real print through the server" under Open Points; `pos_code` on the slip after T-4.11 (#149)
2. **T-1.14 done 27.09.2026** (docs/04 §check_slot): alternatives only within the same service, earliest and latest start from the DB (D12); nothing left to build
3. **First real import from the register - done 12.10.2026 in the workbench database (stack of `~/Maex-Voice-Agent`, invented customers only): 173 dishes, 797 options, no aliases; the second dry run reported no change.** Allergens since 13.10.2026: the five dishes the register gives allergens for carry them, confirmed by Maxi (`confirmed_by` Maxi); every other dish says "no information". Left: the production import, with the same steps on the server. The steps, in this order, on the host (the host Python has no project packages, so every `alembic` and `python` line below runs in the image through this shell function, run from the repo root: `R() { docker run --rm --network maex-voice-agent_default --env-file .env -v "$PWD":/work -w /work maex-voice-agent-api:latest "$@"; }`, for example `R alembic -c db/alembic.ini upgrade head` (workbench only, see step 2 for the server) and `R python -m scripts.import_menu imports --dry-run --apply-price-changes --deactivate-missing`; on the server use that stack's network name; the backup of 12.10.2026 was unencrypted, the database held no customer data). `DATABASE_URL` decides which database the import writes to, and `--tenant-name` (or `TENANT_NAME`) names the tenant in it. Backup and restore must name **the same host, port, database and user as `DATABASE_URL`**: every worktree has its own Compose stack, and on 04.10.2026 the one on port 5432 belonged to another worktree, so `docker compose exec db` from this checkout would have dumped a different database than the import changes (Codex PR #169):
   1. Backup first, before anything changes the database (CLAUDE.md §8): `bash scripts/backup.sh --label before_menu_import`, with the same `DATABASE_URL` as the import and `BACKUP_PASSPHRASE_FILE` set (T-9.3, docs/13 §4). The script reads the dump back before it reports it as written; `bash scripts/restore.sh <file> --check` rehearses the restore without touching the database
   2. Target database at migration 005 (`pos_code` is migration 004; the local stack on port 5432 was at 003 on 04.10.2026): `alembic -c db/alembic.ini upgrade head` on the workbench with its single database user. On the server, where the application has a role of its own that cannot change the schema (docs/13 §3), the same step is `COMPOSE_FILE=docker-compose.yml:deploy/docker-compose.prod.yml bash scripts/migrate.sh`, which runs Alembic as the owner
   3. Fresh copies in `imports/kasse/`, then `python -m scripts.kasse_to_csv imports/kasse --out imports --allergens-confirmed-by Maxi` (Maxi confirmed the five entries on 13.10.2026; without the switch the converter writes empty rows and the import deletes all five). Then set the three corrected rows in `imports/item_allergens.csv` again (`13;D,N;Maxi`, `35a;A,C,F;Maxi`, `15c;B,G,O;Maxi`) before step 4
   4. `python -m scripts.import_menu imports --dry-run --apply-price-changes --deactivate-missing`, read the report
   5. The same line without `--dry-run`
   6. The line from step 4 again, with `--dry-run`: the report must say "Keine Änderung"

   Restore: `bash scripts/restore.sh <file> --replace <database>` with `api` and `dispatcher` stopped; the state from before the restore stays as `<database>_before_restore_<time>`. The dump is the state before step 2: afterwards the schema is back at the old migration, so step 2 comes again before the next import. Backup, check and restore were rehearsed on 04.10.2026 on a throwaway database (docs/13 §4). Open next to it, none of them blocking: the register points under C1 "Register data" and aliases from the chat (`item_aliases.csv`, today no dish has one).   - **Phone line (26.09.2026):** Fritz!Box 6591 Cable on a cable line, three numbers; callers use one number only. Business cable tariff with **4 voice channels** and up to 10 numbers (provider's service description, table "Telefonleitungen/Sprachkanäle", checked 26.09.2026). A forward done by the Fritz!Box takes two channels (in and out), and a transfer back to the team one more. Two forwarded calls fill all four channels and leave none for `transfer_to_team` (docs/02 §5, docs/05, rule 5), so the **safe limit is one AI call at a time** (2 + 1 for its transfer, one channel left for a ringing call); a second AI call would be possible only by giving up the transfer. The line is the bottleneck, not the AI platform or the handsets. Open for C2: does the provider offer forwarding in its network (no local channels), or does the main number move to the platform or a SIP provider
  - **Register data (26.09.2026):** converter built (T-4.11), mapping in `docs/14_MENU_IMPORT_FORMAT.md` §Quelle Kasse. No relay for iPhones and iPads and no web push of the browser maker; either would be a decision of its own (a third party then learns that a message exists; `docs/09_OPERATIONS_LEGAL.md`, E9), and `api/tests/test_push_service.py` fails if a relay is added without changing the test on purpose. Before: Android keeps its own connection to our server, nothing leaves it. iPhone and iPad get a message at once only if the server forwards a wake-up to the relay of the app's maker (message id and a hash of the topic address, not the text), or through web push of the browser maker. Neither is configured. Recommended: Android devices or the web page on the tablet; the relay only if iPhones are needed, and then as a decision of its own | Maxi | done; the devices subscribe once the push server is set up on the server (`n8n/README.md`) |

Grund: Allergie" (E14 stays). It goes on the dish when the guest ties the allergy to that dish, otherwise on the whole order; when that is unclear, on the whole order. Questions about a dish's allergens are answered only from confirmed database values (`get_item_details`, unchanged). When the guest asks beyond what the database holds (traces, cross-contact, "Ist das wirklich sicher?"), the call goes to a team member, or a callback when nobody answers; this must be checked in the tests with the real model. Outside the opening hours a handover by the agent becomes a callback card at once and nobody's telephone rings at night; the outage paths (paused, shadow, `voice` down, a failure) ring `TEAM_PHONE` at any hour as before (rule 5), and now leave a card when nobody answers; inside them the call is handed to the team and the dial result decides: a leg nobody answers at `TEAM_PHONE` becomes a callback card (docs/20 §5). The text phone and the evals keep the opening hours alone |

---

## Made Assumptions (subject to change)

- **The question for a human and the privacy pointer (T-11.5, 26.10.2026), Claude's readings of docs/09 L1 and L4:** (a) The address is read out exactly as stored, so it is written the way a caller should hear it; no code turns a URL into words. (b) Without an address the greeting leaves the sentence out instead of stopping calls: the test phase has no notice online, and the duty of L4 is checked before the first real call. (c) The greeting stays the short one plus the pointer; the longer wording of U1 is not taken. (d) Only the question order counts as the question ("Sind Sie ein Mensch?", "Spreche ich mit einem Menschen?"); "Ich will mit einem Menschen sprechen" stays the wish that goes to the team at once. (e) The offer of the team counts for the next sentence only. (f) The system prompt is unchanged: a variant the code does not catch goes to the model, which says in its first sentence that it is an AI
- **Deletion job, three readings of docs/03** (T-11.2, 26.10.2026): (a) a note counts as a caller's allergy only by the words "Allergi…", "Unverträglich…" or "Intoleran…" (the kitchen wording "Grund: Allergie" included); a hand-typed "Erdnüsse!!" without such a word stays until the guest details go after 90 days; (b) an outbox payload is cut to its ids instead of nulled, because the column is not nullable and the kitchen finds the slips of an order by id and revision; the ids carry no personal data; (c) decided by Maxi on 26.10.2026 (option A, after the ultrareview of PR #371): a callback never marked done loses phone and summary 90 days after its creation, and its card says so. Subject to change
- **Call log rows with the same content in one hour count as two calls** (T-11.4, `scripts/call_log.py` `_twins`): without the minute a row typed twice cannot be told from two calls with the same words. Counting both and naming the lines in the report (only rows with free text) was chosen over merging, which would drop a real call and its reviewed case in silence. A running number per call would split them but lines the log up with the router's call list again (L15). Assumption 25.10.2026, subject to change
- **Retention of the request log of data subjects (U2), Claude's proposal of 21.10.2026, awaiting Maxi (#344):** verification details stripped 3 months after the request is answered, the row without them kept until the end of the third calendar year after the year of the answer and then deleted, cleanup by the daily job of T-11.2, table `data_subject_requests` planned with T-11.8 with a `tenant_id`, lookups and cleanup scoped by it. Reasoning: the details serve only to answer and to defend the answer; the thin row proves the one-month deadline (Art. 12(3), Art. 5(2)) while a claim can arise (3-year limitation from the end of the year, § 195 and § 199(1) BGB). A legal choice; no code or migration written. Docs: `docs/03_DATA_MODEL.md` Löschkonzept, `docs/09_OPERATIONS_LEGAL.md` §1 U2.
- **Dialplan and access after the audit (T-10.13, 19.10.2026), Claude's readings of #252 findings 1 to 3:** (a) A transfer target is compared with `TEAM_PHONE` and `TRANSFER_TARGETS` exactly as written, no normalizing: a `service_config.team_phone` in another form than the one in `.env` rings `TEAM_PHONE`, never the number itself, which errs towards the team. (b) After an unanswered leg at a listed number only `TEAM_PHONE` is tried, once; the other listed numbers are not tried in turn. (c) `ACCESS_HOST` is looked up once when `asterisk` starts, as Asterisk itself looks up a name in `match`, as the servers of its `_sip._udp` SRV records, or its own A records when it has none; an access that changes its address needs a restart, and a name with several addresses gives each of them. (d) IPv4 only, as the SIP transport. (e) The VPN to the pilot's router (T-10.1 step 2) and a trunk (T-10.12) may deliver calls from other addresses than `ACCESS_HOST`'s; then those go into the ACL and `match` with that task.
- **The AI telephone (T-1.11 part 1, 14.10.2026), Maxi's decision and Claude's readings of docs/20 §2 and §5:** Maxi let T-1.11 start without T-10.2. Claude's choices: (a) the silence before "Sind Sie noch da?" is 8 s (`VOICE_SILENCE_SECONDS`), a guess until real calls; (b) `voice` has 10 s to answer a call, then the team; a call on our leg is cut after an hour whatever happens, above any call limit a tenant is likely to set; the team leg rings 30 s; (c) at most three messages of audio (about 6 s) unplayed at Asterisk, and a frame Asterisk does not take within 2 s closes the connection; (d) the media range is 10000 to 10039, two ports for each of 20 legs, published on the server only with the trunk; (e) the dialplan's question is answered once, a second answer is empty and rings the team; (f) the tenant of `voice` is the one named `TENANT_NAME`, two of that name stop the start; (g) the team leg carries the user of our registration, not the guest's number (open in docs/20 §12); (h) the drills run with an ear that hears nothing and a mouth of noise, so they test the telephone and the fallbacks, not speech.
- **Ear and mouth adapters (T-10.5, 14.10.2026), Maxi's decision and Claude's readings of docs/20 §6:** Maxi let T-10.5 start on the local pair of T-10.3 (Whisper turbo for orders, a silence detector at 700 ms or more, Piper `thorsten`) while T-10.3 stays open for the thresholds, the voice (D4) and the EU services. Claude's choices: (a) the end of an utterance after 22 windows of 32 ms (704 ms), the detector's thresholds (0.5 and 0.35) as measured, 288 ms of audio before and after the speech, speech under 256 ms reported as none, a partial every 0.5 s; (b) key terms are the dishes named in what the agent just said, then the order; nothing at the first mention of a dish, since no small window can be chosen before the guest names one; names as a guest says them, without the number of pieces; card numbers are not handed over, since only names were measured; (c) `audioop` converts the mouth's rate (in the standard library up to Python 3.12, which every image pins); (d) no settings yet: the adapters take their model folder, device and voice file as arguments, and T-1.11 builds them from `.env`.
- **Leftovers in a `main` folder (Maxi, 2026-10-14):** the Windows clone could not be pulled for two days and stood 192 commits behind, because two uncommitted edits lay in its `main` folder (a note on TypeScript as a future GUI option, written 12.10.2026, probably by a session that worked in that folder directly). Fetching worked the whole time, and both sync hooks warned at every session start; no session passed the warning on. A session on Windows then kept the files on a local branch `leftovers/main-folder-2026-10-14`, synced `main`, and brought the note into PR #288. Since 14.10.2026: nothing is edited on `main` and no session ends with uncommitted changes or a stash (`CLAUDE.md` §8, `AGENTS.md` for other agents), and `/start` reports uncommitted files in a `main` folder, stash entries and `leftovers/*` branches at every session start until they are gone. Claude's reading: the reminder lives in `/start` and not in a hook, because a hook cannot know what a leftover is worth; a hook that refuses edits on `main` would need an entry in the user settings and is not built.
- **Call session (T-10.4, 13.10.2026), Maxi's decisions and Claude's readings of docs/20 §4:** Maxi decided that step 4 (real voices) waits for the phone path and that T-10.4 starts on the result of T-10.1 step 1. Claude's choices, each a line in the table "as built" of docs/20 §4: (a) the silence timeout is an argument of the session and has no setting yet; the tests use 6 s, the value for the service `voice` is open and comes with T-1.11. (b) The two questions into a silent line are drafts ("Sind Sie noch da?", "Ich höre Sie leider nicht. Sind Sie noch dran?", docs/05 §6). (c) Speech without a transcript is a failure of the ear only the second time in a row; the table says it without a count, and a single cough would otherwise send a call to the team. (d) A finished one-word utterance stops a sentence that may be interrupted, beyond the table's two words of a partial. (e) Sentences are interruptible per turn, not per sentence: everything said in a turn that ends in a read-back, a transfer or the end of the call is protected. (f) The AI disclosure at the start cannot be talked over, so a "Hallo" spoken into it is dropped. (g) After the length of the audio plus 5 s without a report from the line, the call counts as failed and goes to the team; a sentence of the mouth has 10 s. Both values are constants of the session.
- **Reservation limits (#252, 13.10.2026), Claude's readings of Maxi's decision:** (a) "The same day" is the calendar day of the restaurant; a table after midnight in a service that began the evening before counts as the next day (the pilot closes at 22:00). (b) The horizon comes first and holds for every party size: a party of twelve for a day beyond it hears the limit and is not taken as a callback. (c) `team_callback` replaces only a table that is free by the rules: on a closed day and in a full service a large party hears what everyone hears and can name another time, so the team gets only wishes it can say yes to. (d) The callback uses the existing reason `out_of_scope` ("Anderes Anliegen" on the tablet) and starts its summary with "Reservierungswunsch, vom Team zu bestätigen:"; a reason of its own with its own label would need the CHECK on `callbacks.reason`, the tablet and the n8n workflow, proposal only. (e) The core creates the callback in the turn that learns of the table, also when the guest has not said a name yet: the card then holds party size, day and time, and the call ends there. Waiting for the name would bring back the call that ends without the team. The summary is written by the core from the arguments the tool took, the time in the restaurant's zone. (h) A caller whose number is withheld is not asked for one: the call is transferred where the team is reachable, and outside the opening hours the guest hears that nobody can be reached, as in every handover (open point from T-1.13 under "What's Next", item 11). A first version asked for the number and kept the wish across turns; every rule for when the waiting wish still stood either lost a wish or sent a stale one to the team (four review rounds on PR #280), so the waiting is gone. (f) Both sentences are Claude's wording, and the first promises no callback: that sentence comes from `create_callback` once the callback exists. (g) Zero is a setting for all three fields: no day ahead means today only, a party limit of zero sends every table to the team.
- **Lettered card numbers, scope of the rule (12.10.2026):** the open point proposed a question back for a number after "ist" and for a bare number that also exists with a prefix. Built is the first only (Claude's call, numbers in docs/20 §6 and §12): the second covers 1 of 88 lettered utterances on the picked ear and would ask on plain orders such as "zwölf bitte" wherever the menu has 12 and S12. Maxi says so if the question is wanted anyway.
- **Spoken amounts (12.10.2026, `spoken_euro`):** Maxi named "25 Euro 70" and "50 Cent". Two forms next to them are Claude's choice: single cents behind euros are said with their unit ("17 Euro 5 Cent", since a bare "5" is easily taken for "50" on the phone), and a lone one is written as a word ("ein Euro 50", "ein Cent"). Maxi changes either with one line.
- **Own voice layer, web research of 07.10.2026, nothing verified by us** (`docs/20_VOICE_LAYER.md`): Asterisk 22.6 or later streams call audio over WebSocket in both directions; the pilot's router accepts a software client as an IP telephone, delivers the caller number to it, counts two of its four connections per such call and supports handing a call from it to another telephone only partly; a VPN peer may register as an IP telephone (not confirmed by the router's vendor); a chain costs about 20 to 60 USD a month at 2,000 call minutes and answers in 1.0 to 1.8 s voice to voice; speech-to-speech models resolve fewer tasks than text models and are not faster; the effort is 1 to 2 weeks to a demo and 5 to 8 weeks to reliable. T-10.1 to T-10.3 replace these lines with measurements (docs/20 §11).
- **An order by phone is a pickup until delivery is built** (`agent/intent.py`, 08.10.2026): "ich möchte etwas bestellen" without a table word sets `intent` to `pickup` in the conversation state. A sentence that names a delivery sets nothing. With T-6.5 the bare order word needs its own reading, and delivery its own value.
- **Call volume** (Maxi's estimate, 07.10.2026, no call log yet): under 30 calls on weekdays, 30 to 60 on weekends; with two minutes a call (assumption) about 2,000 call minutes a month.
- **Workbench facts** (checked 07.10.2026 in a session in Ubuntu on Maxi's PC): `sudo` asks for a password, Docker runs without it, the distribution ships Asterisk 20.6, WSL runs behind address translation. The router at Maxi's home is a provider-supplied cable router without a registrar, so the router test of T-10.1 needs the pilot's router.

- **Model cost per call** (`docs/18_MODEL_SELECTION.md` §2): about 10 model turns per call, about 1,500 input and 100 output tokens per turn, 30 calls a day. Replaced by the logged values once T-2.4 part 2 writes `calls.cost_cents`, and by the call log count (C1)
- **Phone line behaviour (T-1.13), assumptions from 06.10.2026:** the greeting with the AI disclosure is said by the code, not the model (docs/05 §6, name from `tenants.name`, no recording notice because nothing is recorded); a goodbye is recognised in the agent's last sentence by a short word list ("Auf Wiederhören", "bis dann", "bis gleich" and the like), a goodbye it misses is said twice rather than not at all; keypad digits reach the agent as the caller's answer, `*` and `#` dropped; a call in `shadow` or `paused` that reaches the agent anyway is transferred without a word; live calls are held in the memory of one process, and an event for a call that process does not know goes to the team. The real adapter (T-1.11) has to keep a call's events on one process or this changes
- **Card numbers stay as built, no further polishing of exotic forms** (Maxi, 04.10.2026): most restaurants use digits or digits with a letter behind them, at most three or four characters; a letter in front (`S20`) is rare and exists here only because Sushi joined the menu later. Forms like `S2000` or `Z194` are not a real use case. The handling built in T-4.12 stays because removing it would cost more than it saves and it only ever answers `not_found` or asks back. Review findings about such forms are recorded, not fixed, unless they can pick a wrong dish.
- **D12 applies to reservations only** (T-1.14): pickup and delivery keep the full opening window. **Morning** means before the first opening of the day and closer to it than to the last closing before (so 01:00 after a night window is "closed", 09:00 is morning; after a closed day midnight counts as the last closing, so 01:00 is "closed" there too). **11:15 is bookable** when asked for directly (opening + 15), only the offered grid starts at 11:30. Assumptions from 27.09.2026
- **`+` in `GROESSE` means "extras allowed"** (T-4.11): the converter offers register extras only for such dishes. Only fewer offers if wrong, never more. Extras price from `ZPREIGRP3`, not `ZPREIGRP` - all active extras fit, `Extra_Ente` has no `ZPREIGRP`; to be checked once in the dry-run report against the register. Assumption from 26.09.2026
- **Spoken allergy sentence for wishes is a draft** (`domain/menu/search.py` `SAY_ALLERGY_NOTE`, T-4.10): "Ihren Hinweis zur Allergie gebe ich an die Küche weiter. Ob … frei davon ist, kann ich Ihnen nur sagen, wenn es bei uns hinterlegt ist." No promise that a dish is free of anything; checked with the legal check (docs/09) before go-live. The kitchen note itself uses the fixed wording of E14. Assumption from 24.09.2026. **Checked 20.10.2026 (legal check L13, L17):** the "no promise" wording holds; the sentence changes with T-11.1, where the agent asks for consent before it passes the note on
- Python 3.12, FastAPI, PostgreSQL 16, Alembic, pytest, ruff
- GUI as FastAPI + Jinja2 + HTMX + SSE, one container, no Node build
- **Future GUI option (12.10.2026, Maxi):** reassess TypeScript when concrete UI features need substantially more client-side state or interaction; criteria in `docs/06_GUI.md` section 2. D6 remains in force; no migration is approved and the backend stays Python.
- One location (<Pilotbetrieb>), but multi-tenant schema: each location-related table carries `tenant_id`
- German as the only language in stages 1–6
- Payment at pickup or delivery, no phone payment
- **Operations day begins at 05:00 local time** (`api/core/time.py`, `DAY_STARTS_AT`): an order at 00:30 counts toward yesterday. Not defined in any document, assumption from 16.09.2026, subject to change
- Domain logic errors respond with HTTP 200 in envelope so voice platform presents them to agent; only missing auth is 401
- **Test configuration in seed is placeholder** (`scripts/seed.py`): Monday closed, 11:30–14:00 and 17:00–22:00 for all services, capacity 30 (midday) / 40 (evening) guests in 30-minute slots, wait time 20/45 min. Real values come with C1 and then replace the seed
- Seed never overwrites `service_config`: live switch (mode, delivery, wait time) belongs to team
- **Capacity without dwell** (`domain/reservations/capacity.py`): `capacity.max_guests` is the sum of all guests whose reservation begins in the slot; slots represent seat turns (e.g., 18–20 and 20–22 hours). Drafts count, cancelled and soft-deleted don't; the drafts of the asking call do not count against it (#207, 14.10.2026), its confirmed tables do. `check_slot` additionally requires an open `dinein` slot (special days override). Alternatives only same day, in `slot_minutes` grid, two next closest to request, never in the past. Not defined in any document, assumption from 16.09.2026, subject to change
- A draft holds its seats only while its call can still confirm it (#250, 12.10.2026): the call has not ended and the draft is younger than `service_config.max_call_seconds`. After that it no longer counts in `check_slot`, with no cleanup job; the row stays `draft`. `confirm` checks the seats again under the day lock and answers `conflict` ("in der Zwischenzeit leider vergeben") when they went to someone else. The conversation core cuts every call by the same `service_config.max_call_seconds` the hold uses (the phone line since 12.10.2026, the text phone, the dev phone and the evals since 14.10.2026, #207). One limit stays: a draft that a correction replaced still holds its seats against other callers until its call ends or `max_call_seconds` passes; against its own call it no longer counts (#207, 14.10.2026), so a party that fills the window can move to another time in it during the call
- **`create_reservation` requires a known call** (`domain/reservations/create.py`): the `calls` row must exist before the first write, else `not_found` with outage `say`. No silent creation, so wrong IDs from platform surface immediately. Since T-1.9 (17.09.2026), `POST /v1/calls/start` is how that row gets created; before that it had to be created by hand or in tests. Assumption from 16.09.2026, subject to change
- **Draft checks the slot again** by the same rules as `check_slot`; booked → `conflict` with alternatives in `say`. Idempotency replay doesn't check again and returns the stored response; same key under a different tenant → `conflict`
- **Check and create are locked** (`_lock_capacity_days`): advisory locks per tenant for the day **and the day before** (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`, ascending) hold to transaction end, else two simultaneous calls read the same free capacity and overbook the slot. Both days because that is what `check_slot` reads: Monday 23:30 and Tuesday 00:30 share an overnight window and so the lock "Monday" (review PR #152). Neighbouring days now wait on each other briefly; harmless at phone load. Same pattern used later by `confirm`
- **Readback format** (`domain/reservations/spoken.py`): "A table for four people today / tomorrow / on Tuesday the 22nd of September at half past six under the name Müller[, with the note: …]. Does that work?" Party size up to twelve as word, above that digit. Reference point for "today" and "tomorrow" is `created_at` of the draft, not current time, so a replay after midnight delivers the same sentence. Wording is suggestion, to be refined in dialog test (D4)
- **Phone numbers** are normalized to E.164 format in `domain/customers/phone.py`, default country `+49`; `0049…`, `0…`, spaces, slashes, and parentheses are resolved. Suppressed number not yet modeled (comes with `find_customer`)
- Each write writes `audit_log` (`actor: agent`, `action: reservation.draft_created` or `reservation.confirmed`), inline in domain code. Two places don't justify a shared helper yet; check again at three
- **`confirm` is idempotent over state, not key** (`domain/confirm.py`): a second call reads `confirmed` and responds the same way, even with different `idempotency_key`; a second outbox event never occurs. The key only goes in `audit_log`. This saves a separate key table; if later a transaction can exit `confirmed` state, this assumption flips. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **`confirm` requires the same call as the draft** (`domain/confirm.py`): `reservation.call_id` must match the calling `call_id`, else `not_found`. Otherwise one caller confirms the table of another guest, and the "yes" from rule 3 comes from the wrong person. Today this doesn't break anything because the draft UUID only comes from `create_reservation` of that same call. A later "guest calls back and confirms" would be its own path with its own check. Codex review PR #90 (P1), 17.09.2026
- **Simultaneous `confirm` calls are serialized per row lock** (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the reservation), else two calls write two events to outbox and kitchen gets the transaction twice
- **`entity: "order"` is in contract but responds `not_found` until stage 2**: the branch in `domain/confirm.py` is where T-4.x adds the order. `pickup_code` stays `null` for reservations
- **`approved` (overflow operation) deliberately missing:** `RESERVATION_STATUSES` knows only `draft`, `confirmed`, `cancelled`. Approval flow comes with T-8.2
- **Backoff sequence and retry count** (`api/events/dispatcher.py`): 5 s, 30 s, 2 min, 10 min per docs/03, then `failed`. That's five delivery attempts total; `attempts` counts every attempt, including the successful one. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **Alarm is for now an ERROR line in log** (`dispatcher._on_failure`): an outbox event that finally fails reaches nobody. The team push (D13) is itself delivered through the outbox, so it cannot report its own failure; a GUI banner for failed events is still missing. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **One event per transaction, fetched with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`**: no event goes out twice even if two dispatchers run, and a slow HTTP call doesn't hold foreign rows. Order follows `next_attempt_at`, not arrival order
- **n8n treats event id as idempotency key** (header `X-Idempotency-Key`, docs/03 § outbox): a retried delivery must not notify the team twice. `n8n/team_events.json` does this (04.10.2026): the id is kept in the workflow's static data, the last 1000, and stored only after the channel node succeeded - a failed notification leaves no mark, so the retry is processed again. A notification can therefore arrive twice, never be swallowed. `order.confirmed` does not pass here (print bridge). The workflow keeps no execution history (a saved run holds the whole event with name and phone) and pins `Europe/Berlin` for the time in a message; `tenant_id` is not used for routing, a second tenant needs its own channel
- **Migrations no longer silence the app logger** (`db/migrations/env.py`): `fileConfig(..., disable_existing_loggers=False)`. Before, after the first migration in the same process every already-imported logger went silent, even in production after `alembic upgrade` from the same process
- **Lint rules live in `pyproject.toml`, not ruff default** (17.09.2026): without config, ruff version determines the rule set, and a Dependabot update fails red without code changes (PR #85, ruff 0.7 to 0.16: 14 violations). Set are `E, W, F, I, B, BLE, C4, UP, SIM, DTZ, RUF` without `E501` (formatter determines line length), plus `extend-immutable-calls` for `Depends` and friends because FastAPI needs the call in the default argument and B008 is not an error there. Verified with ruff 0.7.4 and 0.16.8, both green
- **Python stays 3.12, Dependabot jumps on the image are disabled** (17.09.2026): version lives in seven places (Dockerfile, CI, `pyproject.toml`, README badge and text, `CLAUDE.md`, this document, `CONTRIBUTING.md`); a PR that only bumps the Dockerfile (PR #82, 3.12 to 3.14) moves one and isn't tested by CI because CI doesn't build the image. 3.12 gets security updates through October 2028. The jump comes as its own work package when a dependency requires it or support end nears, moving all places together plus a `docker build` step in CI
- **`create_callback` is idempotent over state** (`domain/callbacks/create.py`): per call there's at most one open callback, a second call returns it. No `idempotency_key` in contract and no extra column; platform timeout must not leave the team two slips for the same guest. A completed callback (`done`) doesn't block a new one. **Check and create are per-call locked** (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`, like draft reservation): a lock on the callback row reaches for nothing because it doesn't exist yet. Contract: every future write path on `callbacks` takes the same lock, and database runs `READ COMMITTED` (set in `api/db.py` as of 17.09.2026 instead of server default) — under `REPEATABLE READ` the waiter doesn't see the foreign commit and creates a second one anyway. A second call does **not** update the existing callback: differing `summary`, `phone`, or `reason` are discarded. Codex review PR #95 (P1), red test first; plus a controlled overlap test (A holds lock, B waits and then reads A's callback) and a rollback test. No idempotency rule for this tool was set in docs/04, assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **The sim understands time by rule, not by model** (`sim/scripted_llm.py`): "halb acht" is 19:30, an hour below eleven means the evening ("um sieben" is 19:00), from eleven the number stands as said so the 11:30 lunch table does not become 23:30. A time without a day means today if it is still ahead, otherwise tomorrow. A day without a time is not enough for a reservation and counts as not understood. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change and only relevant for the simulator — a real model (T-2.4) resolves this itself
- **The sim writes into the same database as the API** (`sim/session.py`): a call from the terminal is a normal `calls` row with normal tool calls, not a separate test path. That is the point of the through-cut, but it also means test conversations show up in the GUI and in reports later. A separate marker for simulated calls does not exist yet; candidate as soon as shadow measurement (stage 4) counts real calls. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **Read-back sentence after callback** (`SAY_NOTED`): "I've noted your number. The restaurant will call you back as soon as possible." Wording is suggestion, to be refined in dialog test (D4)
- **Bugfix in `normalize_phone` (17.09.2026):** a bracketed `(0)` after the country code was kept as digit, `+49 (0)7221 5551234` gave `+4907221 5551234` — a number that doesn't exist. Now it's dropped for international format and kept as leading zero for national. Found building T-1.7, red test first (`api/tests/test_domain_phone.py`)
- **"KI einschalten" restores the mode from before the pause, read from `audit_log`** (`domain/status/config.py`, `_mode_before_pause`): the pause writes `{from, to}` anyway, a separate column would be a second copy of the same fact. Without a readable pause entry (mode set to `paused` directly in the DB) it falls back to `shadow`, the mode in which the AI never picks up itself - never blindly to `primary`. Assumption from 18.09.2026, subject to change
- **Wait time per service, lowerable (T-3.6, 26.09.2026)** (`domain/status/config.py` `change_wait`): pickup and delivery separately, step 15 minutes, floor 10 and cap 180 minutes, both assumptions subject to change. The limits only act in the tap direction: a value already below 10 is not raised by "-15". Replaces the former "Wartezeit +15/+30 raises both" assumption (Maxi, from the demo)
- **Browsers play the callback tone only after the first tap on the page** (`app.js`, autoplay policy): a tablet freshly loaded and never touched stays silent. In operation the first tap happens within minutes; check at T-3.5 whether a "Ton an" hint is needed. Assumption from 18.09.2026
- **"Erledigt" on a callback that no longer exists returns the fresh list, not an error** (`gui/router.py`): another tablet was faster or the page is stale, the current list is the right answer. Assumption from 18.09.2026
- **Card numbers are matched as text, without leading zeros** (`domain/menu/search.py`, Codex review PR #116, P1): "Nummer 23a" and "23 a" find `23a`, never `23`; "Nummer 07" finds `7`, "Nummer acht" finds `08`. Letters a to g only (g since T-4.12) - an "x" after a number is the quantity marker. Two dishes with the same number after stripping zeros (`7` and `07`) give `ambiguous`. Number, suffix letter and "Nummer" marker always come from one spot in the sentence (`numberwords.find_item_number_ref`, Codex review PR #117, P1): "23a, nein, Nummer 23" is 23, and "Nummer" without a number right behind it ("Nummer weiß ich nicht") does not turn a quantity into a dish number. **The import enforces the same card-number format** (Codex review PR #117, P1): digits with optional leading zeros, at most one letter a to g after them and, since T-4.12, a prefix of one or two letters in front (`s12`, `sm1`); `23h`, `35AE` or `12-3` are rejected with a clear message, because the search would otherwise silently resolve "Nummer 23h" to 23. docs/14 is updated; which prefixes the search understands follows from the numbers on the menu, and a prefix the menu does not have ("Nummer A12") is `not_found`. Compact quantities ("2x", "2 Stück", "2 stk", "2 st") are stripped from the query like in numberwords (P2). Card numbers are capped at 999, the range `numberwords` reads, and stored lowercase so `23A` and `23a` are one number (Codex review PR #117, P2x2). Two different explicitly marked numbers in one sentence ("Nummer 23, nein, Nummer 24", "Nummer 23 oder Nummer 24") give `ambiguous` with both dishes, never the first one (P1). A suffix no menu can carry ("Nummer 23h", "23ab", "23 h") makes the spoken number invalid - `not_found` naming 23h, never dish 23 (P1); whether letters are glued to the digits is decided per token position, not per digit string. Connectors ("von", "vom") and a separated suffix ("die 23 a") count as part of the number expression; "Nummer 07 oder Nummer 7" is one number; a second number after a single marker ("Nummer 23 oder 24", "Nummer 23, nein 24") is an alternative and asks back - only when a word like oder, nein, sondern, bzw. or lieber connects it; "Nummer 23 mit 2 Soßen" or "um 12 Uhr" stays dish 23; a number directly after a candidate ("Nummer zwei drei", "Nummer 23 24") is also an alternative, and a correction word only links the number right before it ("Nummer 23 mit 2 oder 3 Soßen" stays 23); "und" and a bare comma also link ("Nummer 23 und 24", "Nummer 23, 24" ask back); a marked number out of range ("Nummer 1000 und 23") stays invalid instead of letting 23 move up; the import treats "7" and "07" as the same number, exactly as the search compares, while numbers before the marker stay quantities ("zwei Nummer 23"); an import upgrades an older "23A" row to "23a" instead of adding a second dish; if the tenant already holds both "23A" and "23a", the import stops with a clear message instead of hiding one (a person merges them); "Nummer 23x" and "Nummer 23 x" are invalid, not 23 (Codex review PR #117, P2x3). A spoken letter after a number word ("dreiundzwanzig a") is not supported yet. Assumption from 18.09.2026
- **`search_menu` never falls back from a spoken number to a name** (`domain/menu/search.py`): "Nummer 99" that is not on the menu is `not_found` with "Die Nummer 99 habe ich nicht auf der Karte", not the nearest-sounding dish. A number **without** "Nummer" next to other words is a quantity ("zwei Frühlingsrollen" is not dish 2, it searches "frühlingsrollen"); a number alone ("die 23") is the dish. Assumption from 18.09.2026
- **Exact alias match ignores filler words on both sides** (`domain/menu/search.py`): aliases are stored as written on the menu ("die knusprigen rollen"), callers add "bitte" or drop the article. The comparison runs in Python over the tenant's aliases (a few hundred), because `normalize_query` exists only in Python. Revisit if a menu grows past a few thousand aliases. Assumption from 18.09.2026
- **Menu import: the file is the truth for the dishes it names, not for the whole menu** (`domain/menu/importer.py`): options and import-aliases of a listed dish are synced, but a dish missing from the file is not deactivated - it may be referenced by orders, and a forgotten line should not silently take a dish off the phone. The report lists such dishes; deactivating stays a deliberate `active;nein`. An empty allergen line deletes previously confirmed codes ("no information" beats a stale value). Assumption from 18.09.2026
- **`orders.pickup_code` added, not in docs/03 before** (`api/models/orders.py`): docs/04 `confirm` returns it and docs/06 shows it large; docs/03 is updated. Generation (format, uniqueness per day) comes with `confirm` for orders in T-4.x. Assumption from 18.09.2026
- **Menu child tables carry no `tenant_id`** (`item_options`, `item_allergens`, `item_aliases`): they belong to exactly one parent row and reach the tenant through it, as docs/03 specifies per table. **`order_items` does** (Codex review PR #114): it has two parents, and with single-column foreign keys the database would accept a dish of tenant B in an order of tenant A; both are now referenced via `(id, tenant_id)`. The general rule "every business table carries tenant_id" is read as "every table with its own business key". Assumption from 18.09.2026
- **`orders.customer_id` has no foreign key until migration 003** (`customers` does not exist yet); `address_id` arrives with 003 as docs/03 plans. The downgrade of 002 keeps `pg_trgm` installed, because an extension is database-wide. Assumption from 18.09.2026
- **GUI writes require the `HX-Request` header** (`gui/router.py`, `_require_htmx`): basic auth from the proxy is sent by the browser even for a form on a foreign site, a custom header is not without a CORS grant. Cheap protection against forged taps without a form token. Assumption from 18.09.2026
- **E10 (set by Maxi, 24.09.2026):** The call log (`docs/17`) may be kept before the legal check is complete. The DSFA draft of 24.09.2026 still counts it as personal data (D12: time plus wording can be matched to the router's call list), so the rules in `docs/17` apply and a deletion period for the CSV is open for the data protection officer. **Confirmed 20.10.2026 (legal check L15):** E10 and 90 days stand; Maxi decided that the log records only the hour of a call (docs/17 §2, T-11.4), and the privacy notice names the log once it is online.
- **E12 (set by Maxi, 24.09.2026):** Recognising callers by `caller_id` is fine; the register's VoIP already keeps the customers. Legal basis per DSFA: Art. 6 Abs. 1 lit. f with objection right (Art. 21); the balancing of interests is still to be written. Recommendation from DSFA M10, not yet decided: the agent does not volunteer stored data (name, address, order history), a shared number can belong to someone else (R6). **Confirmed 20.10.2026 (legal check L12), with M10 now binding:** the agent never says stored data aloud unprompted and only asks, a name that does not match means a new customer record, an objection switches recognition off for that number (built with T-6.2); the balancing goes into the DSFA as one page.
- **E14 (set by Maxi, 24.09.2026):** A caller's own allergy is stored as a kitchen note on the item or the order, fixed wording "WICHTIG: Keine <Zutat>. Grund: Allergie", repeated in the read-back, never a promise that a dish is free of it (docs/05 §Allergie, docs/03 `order_items.note`, T-4.10). Maxi: no extra consent needed, the caller volunteers it and ignoring it could harm the caller and the business. Note for the data protection officer: the DSFA draft rates this as health data (Art. 9) and marks the legal basis "unsicher, Anwalt fragen" (V Frage 7); Art. 9 Abs. 2 lit. a asks for explicit consent for set purposes, other exceptions are not in the norm corpus. Open recommendation: remove the note text once the order is done, orders are kept for years (docs/03). The call log rule (docs/17, no allergies in the CSV) is unaffected. **Replaced 20.10.2026 (legal check L13, decided by Maxi):** the note needs the caller's explicit consent (Art. 9(2)(a)). The agent asks "Darf ich die Allergie als Hinweis für die Küche notieren?"; on "ja" the wording above stays (D24), on "nein" only "ohne <Zutat>" without a reason. The note text is deleted at the end of the business day of the order (docs/03). Built with T-11.1 and T-11.2; until then no real caller may reach an order flow
- **AI disclosure stays as E7 (24.09.2026):** Maxi asked whether the agent could reveal itself as AI only when asked, assuming the law is not adopted yet. Not taken over: the AI Act (VO (EU) 2024/1689, amended by VO (EU) 2026/1744) applies from 02.08.2026 (Art. 113), Art. 50 is not among the later dates, and Art. 50 Abs. 1 and 5 require the information at the first interaction at the latest. What is not adopted is the GDPR part of the Digital Omnibus (norm corpus, as of 08.09.2026), which does not touch Art. 50. The greeting stays short (`docs/05` §6). Lawyer confirmation welcome, the norm corpus is a non-official copy
- **E11 (set by Maxi, 24.09.2026):** Audio recordings and any further processing of them need the customer's consent. Maxi's view: transcribed text without audio may go into the repo. The code follows the stricter rule for now, DSFA M15 and CLAUDE.md §8 (real customer sentences never in git): eval drafts from the call log carry no real sentence. Loosening this needs the data protection officer's confirmation and a CLAUDE.md change. **Settled 20.10.2026 (legal check L14):** Maxi's view is not taken over. No real caller wording goes into the repository, also not after names and numbers are removed; eval cases from transcripts are synthetic sentences (T-7.x). CLAUDE.md §8 stays as it is.
- **E9 (set, 16.09.2026):** Everything runs on EU servers or with EU vendors, including transcription and analysis. Maxi's PC is only a development workbench.
- **`prompts/system_v1.md` covers Stage 1 only, not the full skeleton from docs/05 §1** (`prompts/system_v1.md`, `prompts/tools_v1.md`): docs/05's example skeleton includes a menu index and order-taking rules, but `search_menu`/`draft_order`/etc. don't exist until Stage 2 (T-4.x). Writing a prompt that references them would tell the model it can do something the code can't (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 1). Instead, unbuilt tools are named once as "not yet available" so the model routes those requests to `create_callback` (`reason: out_of_scope`) instead of guessing. `tools_v1.md` is a new file, not named in docs/05 — the task title asked for "tool descriptions for the platform" without specifying a format or filename, so it's versioned alongside the system prompt (`tools_vN.md`) rather than folded into `docs/04_API_TOOLS.md`, which stays the full contract (error codes, idempotency rules) and isn't meant to be handed to a model as-is. Token budget is checked with `len(text) / 4` as an approximation (~440 for v1 against an 800 budget) — a real tokenizer arrives with `evals/` (T-5.1); until then this is a rough but honest floor check, not the number a real model would report. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change once D1 and `agent/prompt.py` (T-2.1) exist
- **`/v1/calls/start` and `/end` are our own contract** (`api/schemas/calls.py`, `domain/calls/`): docs/04 lists the two endpoints (§Endpunkte) but, unlike the `/v1/tools/*` tools, doesn't spec their request/response shape. Designed to match the existing conventions: `start` takes `tenant_id`/`external_session_id`/`caller_id`, is idempotent over `external_session_id` while a call with that id is still open (a platform retry gets back the same `call_id` instead of a second row); `end` takes the same `call_id`/`tenant_id` shape as the tool contracts plus `outcome`/`intent`/`cost_cents`/`model`, and is idempotent over state like `domain/confirm.py` (a second `/end`, e.g. a late "error" after an already-recorded "completed", reads back the first result instead of overwriting it). Both live directly in `main.py`, like `/health` and `/v1/tools/ping`, since they aren't part of the agent tool surface. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **`calls.delete_after` is 24 months from `started_at`** (`domain/calls/start.py`, `_delete_after`): docs/03 §Löschfristen names 24 months for anonymizing `calls` rows without personal fields, but doesn't say when that date is computed. Set once at call start rather than at `/end`, because the column is `NOT NULL` and a call must never sit without one. No cleanup job reads it yet (candidate for `jobs/`, docs/01 already lists a similar gap for abandoned reservation drafts). Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change. **Superseded 20.10.2026 (legal check L8):** `caller_id` is nulled 90 days after the call; `RETENTION_MONTHS` and `_delete_after` change with T-11.2
- **`caller_id` is normalized best-effort, never rejected** (`domain/calls/start.py`, `_caller_id`): a suppressed number stays `NULL`; anything else goes through `normalize_phone`, but if the platform's caller id isn't a phone number `normalize_phone` can parse, the raw value is kept as-is rather than failing the call. A call must never be lost over the log being imprecise (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 5). Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **`calls.tool_calls` is written by a generic middleware, not by each tool** (`core/tool_log.py`): every `/v1/tools/*` response (except `ping`, which has no `call_id` and isn't a real tool) gets one entry `{name, duration_ms, ok, error_code?}` appended via an atomic `jsonb ||` update, keyed off the `call_id` in the request body. Best-effort: a broken `call_id` or a DB hiccup logs a warning and never changes the response the agent gets. Runs in the thread pool (`starlette.concurrency.run_in_threadpool`), not inline in the middleware's `async def`, so one slow write can't serialize concurrent tool calls on the event loop. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **`transfer_to_team` availability is derived from opening hours** (`domain/callbacks/transfer.py`): `available` is true whenever any service window (`dinein`/`pickup`/`delivery`) is currently open, false otherwise. There is no real reachability signal (a line ping on the extension) before the telephony adapter exists (T-1.11, after D1); this is the only fact in the DB today that says anything about someone being present. `available: false` doesn't fail the call — the agent falls back to `create_callback` per docs/05. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change once the adapter can report a real answer/no-answer **Since T-10.8 (D25, 20.10.2026):** the opening hours only decide whether the team is rung; inside them the dial result has the last word on the phone line (`callbacks.team_missed`).
- **The team leg nobody answered (T-10.8, 20.10.2026), Claude's readings:** (a) every unanswered status at `TEAM_PHONE` counts (no answer in 30 s, busy, refused, not reachable), and a listed number first is followed by `TEAM_PHONE` before the card; (b) the reason of the card is the transfer's, a cancellation becomes `human_requested` as in the core, a transfer without a reason (paused, shadow, the outage path) `out_of_scope`, so no new reason value and no migration; (c) the card says what happened and the reason, not the guest's words, which are gone with the conversation's state (ponytail, docs/20 §12); (d) the call row is the newest of that line id within two hours (the dialplan's backstop of an hour plus two legs); (e) the audit actor of such a card is `telephony`; (f) the two sentences are drafts (docs/05 §6), and only a caller the AI answered hears one.
- **`agent/dispatch.py` re-does what `api/tools/*.py` does over HTTP, directly** (T-2.1, docs/11 §agent: "`agent/` darf `domain/` benutzen, nie `tools/`"): validates arguments into the same Pydantic request schemas, calls the same `domain/` functions, and writes the same `calls.tool_calls` entry itself (via a new `core/tool_log.append_tool_call()`, factored out of the HTTP middleware so both paths share one SQL statement) — because that middleware only runs on the `/v1/tools/*` HTTP path and never sees calls the agent core makes directly. On an `AppError` it rolls the shared session back before logging, since (unlike the HTTP path's fresh session per request) this session lives for the whole call and a partial write from a failed tool must not ride along into the next one. `idempotency_key` for `create_reservation`/`confirm` is derived with `core/ids.idempotency_key()` from the call and its own arguments whenever the model doesn't supply one — the model must never decide it (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 1). Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **`agent/loop.py`'s `_handoff()` defaults to reason `"not_understood"` for a self-triggered handoff** (`max_call_seconds`, too many tool hops, or the understanding ladder giving up — T-2.2): closest existing fit for "the loop itself gave up" rather than a genuine customer request; `TransferReason`/`CallbackReason` (docs/03) have no dedicated value for it and adding one is a schema change out of scope here. Superseded the earlier T-2.1 fix's `"human_requested"` default once T-2.2's `escalation.py` started passing real, customer-triggered reasons (`complaint`/`human_requested`/`cancellation`) through the same `_handoff()`, making the fallback default's own label matter for the cases nobody actually chose. `create_callback`'s `reason` falls back further from `"cancellation"` to `"human_requested"` when the real reason is passed through, because `CallbackReason` (docs/03) never included cancellation — it's a transfer-only reason (`api/schemas/transfer.py`), same gap noted in T-1.8's Codex fix. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **`agent/escalation.py`'s trigger words are a first pass, not a linguistic study** (`docs/05 §4`): plain substring matching on lowercased text for the three Stage-1-relevant triggers (complaint, human-requested, cancellation) — deliberately narrow (misses paraphrases, synonyms, negation like "keine Beschwerde") to keep false positives low, since a false trigger here skips the model and the ladder entirely. Widening it is an evals question (T-5.1: measure false-negative rate against real transcripts), not something to guess at from first principles now. `agent/ladder.py`'s field names (`party_size`, `reserved_for`, `guest_name`, …) aren't yet a fixed contract anywhere — they're whatever string a real `LLMClient` (T-2.4) chooses to report in `LLMTurn.understanding_failure`, matching whatever key it also uses in `state_patch`. Assumption from 17.09.2026, subject to change
- **Bugfix in `db/migrations/env.py` (17.09.2026, found building T-2.1):** `db/alembic.ini` sets `[logger_root] level = WARN`; `fileConfig()` applied that unconditionally, so any migration run in the same process as the app (every test through the `migrated_db_url` fixture) silently downgraded `api.core.logging.configure_logging()`'s INFO level to WARN for the rest of the process — dropping every later INFO-level app log line without any single test noticing locally. Only visible because `api/tests/test_agent_dispatch.py` and friends (alphabetically before `test_core_logging.py`) now run a migration before the two tests that actually assert on captured log output; before T-2.1 the file order hid it by luck. Fixed by saving the root logger's level before `fileConfig()` and restoring it after — alembic's own logger levels from the ini still apply, only the accidental root override is undone. Red before the fix: `test_core_logging.py::test_request_id_aus_header_wird_uebernommen_und_geloggt` and `::test_kontext_bleibt_nicht_am_naechsten_request_kleben`, run together with `test_agent_dispatch.py`

- **`draft_order` (T-4.5, 23.09.2026):** (a) `idempotency_key` is part of the request, as for every write tool (docs/04 §1), though the draft_order example lacked it. (b) A required option group without a choice is asked for, even when the menu marks a default - filling it in would be a guess (CLAUDE.md §2 rule 2). Required group = exactly one choice; optional groups allow several distinct options. (c) `ready_at` = now + `pickup_wait_minutes`, no load model yet (docs/11 `ready_time.py` stays unbuilt until there is a signal for load). (d) Limits 30 per position and 30 positions guard against mishearing, bigger orders go to the team. (e) Item order in the readback is kept via microsecond offsets in `order_items.created_at`, as the table has no position column - a column would cost a migration for no other gain today
- **Overdue orders on the tablet (#252, 13.10.2026):** (a) Overdue from the fifth minute on: an order that has waited exactly the restaurant's minutes is red. (b) The wait starts at the guest's yes (the audit entry `order.confirmed`), not at the draft, which may be minutes older in a long call. (c) Red is a mark, nothing more: no cancellation, no automatic approval, no message to the guest. (d) The card shows the clock time of the yes, not minutes waited, because the column is redrawn on a change and not every minute. (e) The sound is the alarm of a complaint, played once when a card turns red while the view is open; a card that is red when the page loads does not ring, like every other card. (f) At least one minute; there is no value that switches the mark off.
- **Turn deadline (#252, 13.10.2026):** (a) The deadline is for one guest turn as a whole, from the moment the core gets the sentence to the moment it has something to say, model calls and tools together; what the ear needs before that is not in it. (b) An answer that arrives exactly at the deadline counts as too late. (c) Phone line only: the text phone and the evals pass no deadline. (d) The guest hears the existing outage sentence, no new wording. (e) A model request is cut off after what is left of the deadline, counted per phase on the wire, so in the worst case a request outlasts it by the time the connection took; the clock is read again when the answer is in. (f) `LLM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` stays the upper limit of one request.
- **Order approval threshold (#252, 13.10.2026):** (a) "Above 150 euros" is read as strictly above: an order of exactly the limit goes to the kitchen. (b) What counts is `total_cents`, items plus delivery fee, the amount the guest was read. (c) The limit is read when the guest confirms; a correction on the tablet afterwards is the team's own act and sends its ticket as before, whatever the new total. (d) The guest hears what a guest hears for any order that waits ("Das Team bestätigt die Bestellung gleich noch." on the text phone); nothing new is said about the amount.
- **Postgres application role (#252, 13.10.2026):** (a) The role is the user of `DATABASE_URL`; no new variable names it, and `OWNER_DATABASE_URL` is the only new line. (b) The owner stays the image's superuser and owns every table; nothing changes its owner, so the move is two lines in `.env` and back again. (c) Separate roles are for the server; `.env.example` ships the single user because tests and evals create databases. (d) Not asked for and added: the API refuses to start as a superuser outside dev and test, and `api` and `dispatcher` do not get the owner's password in their environment. (e) The role keeps what every Postgres role has by default: temporary tables and a connection to the other databases of the server.
- **Callback number mark (#252, 12.10.2026):** Maxi decided "mark a number that differs from the caller ID". A call with a withheld number is marked too, in its own words ("Anruf ohne Rufnummer"): the named number is just as unbacked there. A caller ID the platform sent in a form that cannot be normalized counts as another number. The mark is shown on the tablet only; the team notification (`callback.created`) is unchanged.
- **`confirm` for orders (23.09.2026):** (a) Only `primary` sends an order straight to the kitchen; `overflow`, `shadow` and `paused` all wait for approval - docs/02 says so for `overflow`, and `shadow`/`paused` take no real calls, so an order there is a test and must not reach the kitchen by itself. (b) Pickup code format "A" + running number per business day, as in the mockup; the letter keeps it apart from a card number at the counter. (c) The response keeps `status: confirmed` also for `approved`/`handed_over` rows: for the caller it is confirmed either way
- **Tablet column "Neue Bestellungen" (T-4.7, 25.09.2026):** (a) "Passt" sets `approved` in every mode, not only in `overflow`: it means "the team checked it"; outside `primary` it is also the release. docs/03 is updated; T-8.2 makes the release switchable. (b) Corrections live in `audit_log` (`order.corrected`), not in a table of their own, and the ticket reuses `order.confirmed` with `revision` and `correction_reason` instead of a new event type - both would need a migration, and PR #139 holds 003. (c) The team may add a dish that is sold out today; it is marked "heute aus". (d) Card numbers are resolved like `search_menu` ("7" finds "07"), via a private import of `_by_number` until #139 no longer touches `search.py`. (e) A swapped or added position gets the base price of today's menu; a kept position keeps its frozen price; swapping only the options of the same dish keeps the frozen base price. (f) The correction box closes after 90 seconds without a tap. (g) Form fields are parsed with the standard library, not `Form()`, to avoid `python-multipart` as a new dependency.
- **Agent splits, HTTP asks back (23.09.2026):** the same multi-dish sentence gets two answers. Over HTTP `search_menu` answers `ambiguous` with "eins nach dem anderen", because a foreign voice platform may not know `split_positions`. The own agent core splits and searches per part, because it can: the caller does not have to repeat themselves. Both paths use the same `split_positions`, so they cut at the same places
---
