| Point | Why still open | When due |
|---|---|---|
| Two helpers where one does for the forms of a number (`deploy/asterisk/render.sh`, `e164` and `same`) | Ponytail review of PR #340 (P2), 20.10.2026: `e164` turns a number into `+...` and `same` strips the `+` again; one function that returns the bare digits (`+` and `00` dropped, a leading `0` becomes `49`) and a one-line `same` compare the same, 4 lines shorter. Not on PR #340: a new commit there needs CI and a review again, Maxi shipped it as it was. The render tests in `api/tests/test_asterisk_render.py` cover every form | With the next telephony PR |
| `[global]` with `endpoint_identifier_order=ip` may do nothing (`deploy/asterisk/conf/pjsip.conf`) | Ponytail review of PR #340 (P2), 20.10.2026: the block's own comment says it does not switch identification by the From user off, `identify_by=ip` on the endpoint does; and Asterisk's default order is believed to start with `ip` already (not checked). Check `pjsip show settings` on the workbench with and without the block; same output and the drill "stranger" still green: delete the block and its comment, else keep it and say in the comment what it changes | With the next telephony PR |
| The call handler is not safe for events of one call on two threads at once (`telephony/handler.py`) | Codex on `dd35c0f` (PR #310, P2), 15.10.2026: a failure and the end of the leg on two threads can still use the call's database session at the same time (the rollback of `_fail` next to the write of `_finish`). `CallSession` and the fake adapter report the events of a call one after another, and `port.CallEvents` now says so. Fix when an adapter that does not is added: a lock per call around its session. | With such an adapter |
| With the ear on the processor a sentence whose transcription takes longer than 10 s goes to the team (`telephony/session.py` `UTTERANCE_SECONDS`) | Codex on `84ec82e` (PR #310, P2), 15.10.2026: the watchdog's 30 s only end a run that hangs; the call session still waits 10 s for the final after the last partial, time in line behind another call included. On the graphics card a minute of speech takes about a second. The deadline would have to follow the device (or the ear report that it is still working). Production names `VOICE_EAR_DEVICE=cpu` only so that `voice` starts there; its ear and mouth are to be services (docs/20 §6). | Before a host runs the local ear on the processor for real calls |
| A call whose process dies while its goodbye or transfer is still playing stays open (`telephony/handler.py`, `_ending`) | Ultra review on PR #298, 15.10.2026: since PR #298 the call log is written when the line reports the end of the leg, not when the core asks for the hangup or transfer. A process that ends in between (watchdog, deploy, out of memory) leaves the `calls` row without `ended_at`, outcome and cost, and its drafts count against capacity until they expire; the same holds for a call in progress, as before. So does a final write that waits on a lock past 30 s. Fix: a job in `jobs/` that closes calls open longer than `max_call_seconds` as `error`. | Before the first test call with real guests (T-10.7) |
| A tool's wait on a lock is bounded at 2 s, not by what is left of the turn (`telephony/app.py` `DB_TIMEOUT_MS`, `db.bounded_sessions`) | Codex on `879f51e` (PR #298, P2), 15.10.2026: with 9.5 s of a 10 s turn spent on the model, a tool that waits on a row lock keeps the guest up to 2 s past the deadline; with `turn_deadline_seconds=1` more directly. Fix: `set local statement_timeout` from `ConversationLoop._left_in_turn` before each tool, capped by the bound. Touches the fate of a call (`xhigh`). | Before the first test call with real guests (T-10.7) |
| `telephony/app.py` builds Whisper and Piper itself (`_whisper`, `_piper`) | Codex on `879f51e` (PR #298, P2), 15.10.2026: AGENTS.md wants a provider only under `api/telephony/adapters/`; the loaders should move behind an adapter-owned factory, so `app.py` only picks an `Ear` and a `Mouth` by name. Same kind as the row on `MediaLine` above. | With that row |
| A partial run of the decision issue keeps every old key (`scripts/decision_issue.py`, `plan_partial`) | Codex on `2bff6f5` (PR #307, P2), 15.10.2026: while one source fails over several days, a finding the other source clears and later reports again is not mentioned again, because the marker keeps all old keys; the section itself is cleared correctly. Fixing it means keys per section in the marker. Noted under the rule for P2 after a finished review round | When a source fails for more than a day, or with the next change to `decision_issue.py` |
| `telephony/router.py` and `app.py` know Asterisk's media channel (`MediaLine`, `call_of`, MEDIA_START) | Codex on `661b936` (PR #297, P2), 14.10.2026: AGENTS.md wants a provider name only under `api/telephony/adapters/`. The spec placed the WebSocket endpoint at `telephony/router.py` and the process at `telephony/app.py` (docs/20 §2, docs/11), and both are about one telephone system only. Keeping them where the spec puts them; moving the endpoint into `adapters/` (an `asterisk_router.py`, with `app.py` taking the router from there) is a small refactor without a change of behaviour, for when a second access other than Asterisk is in sight, or for Maxi to ask | With a second telephone system, or Maxi's word |
| A sentence goes to the line only when the mouth has made all of it (`telephony/session.py` `_synthesize`) | Codex on PR #286 (13.10.2026, P2). `Mouth.speak` delivers audio in pieces, and the session joins them before `Line.play`: with an engine that streams, the caller waits for the whole sentence instead of the first piece. Queuing piece by piece changes the mark, the deadline and what a failure in the middle of a sentence means. Measured with T-10.5 (14.10.2026): Piper takes 0.17 s to the first piece of a read-back of 12.5 s and 0.76 s to all of it, so a long read-back starts about 0.6 s later than it could; a short sentence differs little. | T-10.7, with latency by stage on the phone path |

Nobody sets these flags in this project; the hook's warning names the blocked pull anyway (PR #290). Left off on purpose: next to a tool result the model said its last sentence again word for word instead of the read-back. | The scripted stand-in takes the first word of a split number as a quantity (`sim/scripted_order.py` `_stated_quantity`) | Found 12.10.2026 while fixing PR #271: the order "Die drei und zwanzig." books dish 23 three times, because "drei" is read as the quantity and "drei und zwanzig" as the number. Not fixed in PR #271 (its own bug, red test first) | own bug task before the audio evals (T-10.7) |
| Our containers run as root (`api/Dockerfile` has no `USER`; `api`, `dispatcher` and `push` run with uid 0, checked on the dev stack 11.10.2026) | Raised by a friend of Maxi's, 11.10.2026. Fine on the workbench: the bind mounts and `--reload` are simpler as root. On the server a hole in the API would hand an attacker root inside the container, with the mounted source and `.env` values. `db` (uid 70) and `n8n` (uid 1000) already drop root; `caddy` needs it only to bind 80 and 443. To do: a user in `api/Dockerfile`, `user:` for `push`, the production Compose file without source mounts, and on the server a deploy user instead of a root login. The voice layer's containers start that way from the beginning: since T-1.11 `voice` runs as uid 10001 in every Compose file, and Asterisk drops root itself | With T-9.2, before the server takes a real call |
| `.claude/hooks/sync_main.sh` (and its Codex copy) does not warn when a clean, checked-out `main` holds unpushed commits | Raised by the Codex review of PR #278 (13.10.2026), older than the PR: `git pull --ff-only` answers "already up to date" when local `main` is ahead, so a new task can start from unpublished commits. | A phone number without an area code is stored as an international one (`api/domain/customers/phone.py` `normalize_phone`) | Found while building the guard for guest details (12.10.2026), older than it: digits that start with neither `0` nor `+` get a `+` in front, so "555 1234" becomes `+5551234`, passes the E.164 check and is booked. Not read back, so the team would dial a number that does not exist or reaches someone else. On the path through the conversation core the guard refuses it since the review of PR #244: a number counts only with an area or country code, and the hint tells the model to ask for it. `normalize_phone` itself is unchanged, so the HTTP tools and a caller ID in that form still take it. Proposal: such a number is refused in the domain with a sentence that asks for the area code; touches `create_reservation`, `draft_order` and `create_callback` (effort `xhigh`, own PR, a red test first) | Maxi decides; before anything but the conversation core calls the tools |
| A number the core does not take is dropped without a word to the model (`api/agent/state.py` `note_guest_details`, `apply_state_patch`) | Independent review of PR #244 (12.10.2026); smaller since the core writes a number the guest gave into `slots` itself. What is left: a number the core cannot take (no area code, other digits next to it, no word that calls it a number) leaves the slot as it was. With a caller ID in the state the booking can then go on under the caller ID, the number they call from, without the guest having been asked again; seen on the scripted stand-in in the reservation flow. Reachable, but not what the guest asked for. Options: tell the model at once when digits of a turn were not taken as a number (a line in the state, which is prompt text and needs a run against the baseline), or leave it. Needs numbers from a real model | with the first full run on a real model |
| The tail of a number can stand as a number when its head is not told as one (`api/agent/guest_details.py` `_may_be_a_head`) | Verification of `8835fa3` by the independent reader (12.10.2026), low likelihood, left: the last digits of a turn count unless other digits of the turn may be the head of the number, and a head is told by how it begins (a plus, a zero with three digits or more, or six digits). Not told: two to five digits that begin with 1 to 9 ("49 171, 0345678" gives +49345678, a country code said without plus), a first half written like a date with periods or hyphens ("02.11.55, 012345"), and a head in an earlier turn ("0171 20", then "034567"; "0171 2345" as a whole turn is itself a valid number). Each needs the text to break right before a group that begins with 0 and a model that writes exactly the tail. Where a speech-to-text engine ends a turn and where it puts commas decides how real this is. Seen with the ear of T-10.5 (14.10.2026, one synthetic voice through the modelled line, docs/20 §11): Whisper writes numbers said in words as digits and groups them its own way ("0721 5.551.234", "01712345678", "0171 2345678"); the core read four of six numbers as said, "plus" stayed a word ("Plus 491712345678") and "01712.345.678 um 19 Uhr" was not read, so the guest is asked again; nothing wrong was taken. A head told apart from its tail was not seen. | T-10.7, with real voices |
| Blanks alone do not tell where a phone number ends (`api/agent/guest_details.py` `numbers_said`) | Independent review of PR #244 and Codex on `31a3a81` (12.10.2026), a known ceiling: "0171 2345678 19 Uhr" without any punctuation is read as one number of thirteen digits and, since the core writes the guest's number into `slots` itself, stands there as that. Numbers are said in groups, so a blank cannot end one; behind a hyphen, a slash or a closing bracket one or two digits are refused since the fix of the open threads. A speech-to-text engine without punctuation makes the blank form likely. Closing it needs knowledge of number lengths (a German mobile number has 11 or 12 digits) or the readback of a spoken number (row below). Seen with the ear of T-10.5 (14.10.2026, one synthetic voice through the modelled line, docs/20 §11): Whisper writes numbers said in words as digits and groups them its own way ("0721 5.551.234", "01712345678", "0171 2345678"); the core read four of six numbers as said, "plus" stayed a word ("Plus 491712345678") and "01712.345.678 um 19 Uhr" was not read, so the guest is asked again; nothing wrong was taken. The number before a time was not read at all, rather than read too long. | T-10.7, with real voices |
| A transfer the model asks for and that finds nobody is not followed by a callback (`api/agent/loop.py` `run_turn`, `sim/scripted_llm.py` `_after_tool`) | Codex PR #244 (P1, 12.10.2026), older than that PR: the loop falls back to a callback only when it hands over itself (`_handoff`). When a model calls `transfer_to_team` and the result says nobody is reachable, the result goes back to the model; the scripted stand-in then says "Ich gebe an das Team weiter" and nothing exists. PR #244 took away the one new way into it (the stand-in asks again for a refused name or number, going by the hint of the refusal), every other tool failure without a sentence still leads there. Belongs with the open decision of T-1.13 (ask for a callback number instead of ending the line): if the loop took over, a guest with a withheld number would hear the last-resort sentence where a model could still ask for a number (rule 5, effort `xhigh`, own PR) | Maxi decides; with T-10.8 at the latest |
| A caller who hangs up while the team rings gets no callback card (`deploy/asterisk/conf/extensions.conf`, extension `team`) | T-10.8, 20.10.2026: the dialplan ends with the caller, and the report runs only after a leg nobody answered. The team telephone shows a missed call from our registration, not the guest's number (docs/20 §12). An `h` extension could report it as well, about four lines | Maxi decides; before the first call from a real customer |
| A phone number taken from speech is not read back (`api/domain/reservations/create.py` `readback`, `api/domain/ordering/readback.py`, `prompts/system_v2.md`) | Codex PR #244 (12.10.2026). The core takes the last number the guest named as one to call, and a tool books under it; what it cannot know is whether the guest meant it as the number to be reached under ("meine alte Nummer war 0172 ..." has the word and is taken), and it reads digits joined by blanks as one number (row above). The name is read back, the number is not, so either goes unnoticed and a callback or an order can reach someone else. Proposal: when the number of a draft is not the caller ID, the readback names it ("erreichbar unter null eins sieben eins ...") or the agent has it confirmed first (docs/05 §2, rung 2; the state example of §5 already lists "Rufnummer bestätigen"); a dialog change, so it needs a run on a real model | Maxi decides; before a real call with a withheld number |
| The check on name and number hears digits and whole words only (`api/agent/guest_details.py`) | By design (12.10.2026): a number said in words, a number without its area code or with a country code and no plus, a number named without a word for it before the agent has asked, a number followed by other digits in the same turn, one with a dash or a slash between blanks or named in two turns, letter names when spelling ("Doppel-L", "Ypsilon") and a name with another accent are not heard; the model's name or number is then refused and the agent asks again, which can end with the team for a guest whose number is withheld. Nothing is booked wrongly. How numbers and letters arrive as text is decided by the speech-to-text engine. Seen with the ear of T-10.5 (14.10.2026, one synthetic voice through the modelled line, docs/20 §11): Whisper writes numbers said in words as digits and groups them its own way ("0721 5.551.234", "01712345678", "0171 2345678"); the core read four of six numbers as said, "plus" stayed a word ("Plus 491712345678") and "01712.345.678 um 19 Uhr" was not read, so the guest is asked again; nothing wrong was taken. A spelled name arrives as "M-E-I-E-R", which is heard. | T-10.7, with real voices |
| The evals do not count a refused name or number (`evals/recorder.py`) | By design (12.10.2026): the recorder counts a refused attempt only where it has a rule of its own (a guessed dish, a `confirm` without a yes). A `draft_order` refused for an invented name is in `calls.tool_calls` and in the replay, not in "Refused attempts"; a wrong name or number that got through shows in `expected` (`customer_name`, `phone`). For prompt work the count would show how often a model tries. A reservation with a number without its area code, then with it, is `reservierung_0031` in `evals/cases/` since the own review of PR #244. In the pickup and the callback flow the scripted stand-in asks again as well since the review of `13ed34f`; cases for those two flows are still missing | with the first full run on a real model |
| A wish for the other flow, heard while a draft waits for its yes, does not drop the draft (`api/agent/loop.py` `run_turn`, `state.note_intent`) | Own review of PR #241 (08.10.2026), older than that PR: a table is read back, the guest says "ach, lieber nur etwas zum Abholen", the model answers with a question and no tool call, so nothing supersedes the draft. A later "Ja" lets a `confirm` of that table through the guard (explicit yes, draft read back before the turn). It needs a model that calls `confirm` there. Proposal: a wish for the other flow at `readback_pending` drops the draft like a changed cart does; safe direction, it costs one more readback. Touches the path to `confirm` (effort `xhigh`, own PR) | Maxi decides; with the guard for guest details or after the first full run |
| A wish the guest takes back in words stays in the state (`api/agent/intent.py` `heard`, `state.note_intent`) | Codex PR #241 (P2, recorded): after "ich möchte etwas zum Abholen bestellen" the guest says "doch nicht zum Abholen, ich möchte einen Tisch für vier". The sentence holds a negation and says nothing, so the state keeps `pickup` until `create_reservation`. Only the first wish of a call is taken on purpose (a later word must not flip it), so a correction in words is the price. Fix: a sentence that rules out the wish the state holds clears it, while no draft exists | with the first full run on a real model, which shows whether the hint helps at all |
| Table details of the same turn come too late for the heard wish (`api/agent/loop.py` `run_turn`) | Review of the head of PR #241 (P2, after three fully worked rounds, rule 24.09.2026: recorded, not blocking): `note_intent` runs before the model, so in "wir würden gern morgen um sieben zu sechst kommen, kann man da was vorbestellen?" the order word makes the call a pickup, and the party size the model notes in that turn arrives after it. It stays a pickup until `create_reservation`. Fix: withdraw a wish that came from the bare order word once the turn's `slots` show a table, or drop the bare order word | with the first full run on a real model, which shows whether the hint helps at all |
| The state shows no open question (`api/agent/state.py`) | Deliberate (07.10.2026): the candidates of an offer are not shown after a real model ordered the one the guest had not chosen. Ceiling: "die erste" as an answer to an offer cannot be resolved from the state, the model searches again or asks. Fix: the bridge's poll is already a sign of life, so the server can remember the last one and show "bridge silent since ..." on the tablet and in the monitoring | with the monitoring (T-8.3), latest before orders run in `primary` |

None of the 3,433 transcripts of the T-10.3 runs drops the "und" of a split number, so the question costs nothing on real speech. A hesitation is one pattern, read by `numberwords`, `split` and `normalize` alike (`numberwords.hesitation`): "öh", "eh", "äääh", "ähh", "hmm" are pauses as "äh" is; "ah" and "oh" are not, the ears wrote them for the letters a and o, and "eh" or "äääh" behind a number may be the e as "äh" may. The tens half of a cut number is no quantity (`find_quantity("drei und äh zwanzig Portionen Pho Bo")` was 20). A comma on either side of the "und" is the same pause for the splitter ("die drei und, zwanzig", "die drei, und zwanzig Frühlingsrollen", "und äh, zwanzig"), and a name that hits several dishes before an unclear number no longer lets the name search drop the number ("einmal Suppe, und 30", `search._position_parts`). | 19.10.2026 | **A pause between "und" and a tens word no longer gives a wrong number** (open point from Codex PR #147): the tens-word gate in `sole_item_number` (`_opens`) looked only at the token right after the "und", so "und äh zwanzig" and "und bitte zwanzig" returned item 20, the cut-off half of "drei und zwanzig" with a hesitation in between. It now looks past what can sit inside a number split by a pause (`numberwords.INSIDE_SPLIT`: punctuation including "..." and a stray full stop, hesitations, "bitte"), and the tens may be digits as well ("und 20": the ear wrote "Nummer 3 und 20" for a 23, docs/20). The pause is transparent: a form asks back exactly when its form without the pause does on `main` ("und äh vierzig sieben" like "und vierzig sieben", "und äh zwanzig Nummer 24" like "und zwanzig Nummer 24"). With digits the same now holds for a quantity: "und 20 mal die 13" and "und zwanzigmal die 13" ask like "und zwanzig mal die 13" on `main`, since "drei und zwanzig mal die 13" is 23 portions. An article, opening word, marker or card prefix cannot sit inside a split number and keeps the continuation: "und noch die zwanzig" is 20, "und S zwanzig" S20, "und Nummer zwanzig" 20, "und ein und zwanzig" 21, as on `main`. The splitter had the same gap: `split._split` read only the words right at the "und", so "die drei und äh zwanzig" and "die drei äh und zwanzig" became the 3 and the 20 as two dishes on the agent's path; it looks past the same pause words now (before the "und" without "bitte": "die 4 bitte und 20" stays two) and keeps the number whole, and `sole_item_number` asks for any unit, pause, "und" and tens half (`_split_by_pause`), also next to a prefix, a letter or a name ("die S drei und äh zwanzig", "die drei und äh 20a", "die drei und bitte zwanzig Frühlingsrollen"); "zwei und zwanzigmal die 13" is joined like "zwei und zwanzig mal die 13" (`tens_half`). **The sink of every such question was a silent drop:** in a sentence with several dishes, `search.position_parts` handed the whole sentence to the name search as soon as one piece had an unclear number, and the name search strips the numbers, so "einmal Pho Bo, und 30" became Pho Bo alone (the same on `main` for "einmal Pho Bo, und zwanzig"); the dish part before a wish did the same ("und 30 mit Reis" searched "mit Reis" as a name and found another dish). An unclear number in a piece or in the dish part now makes the whole sentence ask for the number; in a sentence of several parts only the part with it asks ("die 13 und die 24, und 30" takes the 13), unless that part would not ask on its own ("Pho Bo, und zwanzig"). A first version kept such a piece as a position of its own; the ultra review of the PR (19.10.2026) showed that this took the half before a cut as a dish ("die drei, und zwanzig" took the 3), hung an allergy on the wrong part and still dropped numbers after spellings `normalize` does not know ("und öhm 30"), so it was replaced before the merge; the speech kit judge (`evals/heard.py`) stays as on `main`. My own xhigh review (eight rounds) and the ultra review found the splitter, the sink, digits, the ellipsis and that a first version which skipped every word that is no number asked back on all the continuations above. Checked against `main` on 59 forms and 23 splits: every change is a question instead of a number, or one piece instead of two, and every multi-dish sentence that then went to the sink asks now (8 `search_menu` and `position_parts` tests, red on `main` and on the first version). 21 new lines in `evals/cases/nummern.jsonl`, 12 red on `main`; number eval 232/232; 10 new split cases, 8 red on `main`; full suite 4,877 passed, 230 skipped, latency 32 passed (after merging `main`; the banner re-rendered to 2,600+ tests); cases 41/41 and targets 1/27 with the same red cases as `main`. Not changed: "und äh ja zwanzig" and "und äh also zwanzig" still give 20 ("ja" and "also" open a continuation; noted on #327). Left as open points: #325 (`_scan` joins "drei zwanzig" into 23 without an "und"), #327 (hesitation spellings outside `_HESITATIONS`) and #328 (a pause inside a number for `find_quantity` and the comma forms: "drei und äh zwanzig Portionen" is 20 portions, "und äh, zwanzig" splits before the number rule sees it) |

| 12.10.2026 | **Backup and restore no longer race on the gpg home (T-9.3, flaky CI test):** CI run 38240153911 (PR #244, commit `df98ccf`) failed with `test_two_backups_in_the_same_second_never_share_a_file` alone: one of the two backups died with `gpg: Fatal: can't create directory '/home/runner/.gnupg': File exists`, which the script reports as "pg_dump failed, nothing kept". gpg creates a missing home directory without a lock, and the runner has none until the first gpg call of a run, so the test failed whenever it was that first call. Not a test-only problem: the nightly cron and a backup by hand can meet the same way on a fresh server. `require_passphrase` in `scripts/lib_pg.sh`, the one gate before every gpg call of `backup.sh` and `restore.sh`, now creates the home gpg will use, asked from `gpgconf --list-dirs homedir`, with `mkdir -p` under umask 077; the test itself is unchanged. Measured in the project image plus gpg 2.4.7 and the Postgres 16 client, against a throwaway Postgres 16: two bare `gpg --symmetric` on a fresh HOME, 270 of 300 rounds with the fatal error, with the directory made first 0 of 300 (and 0 of 100 with six at once); the test with a fresh, empty HOME per round red 3 of 3 before, green 50 of 50 after; the whole file with four workers and a fresh HOME 12 of 12 green; the suite once, 3448 passed (in that image without the two tests that need a docker command, which fail there on `main` as well). New tests `test_the_gpg_home_exists_before_gpg_is_called` (named by `GNUPGHOME`, the default below HOME, and a `GNUPGHOME` with a tilde) and `test_a_gpg_home_that_cannot_be_made_stops_the_script` (exit 2), red 3 of 3 before. They call the gate and not gpg: gpg-agent keeps its socket in the home, and in Git Bash a home as long as pytest's `tmp_path` is too long for it (204 characters failed, 11 worked). In Git Bash on Windows the cases were run by hand, pytest was not; that run showed `mkdir -m 700` creating the directory and then failing there, hence the umask. **Codex review PR #246 (P2) fixed:** the first version built the path from `GNUPGHOME` itself, which gpg reads differently than a shell: with `~/x` as cron hands it over, the script made `./~/x` and gpg used `$HOME/x`. The home is now asked from gpgconf (test case red before, green after); the two-backups test with a fresh HOME 30 of 30 and the whole file 6 of 6 green again |
| 12.10.2026 | **Security audit of the repository, and what production offers to the internet:** three read-only passes (HTTP surface, deployment and scripts, conversation core and domain) plus GitHub's own alerts (none open) and the git history (no key, token, `.env` or dump ever committed). No way was found for a caller or a hostile model to set a price, add a dish without a searched id, reach another call or tenant, or confirm in the turn that built the draft. **Fixed here:** the pinned n8n ignores `N8N_BASIC_AUTH_*` (checked against the image: `/` answers 200 and offers the owner setup to the first visitor), so the editor now sits behind its own access in the Caddyfile; the proxy passes on only `/gui*`, `/v1/kitchen/*` and `/health`, since the voice layer reaches the tools inside the Docker network; outside `ENV=dev` and `test` the API does not start on the placeholder token or on fewer than 32 characters, an empty token opens nothing, the comparison is constant-time; the tool log middleware no longer parses the body or writes to the database for a request without the token (it ran before the token check, so anyone could append entries to a call whose ids they knew) and no longer logs a tool name that matched no route; `/docs` and `/openapi.json` exist in dev only; SQL errors are logged without their values; in production `api` and `dispatcher` run as uid 10001 and every mount of the checkout is read-only, because the host runs `scripts/backup.sh` from it every night; HSTS, no framing of the view, bodies over 256 kB refused; the three host names come from `.env`. Checked live on a private Docker network: API as uid 10001 on a read-only checkout (migrate, seed, serve), n8n and Caddy with this Caddyfile, 21 requests with the expected status, and the same with no access value set (view and editor closed for everyone). 9 of the new tests fail on the old code; suite 3,501 passed, latency 32, evals 27/27, numbers 181/181. Found by the review of this pull request and fixed in it: the dispatcher did not carry `ENV: prod`, so it would have started on the placeholder token, and a tool path called with the wrong method was logged as a tool call. **From the same audit, in their own pull requests:** the print bridge no longer passes ESC/POS control bytes through or follows a redirect with its token (#254, merged); the CI review (#255, merged, docs/13 §6); #250 draft reservations held seats for good (#256); #251 the yes detector accepted questions and `confirm` did not require a spoken read-back (#258). **Open as issues:** #252 the rest (Postgres superuser, no lock file, `ENV` defaults to `dev`, bounds and CHECKs, retention of outbox payloads), #249 mypy is named in the stack but never runs (about 40 real errors) |

Proven by mutation: with the writer's status ignored (`{ read_dump "$file" || true; } | ...`) the damaged dump passes and the test is red 3 of 3. | 06.10.2026 | **Backup and restore no longer reject a good dump at random (T-9.3):** `verify_dump` and the restore into the scratch database piped `cat` (or `gpg`) into `pg_restore` under `pipefail`. `pg_restore` stops at the end marker without reading the rest, so the writer is sometimes killed by SIGPIPE (status 141) and a valid dump is reported as "cannot be read back, nothing kept". Seen once on `main` after PR #195 (`test_backup_refuses_to_write_plain_text_unless_told`), reproduced locally about one run in ten with four test workers, and the instrumented script showed `141 0` (writer, `pg_restore`). New helper `pg_restore_from` in `scripts/lib_pg.sh` drains the pipe before the status is read (gpg still checks to the last byte); `restore.sh` uses it for the real restore too. **Codex review PR #202 (P2x2) fixed:** the helper runs in a real subshell, because with `lastpipe` a brace group ran in the script itself and its `exit` ended `backup.sh` and `restore.sh` silently with status 0 (test with `BASHOPTS=lastpipe`, red before, green after); and the rest is drained only after a successful `pg_restore`, so a failed restore is not delayed by reading a large dump to its end. Regression test `test_a_dump_with_bytes_after_its_end_is_still_read_to_the_end` (1 MiB after the end marker): red 3 of 3 before, green 3 of 3 after; the whole file 40 times in a row with four workers, all green (before: about one in ten red). Also: handover "demo run of the operations view" carried over from the closed PR #191; `docs/16`: dropped work gets the label `wontfix`, paused work stays an open issue, no status label |

| 22.09.2026 | **`make test` and `make lint` match CI again.** The `api` service in `docker-compose.yml` mounted only the Python packages, so the container was missing everything the suite reads besides code: without `./sim` the four sim tests could not import `sim` (4 collection errors) and ruff sorted `sim` imports as a third-party package (3 false `I001` in `test_sim_cli.py`/`test_sim_replay.py`); without `./Makefile`, `./.claude`, `./deploy`, `./CLAUDE.md`, `./README.md`, `./CHANGELOG.md` and `./docs`, `test_commands.py` (3 tests) and `test_gui_router.py::test_caddy_schuetzt_die_gui` failed with `FileNotFoundError` on `/app/...`. All of it was a mount artifact, no line of application code was involved — the same suite has been green in CI the whole time, because CI runs on a full checkout. The paths are now mounted, read-only wherever the container only reads them (`./docs` stays writable because `scripts/status_bump.py` writes `docs/01_STATUS.md`). Of `.claude/` only `commands/` is mounted: in the main checkout the directory also holds `worktrees/` with full repo copies and their own `.env` files, which have no business inside the container (CLAUDE.md §8), and the test reads nothing but the commands. `dispatcher` needs none of them: it runs neither pytest nor ruff, only `api.events.dispatcher`. Verified on a fresh stack, after merging T-4.4 from `main` into the branch: `make lint` clean, `ruff format --check` 144 files, `make test` 1359 passed in 241 s. `pytest -q` (the CI command) and `pytest api/tests` collect exactly the same set — there is no test file outside `api/tests/`. **Guarded against the next time:** `api/tests/test_compose_mounts.py` parses the compose file and checks the api service's mounts against what the suite actually reads — every file and directory the slash commands name, and every path a test opens through `REPO_ROOT`/`parents[2]`. A new reference to an unmounted root file (say `CONTRIBUTING.md`) now fails in CI, where it is visible, instead of only in the container. Proven by mutation: dropping the `./deploy` mount and adding a `CONTRIBUTING.md` reference to a command file makes exactly those two tests red. It also pins the two decisions that are easy to undo by accident — `dispatcher` keeps its three mounts, and `.claude` is never mounted whole, because in the main checkout it holds `worktrees/` with other checkouts' `.env` files (CLAUDE.md §8). 1364 tests |

## Versioning of This File

Own, semantic version `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, independent of the `CLAUDE.md` bundle version and of README gate version (`docs/15_README_STRATEGY.md`) — README versions the product outward, this file versions itself inward:

| Bump | Trigger | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **MAJOR** | Gate passed / stage transition / architecture decision (E-Nr) flipped | G0 passed → stage 1 begins |
| **MINOR** | Decision made (D-Nr answered), new work package result changes "What's next" | D1 vendor chosen |
| **PATCH** | pure status housekeeping: task check, new assumption, new/resolved blocker | T-0.1 to done |

**Auto-update:** `python scripts/status_bump.py <patch|minor|major> "<one-line change>"` sets date, version, and changelog line automatically. Called by `/done`, `/task`, `/handover` (see `.claude/commands/`) — by hand only if needed. Extra safety net: CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) fails if `docs/07_WORKPACKAGES.md` changes but `docs/01_STATUS.md` stays unchanged in the same diff.
